L18: ## 1. Executive Summary
L19: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L20: Inclisiran (proposed proprietary name of LEOVIO), a new molecular entity (NME), is a double-stranded small interfering ribonucleic acid (siRNA). In hepatocytes, inclisiran utilizes the RNA interference mechanism and directs catalytic breakdown of messenger RNA (mRNA) for proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9 (PCSK9). The reduction in intrahepatic PCSK9 levels leads to increased recycling and expression of the LDL-C receptor on the hepatocyte cell surface. This leads to increased low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) uptake and lowering of LDL-C levels in the circulation.
L21: Inclisiran was developed as a single use prefilled syringe (300 mg inclisiran sodium in 1.5 mL aqueous solution containing 284 mg of inclisiran) for administration by a healthcare professional. Inclisiran is administered as a subcutaneous injection given at Day 1, again at 3 months, then every 6 months.
L22: The Applicant proposes the following indication: INCLISIRAN is indicated as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) or clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD), who require additional lowering of low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C).
L23: The initial application was submitted December 23, 2019. The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) recommended withholding approval because of deficiencies related to the [REDACTED] (b) (4) drug product manufacturing site in [REDACTED] (b) (4). Reviewers from all other review disciplines supported approval. The application received a Complete Response action on December 18, 2020 stating that FDA could not approve the application in its present form due to objectionable conditions noted at the drug product manufacturing site, [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L24: The Applicant now submits a Class 2 Complete Response Resubmission to address the deficiencies outlined in the Complete Response letter. During the End-of-Review meeting, the Applicant discussed the option of submitting Sandoz GmbH, Austria as a replacement or additional drug product manufacturing site in the Complete Response Resubmission. The Applicant has decided to replace the [REDACTED] (b) (4) drug product manufacturing site in the Complete Response Resubmission with the Sandoz GmbH drug product manufacturing site in Austria. The Applicant is also submitting a safety update that follows feedback given by FDA on April 1, 2021 and June 9, 2021 on the submitted Content and Format Packages with an agreed Data Lock Point (DLP) date of May 10, 2021 for the safety update in the Resubmission. As agreed to with FDA, the safety update includes data from the ongoing open-label trials, ORION-3 and ORION-8, data from the recently completed double-blind phase (part 1) of the
L25: ORION-5 trial, and meeting minutes from the open session Data Monitoring Committee (DMC) meeting from the ongoing ORION-4 trial.
L26: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L27: The initial submission of the application contained substantial evidence that the drug is effective for its intended use. LDL-C is a valid surrogate endpoint for evaluating risk reduction of atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD). Based on data showing robust LDL-C reductions and an acceptable safety profile, the Division of Diabetes, Lipid Disorders and Obesity (DDLO) recommended the following indication after review of the initial submission:
L28: - INCLISIRAN is indicated as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) or clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD), who require additional lowering of low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C).
L29: The effect of inclisiran on cardiovascular morbidity and mortality has not been determined in a dedicated cardiovascular outcomes trial.
L30: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment
L31: The following benefit-risk assessment table is based, in part, on information from the initial primary clinical review document (in DARRTS, dated November 6, 2020). Please refer to that review for details on the clinical efficacy and safety evaluation of inclisiran.
L32: ## Benefit-Risk Integrated Assessment
L33: Inclisiran (proposed proprietary name of LEQVIO) is a double-stranded siRNA that lowers LDL-C levels in the circulation. Based on data showing robust LDL-C reductions and an acceptable safety profile with inclisiran, this reviewer recommends approval of inclisiran as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) or clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD), who require additional lowering of LDL-C.
L34: Hyperlipidemia, specifically an increase in LDL-C levels, is a major modifiable risk factor for the development of atherosclerosis and ASCVD. ASCVD continues to be the leading cause of death and disability worldwide. The goal of lipid-lowering therapy is to reduce the risk for cardiovascular disease.
L35: Current treatment guidelines emphasize reducing the risk of ASCVD through lipid management, based on an individualized approach which considers a patient's overall risk for a cardiovascular (CV) event. Moderate- or high-intensity statins are considered first-line therapy for all patients who require LDL-C reduction, depending on their risk category. Ezetimibe and/or PCSK9 inhibitors are considered as second-line, add-on therapies to a background of maximally tolerated statin therapy in patients at highest CV risk (secondary prevention and HeFH) who require additional LDL-C reduction.
L36: The phase 3 trials ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11 included a population of approximately 3500 subjects with HeFH or ASCVD with elevated LDL-C despite maximally tolerated statin therapy who were treated with inclisiran sodium 300 mg administered subcutaneously at Day 1, Day 90, and every 6 months afterward for a duration of 18 months. The trial population included relevant comorbidities such as increased age, renal impairment, hepatic impairment, hypertension, and diabetes. The data from these 3 trials demonstrated durable, clinically meaningful LDL-C reduction (48-52%) with supportive results in total cholesterol (TC), non-high density lipoprotein cholesterol (non-HDL-C), and apolipoprotein B (ApoB) endpoints. The majority of subjects in the inclisiran group experienced a sizable LDL-C reduction at Day 510 compared to the placebo group. Consistent, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percent reductions from baseline to Day 510 were seen across various subpopulations defined by baseline demographic characteristics and disease comorbidities.
L37: Inclisiran has a prolonged pharmacodynamic effect on LDL-C levels which allows for infrequent dosing, which may be preferable to some patients. It is possible, but not proven, that less frequent dosing with inclisiran may improve a patient's adherence. However, unlike alirocumab, evolocumab, and statins, we do not have CV outcome data with inclisiran that confirms a reduction in CV morbidity and mortality,
L38: nor do we have the long-term safety data assessment that is possible from a CVOT that is several years in duration and enrolls thousands of patients. The lack of this data limits the scope of the indication to therapy that is add-on to statin therapy in individuals at high-risk for ASCVD.
L39: Review of safety data in the original NDA submission identified few issues. More subjects assigned to inclisiran reported and discontinued study drug due to injection site reactions compared with subjects assigned to placebo, but no serious or severe reactions occurred. Other events with small imbalances greater in the inclisiran group included arthralgia and increases in blood glucose in patients with diabetes/impaired fasting glucose. The incidence of adverse events of concern, including hypersensitivity, neurocognitive events, renal events, increases in liver transaminase and ophthalmological events, were similar between the inclisiran and placebo groups. The incidence of persistent anti-drug antibodies was low and was not associated with attenuation of LDL-C lowering.
L40: This Complete Response submission includes new and updated safety information on all reports of deaths, treatment-emergent adverse events (AEs), serious adverse events (SAEs), AEs leading to study drug discontinuation, AEs at the injection site, and clinical laboratory evaluation for inclisiran reported in the relevant ongoing open-label trials, ORION-3 and ORION-8, and from the recently completed double-blind phase (Part 1) of the ORION-5 trial, through May 10, 2021. The safety data set includes 3417 subjects given any dose of inclisiran. The overall safety profile for inclisiran in this update was similar to that previously seen in the phase 2 and 3 trials of the original NDA submission. No new safety concerns were identified.
L41: The benefit-risk analysis for inclisiran is favorable for an indication as an adjunct to maximally tolerated statin therapy in high-risk patients with clinical ASCVD or HeFH, who require additional LDL-C lowering. The data support approval for these conditions of use. The applicant will be issued postmarketing requirements (PMRs) to conduct phase 3 efficacy and safety trials evaluating inclisiran in patients with HeFH ages 8 years (or ≥6 years of age and on apheresis) to less than 18 years. A PMR to collect prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to inclisiran during pregnancy to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant will also be issued.
L42: ## Benefit-Risk Dimensions
L43: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	
Large burden of ASCVD worldwide and in the US:
ASCVD prevalence in United States excluding hypertension (coronary heart disease (CHD), heart failure (HF), and stroke only) is 9% overall (24.3 million in 2016).1
Leading cause of death in United States.
$351 billion annual cost of ASCVD and stroke in US (2015).1
LDL-C reduction with statins and PCSK9 inhibitors is associated with improved CV outcomes:
Meta-analysis of statin trials demonstrated that an absolute reduction of 38.7 mg/dL (1 mmol/L) with statins is associated with a 22% relative risk reduction in 5-year incidence of major vascular events (non-fatal myocardial infarction, coronary death, ischemic stroke, or coronary revascularization).2
CV outcomes trials (CVOTs) of the two approved PCSK9 inhibitors, alirocumab and evolocumab, demonstrated that reducing LDL-C levels led to a reduction in risk of CV events.3,4
LDL-C reduction with other agents is used in high-risk patients who require additional LDL-C lowering:
LDL-C reduction with therapies targeting upregulation of the LDL-C receptor is the cornerstone of preventive treatment (primary and secondary) for CV disease.
Statins are considered first-line, standard-of-care for lowering LDL-C with the goal of reducing CV risk.
CVOTs of statins and monoclonal antibody PCSK9 inhibitors demonstrated that reducing LDL-C levels with these agents led to a reduction in risk of CV events.
Despite the availability of these drugs with proven CV benefit, the burden of ASCVD in the US remains large.

Benefit	
In patients with established ASCVD who took inclisiran for secondary prevention as add-on to background statin therapy, inclisiran lowered LDL compared to placebo:
At Day 510, -51% to -52% reduction
In patients with HeFH who took inclisiran as add-on to background statin therapy, inclisiran lowered LDL compared to placebo:
At Day 510, -48% reduction
A cardiovascular outcomes trial to evaluate the benefit of inclisiran as an adjunct to statin therapy is ongoing.
Inclisiran has a robust LDL-C lowering treatment effect. The LDL-C lowering treatment effect of inclisiran is similar to that achieved with alirocumab and evolocumab; however, alirocumab and evolocumab have both demonstrated CV risk reduction in outcomes trials. The LDL-C lowering treatment effect of inclisiran is greater than that of ezetimibe, bempedoic acid, and low-intensity statins; ezetimibe and low-intensity statins have been evaluated in CV outcomes trials.
In the absence of outcomes data, the CV risk reduction is not definitively described. Nonetheless, inclisiran provides an additional

Risk and Risk Management	
In phase 3 trials, inclisiran was associated with:
Injections site reactions (8.2% of patients) compared to placebo (1.8%)
Arthralgia (5.0% versus 4.0% in placebo)
Urinary tract infections (4.4% versus 3.6% in placebo)
Diarrhea (3.9% versus 3.5% in placebo)
Bronchitis (4.3% versus 2.7% in placebo)
Review of updated safety data from the open-label extension phase 2 and 3 trials is consistent with that previously reported in the initial NDA submission.
option for patients who require LDL-C lowering despite optimized available therapies.
Inclisiran is associated with injection site reactions (ISR) that were mild to moderate in severity and transient in nature. The risk for ISR is monitorable and can be adequately addressed through labeling. Other adverse events with imbalances were infrequent and included small increases in blood glucose in patients with diabetes/impaired glucose tolerance. However, new onset diabetes incidence was similar to placebo.
Inclisiran continues to have an acceptable safety profile for its intended use in adults with ASCVD or HeFH.
L59: ## 2. The Regulatory Background
L60: ## 2.1. U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L61: Inclisiran is an NME, and it is not currently marketed in the U.S.
L62: ## 2.2. Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity
L63: The original inclisiran NDA 214012 was submitted on December 23, 2019. The Complete Response Letter (CRL) was issued to the Applicant on December 18, 2020 stating that FDA could not approve the application in its present form due to the lack of an acceptable facility inspection at the [REDACTED] (b) (4) drug product manufacturing facility located in [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L64: A Type A NDA End-of-Review Meeting was held on April 27, 2021 (refer to meeting minutes in DARRTS dated May 20, 2021). During the End-of-Review Meeting, the Applicant discussed the option of submitting Sandoz GmbH, Austria as a replacement or additional drug product manufacturing site in the Complete Response Resubmission. Subsequently, the Applicant informed the FDA that they would replace the [REDACTED] (b) (4) drug product manufacturing site in the Complete Response Resubmission with the Sandoz GmbH drug product manufacturing site in Austria.
L65: The Applicant submitted an initial Content and Format Package proposal on March 15, 2021, and FDA responded on April 1, 2021 that we agreed with the proposed content of the safety update and the overall proposed content and format to be included in the Complete Response Resubmission. The Applicant submitted an updated Content and Format Package proposal on June 2, 2021. The June submission contained the drug product manufacturing site change and a detailed outline of other sections of the proposed resubmission. FDA provided to the Applicant on June 9, 2021 the acknowledgement of the revised Content and Format for the resubmission and gave advice regarding what should be included in the chemistry, manufacturing, and controls (CMC) sections when it was submitted.
L66: The Applicant now submits a Class 2 Complete Response Resubmission to address the deficiencies outlined in the CRL. This submission contains revised Module 3 drug product sections with information related to the Sandoz GmbH, Austria drug product manufacturing site as a replacement drug product manufacturing site for [REDACTED] (b) (4). The revised module 3 includes the stability data and the drug product quality comparison between the phase 3 inclisiran trials and the to-be-marketed finished product manufactured at Sandoz GmbH site in Austria. The Applicant states that no changes have been made to the drug product composition, to the drug product release and end-of-shelf-life specifications, to the analytical procedure, to the drug product primary container closure system, to the drug substance, and to the excipients.
L67: The Applicant submitted 3-month stability data on September 20, 2021.
L68: The Applicant states that the last round of labeling negotiations with the FDA was submitted on December 16, 2020 (SN 0052) addressing comments on immunogenicity from the FDA as well as a request by the Applicant for FDA to review a counterproposal for the description of inclisiran in the product label. This submission is cross-referenced to the December 16, 2020 labeling submission, which included a response document with the tracked and clean versions of the label, in order to resume continued label negotiations from this timepoint.
L69: ## 2.3. Foreign Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L70: Inclisiran was approved in the European Union (EU) on December 9, 2020, and on December 11, 2020, the European Commission (EC) granted authorization for marketing inclisiran for the treatment of adults with primary hypercholesterolemia (heterozygous familial and non-familial) or mixed dyslipidemia, alone or in combination with other lipid-lowering therapies.
L71: Inclisiran was first launched in Germany and Austria in February 2021. Prior to May 10, 2021, inclisiran was also launched in 3 more EU countries and the United Arab Emirates (UAE).
L72: ## 3. Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
L73: ## 3.1. Product Quality
L74: In the initial review cycle, Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) recommended a complete response (CR) action due to the Office of Pharmaceutical Manufacturing Assessment (OPMA)'s recommendation for 'withhold approval' because of deficiencies related to the [REDACTED](b) (4) drug product manufacturing site in [REDACTED](b) (4) (refer to review by Dr. Ramaswamy, in DARRTS dated October 1, 2020; Reference ID: 4679598 and Dr. Janoria's facility review in Panorama dated September 30, 2020).
L75: For this submission, the recommendation from OPQ for NDA 214012 is approval, which includes an acceptable recommendation for the facilities listed in the application (refer to review by Dr. Ramaswamy, in DARRTS dated November 30, 2021; Reference ID: 4896564 and Dr. Pai's manufacturing process review in Panorama, dated November 22, 2021). The microbiology review concluded that the proposed microbiological controls are adequate to support the NDA (refer to Dr. Pfeiler's microbiology review dated September 8, 2021 and November 30, 2021 in Panorama). In summary, there are currently no outstanding deficiencies related to the drug substance, drug product, process, microbiology, facility, environmental
L76: analysis, and container/ carton label sections of the NDA; the OPQ overall recommendation for NDA 214012 is approval.
L77: ## 4. Review of Safety
L78: The DLP date used for the safety update in the Resubmission is May 10, 2021. The safety update includes new and updated safety information on all reports of deaths, treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), SAEs, AEs leading to study drug discontinuation, AEs at the injection site, and clinical laboratory evaluation for inclisiran reported in the relevant ongoing open-label trials, ORION-3 and ORION-8, and from the recently completed double-blind phase (part 1) of the ORION-5 trial through May 10, 2021. The safety data set includes 3417 subjects given any dose of inclisiran (see table below for details).
L79: ORION-4/KJX839B12301 is currently ongoing and blinded so no safety data from this trial was included in this update. However, per FDA's request, updates from the DMC's review of ORION-4 (open meeting minutes and the most recent recommendation letter) were submitted.

L84: This safety update includes exposure data from 3 ongoing trials (ORION-3, ORION-8, and ORION-5) with a total of 3417 subjects. Across the 3 trials, the total doses given were 16,550, as shown in the following table. In the original submission, the Division had requested the pivotal phase 3 trials for inclisiran to be 18 months in duration, as opposed to the typical 12-month duration, in part to have sufficient exposure to 4 injection administrations. In contrast, the monoclonal antibody PCSK9 inhibitors are given every 2 or 4 weeks so there was ample opportunity in those phase 3 trials to assess for ISRs and hypersensitivity reactions related to the injections. These extension trials provide an additional opportunity to assess for any ISRs or other reactions, such as rash, hypotension, and recall ISRs,12 that may occur in the setting of receiving the injection.

L108: ## 4.1. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L109: ## 4.1.1. Phase 2 Trial: ORION-3
L110: ORION-3 is an ongoing, open-label, multicenter, long-term (4 year) extension trial in subjects who completed the phase 2 trial ORION-1. Subjects have ASCVD or ASCVD-risk equivalents (e.g., diabetes or familial hypercholesterolemia) and elevated LDL-C despite maximum tolerated dose (as determined by the investigator) of LDL-C lowering therapies. Subjects from ORION-1 were eligible to enroll into ORION-3 if they completed ORION-1 to at least Day 210 and if their LDL-C had returned to within 20% of the baseline value or if they completed the Day 360 visit (ORION-1) with no contraindication to receiving inclisiran or evolocumab. In addition, subjects had to fulfill all inclusion and exclusion criteria of ORION-3 and sign an informed consent form.
L111: The trial consists of 2 treatment groups: Group 1 (inclisiran only arm) received inclisiran only and Group 2 (switching arm) received evolocumab for 1 year followed by inclisiran for the remainder of the trial. Subjects in Group 2 were allocated by the site number to two transition patterns:
L112: - Transition 1 (staged): Day 336 was the final dose of evolocumab therapy and the first dose of inclisiran was administered at the Day 360 visit.
- Transition 2 (concurrent): Day 336 was the final dose of evolocumab therapy at home. At the Day 360 visit, subjects received an additional dose of evolocumab and their first dose of inclisiran, both administered by the investigator.
L113: Figure 1 ORION-3: Trial Design
L114: Source: Figure 1-1 from Clinical Complete Response: safety_update_response_inclisiran
L115: In the original submission, the Applicant included all available data up to Day 630 of the trial. In this Safety Update, the Applicant provides all safety data up to May 10, 2021 as well as new safety data since the original NDA submission (from May 9, 2019 to May 10, 2021) for 284 subjects in Group 1 receiving inclisiran and 87 out of 90 subjects in Group 2 who switched from evolocumab to inclisiran and received inclisiran. Group 2 was broken down by transition pattern (Transition 1 [staged] and Transition 2 [concurrent]). Out of the 87 subjects in Group 2 (evolocumab\toinclisiran arm) who completed the Day 360 Visit and were continuing in the trial, 60 subjects were treated in Transition 1 and 27 subjects were treated in Transition 2.

L117: Refer to the Appendix for tables of study assessments for ORION-3.
L118: - Physical examination: Days 1, 360, 720, 1080, and at the end of study (EOS) visit.
- Vital signs (heart rate and blood pressure): Days 1, 30, 90, 180, 210, 270, 360, 390, 450, 540, 570, 630, 720, 810, 770, 990, 1080, 1170, 1260, 1350, and at the EOS visit.
- Electrocardiograms (ECGs): Days 1, 360, 720, 1080, and at the EOS visit.
L119: ## Laboratory assessments include:
L120: - Hematology – Day 1, Day 360, Day 720, Day 1080, and EOS: hemoglobin, hematocrit, erythrocytes, reticulocytes, platelet counts, mean cell hemoglobin (MCH), mean corpuscular volume (MCV), mean corpuscular hemoglobin concentration (MCHC), white blood cell count, differential blood count.
- Coagulation – Day 1, Day 360, Day 720, Day 1080, and EOS: prothrombin time (PT),
L121: - international normalized ratio (INR), activated partial thromboplastin (aPTT).
- Full serum chemistry – Day 1, Day 360, Day 720, Day 1080, and EOS: Aspartate aminotransferase (AST), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), alkaline phosphatase (ALP), gamma glutamyl transferase (GGT), total bilirubin (TBIL), uric acid, creatine phosphokinase (CK), albumin, total protein, urea (BUN), creatinine, sodium, potassium, chloride, inorganic phosphate, direct and indirect bilirubin, estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR), calcium, and bicarbonate.Fasting serum glucose and glycated hemoglobin A1C (HbA1C): Days 1, 1080, and EOS only,
- High sensitivity C-reactive protein (hsCRP) (fasting): Days 1, 360, 720, and EOS only.
- Limited serum chemistry all subjects – Days 30, 90, 180, 270, 390, 450, (510 Group 2 only), 540, (570 Group 1 only), 630, 810, (870 Group 1 only), (900 Group 2 only), 990, 1170, 1260, and 1350: AST, ALT, ALP, GGT, TBIL, CK, creatinine, and eGFR.Fasting serum glucose and HbA1C: Days 270, 540, 810, and 1350 only.
- Tryptase and inflammatory markers such as interleukin 6 (IL6), interferon-gamma (IFN-\gamma) and tumor necrosis factor-alpha (TNF-\alpha) may be performed from stored samples centrally at a later date, as required. Should a subject develop anaphylaxis on days when inclisiran is injected, the investigator is to collect a blood sample for tryptase within 30 minutes of the onset of anaphylaxis (or as soon as logistically possible).
- Anti-inclisiran antibodies: Group 1 (Inclisiran only arm): Days 1, 90, 180, and 270. Group 1 and 2: Days 360, 450, 540, 630, 720, 810, 770, 990, 1080, 1170, 1260, 1350, and at the EOS visit.
L122: **Safety Population:** all subjects who received at least one dose of study drug. Treatment classification was based on the actual treatment received. This was the primary population for the safety analyses.
L123: ## **Adverse Event Assessment:**
L124: - Pre-existing conditions: Planned hospital admissions and/or surgical operations for an illness or disease that existed at baseline and did not aggravate during the study are not to be reported as AEs.
- AE Severity1 = Mild: Discomfort noticed, but no disruption to daily activity. Requires minimal or no treatment.
- 2 = Moderate: Discomfort sufficient to reduce or affect normal daily activity. Requires therapeutic measures.
- 3 = Severe: Inability to work or perform normal daily activity. May require systemic drug therapy or other treatment.
- New onset of diabetes in subjects with no medical history of diabetes was reported
L125: when:
L126: - HbA1C becomes \ge 6.5\% and/or
- Two consecutive values of fasting plasma glucose that are \ge 126 mg/dL
- 'Worsening of the glycemic control' or 'diabetic complications' in subjects with a medical history of disease (HbA1C \ge 6.5\% at baseline) was reported when:HbA1C increases from Baseline (Day 1 of ORION-3) &gt; 0.5\% and/or
- New concomitant medication or increase in dose of current antidiabetic therapy is initiated to improve the control of plasma glucose level.
L127: ## Exposure and Disposition:
L128: ## Group 1
L129: The median duration on the trial was 1262 days (~3.5 years) and the exposure was 941.5 patient-years in ORION-3. A total of 42 subjects (15%) in Group 1 (inclisiran only arm) discontinued the trial (see Table 4). Withdrew consent was the most common reason for discontinuation from the trial. Eight subjects discontinued due to TEAEs and 5 deaths were reported from the initiation of the trial until the cut-off (May 10, 2021). A total of 242 subjects (85%) were ongoing at the cut-off date.
L130: ## Group 2
L131: The median duration on the trial was 1266 days (~3.5 years) and the exposure was 216.4 patient-years in ORION-3. A total of 10 subjects (11%) in Group 2 (evolocumab→inclisiran arm) discontinued the trial. Withdrew consent and physician decision were the most common reasons for discontinuation from the trial. No subjects discontinued due to TEAEs and 1 death (from COVID-19) was reported from the initiation of the trial until the cut-off (May 10, 2021). A total of 76 subjects (84%) were ongoing at the cut-off date and 4 subjects (4%) completed the trial.

L135: Additional information was requested from and provided by the Applicant for the subjects who discontinued for 'other' reasons. The 'other' reasons for discontinuation included: (1) there were 2 consecutive dosing visits missed in the trial; as a result, the subject had been without open-label inclisiran treatment for more than a year and was discontinued from the study at the sponsor's request, (2) it was the investigator's and/or subject's decision to not continue in the trial, (3) a new prescription of ezetimibe was started, and the sponsor requested discontinuation according to protocol since the subject had initiated a prohibited medication, and (4) subject relocated and was no longer able to participate in the study.
L136: *Reviewer Comment: The mean and median duration of exposure to inclisiran were similar in both groups. The percentage of subjects discontinuing was generally similar between Group 1 and Group 2, although there was a higher incidence of adverse events in Group 1. The median duration of exposure to inclisiran of ~3.5 years is helpful in assessing events that are rare or easily attributed to study drug, like ISRs. However, as it is open-label and uncontrolled (i.e., all subjects are on inclisiran) it is a limited data source to determine if a finding is associated or caused by the drug versus merely reflects the events typically seen in that study population.*
L137: ## Demographics:
L138: - The population in Group 1 consisted of 184 (65%) male subjects, the mean age was 63
L139: years, 94% of subjects were White, 4% were Black or African American, 1% were Asian, 1% were American Indian or Alaska Native, and &lt;1% were Native Hawaiian or Other Pacific Islander. Subjects came from the Netherlands (37%), United States (US) (19%), United Kingdom (UK) (18%), Canada (14%), and Germany (12%).
L140: - The population in Group 2 consisted of 54 (60%) male subjects, the mean age was 62 years, 93% of subjects were White, and 6% were Black or African American. Subjects came from the Netherlands (32%), US (18%), UK (18%), Canada (17%), and Germany (16%).
- Combining Groups 1 and 2, there were 238 (64%) male subjects and 136 (36%) female subjects; the mean age was 63 years, median age was 64 years, and 48% were age 65 years and older. Overall, 93% of subjects were White, 4% were Black or African American, 1% were Asian, 1% were American Indian or Alaska Native, and &lt;1% were Native Hawaiian or Other Pacific Islander; 6% were of Hispanic or Latino ethnicity. Subjects came from the Netherlands (35%), US (19%), UK (18%), Canada (15%), and Germany (13%).
L141: ## Treatment Compliance:
L142: - Group 1: Group 1 subjects were to receive subcutaneous (SC) doses on Days 720, 810, 990, 1170, and 1350 since the last safety update (January 31, 2020). As of the cut-off of May 10, 2021, 255 subjects, 242 subjects, 234 subjects, 243 subjects, and 100 subjects received SC doses, administered by clinical study site staff, on Days 720, 810, 990, 1170, and 1350 respectively. The majority (~99%) of the injections were in the abdomen. The dose administered was 300 mg in all subjects except one subject receiving an unspecified dose other than 300 mg on Day 720.
- Group 2 subjects were to receive SC doses on Days 810, 990, 1170, and 1350 since the last safety update. As of the cut-off of May 10, 2021, 82 subjects, 81 subjects, 78 subjects, and 31 subjects received planned SC doses on Days 810, 990, 1170, and 1350. The majority (~99%) of the injections were in the abdomen. The dose administered was 300 mg in all the subjects except one subject receiving 200 mg on Day 990.
L143: ## Safety Data:
L144: The translation of investigator-reported terms for the adverse event to preferred terms was examined by this reviewer. Overall, few errors in translation were identified, and no adverse events were reclassified. Based on comparing the reported term to the dictionary-derived preferred term, this reviewer believes that AEs were generally categorized appropriately. Overall, this safety data submission, in particular the patient narratives, was high-quality, complete, and well-organized.
L145: A summary of the safety data in Group 1 (inclisiran only arm) and Group 2 (evolocumab→inclisiran arm) is shown in the table below. For Group 2, all 90 subjects who were dosed at any time during the trial were included in the “Safety population” as detailed in Table 4. However, for the purposes of some of the following safety analyses, only the 87 subjects who received inclisiran on or after Day 360 (two subjects discontinued before Day 360 and one subject discontinued at Day 360) are included in the Group 2 Evolocumab-Inclisiran population.

L151: ## Deaths:
L152: Since the trial initiation up to the cut-off of May 10, 2021 (all data), a total of six deaths were reported. One death (Group 1) was reported in the original NDA and five deaths (4 in Group 1 and 1 in Group 2) were reported since the initial NDA DLP of the original NDA (May 8, 2019). Five deaths were reported in Group 1 (inclisiran arm only) from the preferred terms (PTs) of Corona virus infection (1), respiratory failure (1), ischemic stroke (1)13, aortic aneurysm rupture

L154: (1) and coronary artery occlusion (1). One death was reported in Group 2 from the PT of Coronavirus infection. None of the deaths were likely related to study drug based on my review of the narratives. Further, deaths secondary to CV events are not unexpected in a trial population of subjects with baseline ASCVD or ASCVD risk equivalents.

L160: TESAEs:
L161: - Group 1: Inclisiran only arm
L162: Since the initiation of the trial until the cut-off date, 95 (34%) subjects had at least one TESAE in Group 1. The most frequent SAEs reported were in the system organ class (SOC) cardiac disorders (11%; 31/284); CV events would be expected in patients with established ASCVD/ASCVD risk equivalents and dyslipidemia. The most common TESAEs were angina pectoris and osteoarthritis (each 2%, 6/284), followed by angina unstable (2%, 5/284), atrial fibrillation, coronary artery disease, coronavirus infection, pneumonia, prostate cancer, and transient ischemic attack (each 1%, 4/284) (as shown in Table 6 below).
L163: - Group 2: Evolocumab/inclisiran arm
L164: As shown in Table 6, from Day 360 to May 10, 2021, 28 out of 87 (32%) subjects treated with inclisiran reported a TESAE. The most common TESAE by PT were coronary artery disease (3, 3%), transient ischaemic attack (2, 2%), and hypertensive crisis (2, 2%).

L172: In the placebo-controlled database submitted with the original NDA, the most commonly reported SAEs were in the Cardiac disorders SOC (9.4% in placebo and 8.0% in inclisiran), followed by Infections (3.6% in placebo and 3.9% in inclisiran), and Nervous System disorders (2.7% in placebo and 2.4% in inclisiran). The preferred terms atrial fibrillation (n=20, 1.1%),
L173: pneumonia (n=21, 1.1%), and myocardial infarction (n=12, 0.7%) were reported at slightly higher incidence rates for inclisiran than placebo (0.8%, 0.9%, and 0.5%), respectively.
L174: Overall, TESAEs in this safety update were similar to those seen in the original NDA and included CV events such as angina pectoris, coronary artery disease, atrial fibrillation, and transient ischemic attack that are expected in a population of subjects with ASCVD or ASCVD risk equivalents, as well as other SAEs commonly seen in this older population, including osteoarthritis, prostate cancer, and infections (pneumonia, coronavirus).
L175: TEAEs leading to study drug discontinuation:
L176: - Group 1: Inclisiran only arm
L177: In all data up to May 10, 2021, 14 (5%) subjects in Group 1 discontinued the study drug due to a TEAE, including 5 subjects who discontinued since the original NDA DLP. These TEAEs leading to study drug discontinuation included multi-organ failure, corona virus infection, eye contusion, muscle strain, hepatic enzyme increased, liver function test abnormal, failure to thrive, muscular weakness, myalgia, Hodgkin's disease stage IV, plasma cell myeloma, prostate cancer, skin depigmentation, epistaxis, and abortion induced.
L178: My review of the subject narratives revealed some events in which a relationship to inclisiran cannot be ruled out:
L179: - Patient: [REDACTED](b) (6), a 71-year-old White male with a medical history which included hyperlipidemia, hypertension, prior MI, prior percutaneous coronary intervention, duodenal ulcer, gout, and vagotomy; and concomitant medications including aspirin, clopidogrel, valsartan, bisoprolol, carbasalate calcium, and colchicine, who experienced muscle strain on Day 635. The patient received the last dose of study drug on Day 726. The patient did not have CK elevations. At the time of this report, the event of muscle strain did not resolve. The investigator considered the event of muscle strain to be moderate and reasonably attributable to the study drug.
- Patient: [REDACTED](b) (6), a 23-year-old Black male, who was administered inclisiran on Day 1 and Day 176. PMH: hyperlipidemia on ezetimibe. No other medications listed. On Day 176, the patient experienced a non-serious adverse event of worsening of myalgia to lower limbs. Lab results for this visit indicated the patient had a creatine kinase level (reference range 25-210) of 256 U/L. Muscle pain was not associated with a clinically significant elevation of creatine kinase (only 1.2 x ULN). Mildly elevated creatine kinase levels were observed from Day 1 throughout the study. The patient was discontinued from the study drug due to the worsening of myalgia to lower limbs. The patient had received the last dose of the study drug on Day 176. At the time of this report, the patient did not recover from the event of myalgia. The investigator considered the event of myalgia to be severe and reasonably possibly attributable to the study drug.
- Group 2: Evolocumab/Inclisiran arm

These TEAEs leading to study drug discontinuation included hepatic enzyme increased, coronary artery disease, intraocular pressure increased, low density lipoprotein increased, and restless legs syndrome.
L181: The patient with hepatic enzyme increased was described in the original review. The patient had hepatitis C and reported recent excessive alcohol consumption. My review of the subject narratives did not reveal events that were likely attributable to inclisiran.
L182: ## TEAEs:
L183: - Group 1: Inclisiran only arm
L184: TEAEs from trial initiation to the current safety update cut-off are shown in the table below. The most common TEAEs include nasopharyngitis, hypertension, urinary tract infection, influenza, and arthralgia. Although the table summarizes all data, the TEAEs across the 3 study periods (submitted in original NDA, Day 1 to May 9, 2019; May 9, 2019 to May 10, 2021; and Day 1 to May 10, 2021 [all data]), were similar.
L185: - Group 2: Evolocumab/inclisiran arm
L186: In Group 2, the most common TEAEs in subjects treated with inclisiran from trial start to May 10, 2021 were hypertension, nasopharyngitis, influenza, and back pain.
L187: In the phase 3 placebo-controlled trials submitted with the original NDA, TEAEs that occurred more frequently in inclisiran-treated subjects included ISRs (includes IS pain, rash, and erythema) (8%); arthralgia (5%); urinary tract infection, diarrhea, bronchitis (4% each); and pain in extremity and dyspnea (3% each). The most common AEs observed in placebo-controlled trials are similar to ORION-3, with the exception of ISRs, which occurred more frequently in the phase 3 trials.
L188: Although several TEAEs were reported with high frequency in the open-label ORION-3 (e.g., nasopharyngitis, hypertension, influenza, diabetes mellitus, back pain, upper respiratory tract infection, fall, dizziness, myalgia, fatigue), an imbalance in these TEAEs was not observed in placebo-controlled Phase 3 data submitted with the original NDA. Further, some reported AEs are common even outside of clinical trials (e.g., back pain), some AEs may be associated with concomitant LLT (e.g., myalgia), and some AEs are common for the enrolled trial population (e.g., diabetes mellitus). Note also that analysis of AEs and blood pressure data during the original NDA review did not indicate a signal for worsening blood pressure with inclisiran.

L194: ## AEs of Special Interest:
L195: ## Injection Site Reactions (ISRs)
L196: - Group 1: Inclisiran only arm
L197: In 'all data' up to the May 10, 2021 data cut-off, TEAEs at the injection site (MedDRA high-level term of 'injection site reactions') in Group 1 were similar to what was reported in ORION-3 in the original NDA submission: 38/284 (13.4%) subjects reported any TEAE at the injection site, compared to 34/284 (12.0%) in the original NDA. Injection site reaction, injection site erythema, and injection site pain were the most common AEs at the injection site in both 'all data' (5%, 4%, and 4%) and the original NDA (4%, 4%, and 4% respectively). My review of the data shows that most cases were transient, mild, and with Grade I or II of erythema.
L198: - Group 2: Evolocumab/inclisiran arm
L199: In all data up to May 10, 2021, TEAEs at the injection site in Group 2 were similar to what was reported in the original NDA: 11/87 (12.6%) subjects reported any TEAE at the injection site, compared to 9/87 (10.3%) in the original NDA. Injection site bruising, injection site pain, and injection site reaction were the most common AEs at the injection site in all data (4%, 5%, and 5% respectively) and in the original NDA (2%, 5%, and 2% respectively).
L200: From trial start to May 10, 2021, ISRs occurred in 13.2% (49/371) of ORION-3 subjects in Groups 1 and 2 combined (Group 2 only includes data when subjects were on inclisiran, which is on or after Day 360 as prior to that time they were on evolocumab).

L210: ## - Group 1: Inclisiran only arm
L211: As of the cut-off of May 10, 2021, 22 subjects (8%) subjects had at least one clinically significant increase in chemistry lab value. Most subjects' ALT or AST levels decreased at their next assessment. One subject had a temporary increase in bilirubin &gt; 2x ULN. There were no Hy's law cases. Additional information on subjects with abnormal hepatic-related chemistry parameters are described below:
L212: - Subject (b)(6): (ULN of ALT=41 U/L) 48-year-old White male with a PMH of asthma, depression, diabetes mellitus, GERD, hyperlipidemia, HTN, insomnia, chronic peripheral venous insufficiency, restless leg syndrome, and seasonal
L213: allergies. Concomitant medications include atorvastatin 20 mg, beclomethasone, Breo Ellipta, famotidine, dulera, fexofenadine, lisinopril, methotrexate, tramadol, and sertraline. Baseline ALT was 0.5 x ULN, ALT 5.3 x ULN at Days 1170 and 1177; at Day 1260 had returned to 0.8 x ULN. No other adverse events were recorded around the time of this event.
L214: - Subject (b)(6): 66-year-old White female with a PMH of hyperlipidemia and HTN on aspirin and atorvastatin 80 mg. Her baseline ALT was 0.5 x ULN; ALT was 5.4 x ULN at Day 1170 and had decreased to 1.2 x ULN at Day 1184. Baseline AST was 0.4 x ULN; AST was 6.5 x ULN at Day 1170 and had decreased to 1.5 x ULN at Day 1184. On Day 729 an AE of liver function test abnormal (mild) was recorded and on Day 1170 the subject experienced elevated liver enzymes due to alcohol intake (mild) which was considered a significant AE. The participant was on study drug treatment when the event occurred.
- Subject (b)(6): 78-year-old White male, baseline ALT 0.5 x ULN; ALT was 4.4 x ULN at Day 180, 5.4 x ULN at Day 189 and had decreased to 0.9 x ULN at Day 270. Baseline AST 0.6 x ULN; AST was 3.9 x ULN at Day 180, 4.7 x ULN at Day 189 and had decreased to 0.9 x ULN at Day 270. This subject with a PMH of hyperlipidemia, diabetes mellitus, chronic kidney disease, hypertension, angina pectoris, and former smoker was diagnosed with Gleason 3+4 prostate cancer on Day 16 and opted for conservative treatment and regular blood monitoring rather than external beam radiotherapy. Due to the non-serious AE of abnormal liver function tests at Day 182, the investigator decided to discontinue the study drug permanently. On Day 209, the patient had a non-serious AE of cholelithiasis. On Day 270, the event of liver function test abnormal resolved. The patient was receiving multiple concomitant therapies at baseline and throughout the study (including atorvastatin 40 mg, aspirin, lisinopril, senna, nicorandil, atorvastatin 40 mg, omeprazole, metformin, diclofenac, and ingenol mebutate (topical for actinic keratosis). The elevated serum aminotransferases accompanied by elevated alkaline phosphatase and gamma-glutamyl transpeptidase suggested a cholestatic pattern of abnormal liver function tests. The events of cholelithiasis and prostate cancer did not resolve at the time of this narrative report. The patient was withdrawn from the study at Day 364 (~ 6 months after stopping study drug). I agree with the investigator that the events of cholelithiasis and prostate cancer are not likely to be attributable to the study drug.
- Subject (b)(6): 63-year-old White female with PMH of coronary artery disease, hyperlipidemia, arthritis, drug hypersensitivity, hypothyroidism, tendon disorder, and vertigo; concomitant medications include fish oil, metoprolol, thyroid medication, diphenhydramine, and ibuprofen. Her baseline ALT was 0.8 x ULN; ALT was 4.9 x ULN at Day 1260 but decreased to 0.7 x ULN at Day 1350. Baseline AST was 1.1 x ULN, AST was 5.7 x ULN at Day 1260 but decreased to 0.9 x ULN at Day 1350. The patient also experienced corona virus infection on Day 1226 and viral diarrhea on Day 1264, around the time she was experiencing elevations in ALT and AST.
L215: 5. Subject (b)(6): AST ULN=34 U/L; 36-year-old White female (concomitant medications include atorvastatin 80 mg, ezetimibe, and Excedrin prn headaches), baseline AST was 0.6 x ULN; AST was 4.5 x ULN at Day 630 but decreased to 1.8 x ULN at Day 636. This subject, described in more detail in the CK labs section, also had a CK 53 x ULN at study day 630 that had decreased to 5 x ULN 6 days later and subsequent values were in the normal range. The AST elevation was likely related to the CK elevation/myopathy event. She had no AEs recorded around the time of the AST elevation.

6. Subject (b)(6): 60-year-old White male with a PMH of hyperlipidemia, hypertension, atrial fibrillation, asthma, GERD, insomnia, osteoarthritis, scoliosis and seasonal allergy. His concomitant medications included fluvastatin 80 mg, ezetimibe, aspirin, metoprolol, meloxicam, lisinopril/hydrochlorothiazide, Symbicort, albuterol, acetaminophen, ondansetron, pantoprazole, and escitalopram. His baseline AST was 0.6 x ULN and the AST was 3.7 x ULN at last study visit, Day 1350. On Day 1244, the patient reported symptoms of nausea, vomiting, fatigue, and hypotension (blood pressure was 98/55 mmHg). The patient was hospitalized due to the SAEs of alcohol withdrawal syndrome and hyponatremia. The patient was treated with lisinopril, lorazepam, and thiamine. On Day 1247, the patient recovered from the events of alcohol withdrawal syndrome and hyponatremia and was discharged. The AST elevation was likely due to recent alcohol-induced liver injury.

7. Subject (b)(6): 49-year-old White female with PMH of DM, hyperlipidemia, anxiety, dysmenorrhea, polycystic ovarian syndrome, hypothyroidism, and sleep apnea. Her concomitant medications included metformin, sitagliptin, perindopril, candesartan, levothyroxine, spironolactone, Marvelon 28, and escitalopram. Her baseline AST was 0.4 x ULN. AST was 3.3 x ULN at Day 390, 2.7 x ULN at Day 450 and 1 x ULN at Day 540. She had recurrent events of mild ISRs during the trial and an upper respiratory tract infection recorded at Day 459, but no AEs recorded around the time of the AST elevation (recorded as an AE of hepatic enzyme increase on Day 386). Drug treatment was continued.

8. Subject (b)(6): 40-year-old White male, with a PMH of DM, diabetic retinopathy, hyperlipidemia, depression, and back pain (concomitant medications include insulin glargine and citalopram), had a baseline AST 0.6 x ULN. AST was 3.3 x ULN at Day 180 but decreased to 0.7 x ULN by Day 270. This subject, described in more detail in the CK labs section, also had a CK 42 x ULN at Day 180 that remained elevated ranging from 1.1 to 3.4x ULN through Day 1350. The AST elevation was likely related to the CK elevation/myopathy event. An AE of CPK increased was recorded on Day 190; there were no other AEs recorded around that time.

9. Subject (b)(6): 72-year-old White male (concomitant medications include simvastatin 10 mg, Lovaza 2000 mg, spironolactone, furosemide, bisoprolol, aspirin, clopidogrel, inhaled fluticasone, tamsulosin, and pantoprazole) with baseline bilirubin of 1.1 x ULN, increased to 2 x ULN at Days 990 and 1080 and was 1.9 x ULN
L216: at Day 1170, last study visit. This subject with a PMH of congestive heart failure, hyperlipidemia, peripheral artery disease, aortic valve disease, COPD, ischemic cardiomyopathy, former smoker and prior PCI, on Day 1201 was admitted to the hospital with severe apical centrilobular emphysema by CT, pneumonia, and respiratory failure. On Day 1214, pneumonia worsened, and the patient was intubated. The patient went into cardiopulmonary arrest and died due to the event of respiratory failure. An autopsy was not performed.
L217: Although some subjects reported elevations in AST or ALT, an imbalance in hepatic enzymes was not observed in the placebo-controlled Phase 3 data submitted with the original NDA. In these Phase 3 trials, the incidence of elevations in ALT or AST was low and balanced between the two treatment groups (for example, ALT &gt; 3x ULN occurred in 0.5% of inclisiran-treated subjects and 0.4% of placebo-treated subjects). There were no Hy's Law cases. The incidence of hepatic disorders SMQ was 3.8% on inclisiran and 4.2% on placebo. There was no signal for drug-induced hepatic injury in the 18-month duration, placebo-controlled trials. Additionally, most subjects who experienced an AST/ALT elevation in ORION-3 had a plausible alternative etiology (e.g., statin use, cholelithiasis, hospitalization).
L218: Abnormal increases in serum creatinine (\ge 50\% increase from baseline or &gt;2 mg/dL) were reported in 7 subjects (2.5%). Most subjects' abnormal increase in serum creatinine levels were transient and decreased at their next assessment. The following subjects had increases that persisted. The creatine elevations are not unexpected given the patients' underlying medical condition and are unlikely drug-associated.
L219: - Subject [REDACTED](b)(6): 74-year-old white male with PMH of hyperlipidemia, hypertension, peripheral artery disease (PAD), prior cardiovascular arterial bypass graft (CABG), MI, TIA, TAVR, former smoker who received the last dose of study drug on Day 805. On Day 918, the patient had an aortic aneurysm rupture and died. Baseline creatinine was 1.12 mg/dL and ranged from 1.68 to 1.85 from Day 390 to Day 870.
- Subject [REDACTED](b)(6): 69-year-old white female with PMH of hyperlipidemia, hypertension, prior CABG, MI, prior PCI, former smoker who experienced the following AEs during the trial: anemia (Day 256), hospitalized for pulmonary embolism and cardiac failure (Day 256), DM (Day 264), renal impairment (Day 275), muscle spasms (Day 533), and urinary tract infection (UTI) (Days 1172 and 1261), who experienced elevated creatinine levels from Day 270 through Day 1080.
- Subject [REDACTED](b)(6): 57-year-old white male with PMH of hyperlipidemia, hypertension, prior MI, left bundle branch block, and current smoker who experienced the following AEs during the trial: MI (Day 640), ventricular tachycardia (Days 955 and 1024) treated with amiodarone and electrophysiological examination with cooled radiofrequency ablation, reported elevated creatinine levels on Days 570 and 990. Creatinine level had decreased and was close to baseline value by Day 1080.
L220: Abnormal increases in creatine kinase (CK) were reported in 8 subjects (2.8%) (&gt;5x to 10x ULN: 5; &gt;10x to 20x ULN: 1; &gt;20x ULN: 2). In subjects who had CK data at a subsequent visit, most of these elevations normalized or improved.
L221: - Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), a 64-year-old white female with a PMH of hyperlipidemia, xanthoma, arcus lipoides, coronary artery stenosis, GERD, arthralgia, and osteopenia. Her background medications included rosuvastatin 40 mg, ezetimibe, metoprolol, aspirin, clopidogrel, omeprazole and ibuprofen. She had a CK 19 x ULN which had normalized within 5 days. The rapid decrease in CK within the setting of continued exposure to inclisiran makes this unlikely to be drug induced.
- Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), 36-year-old White female with HeFH, background medications included atorvastatin 80 mg and ezetimibe 10 mg daily. Baseline CK levels were normal. On Day 630, the CK was unexpectedly increased to 11,155 U/L (normal range: 25-210; central laboratory, 53 x ULN). The last subcutaneous injection of inclisiran prior to this abnormal laboratory value was administered on Day 540 (90 days before the CK elevation). This triggered an unscheduled visit 6 days later and the CK values remained elevated but at a much lower value of 1054 U/L (5 x ULN). By Day 720, the CK had returned to normal and remained so for the remainder of the trial on active treatment (2 years). Liver enzymes were normal. The inclisiran treatment was not interrupted by the investigator, and this CK abnormality was not reported as an adverse event. The investigator concluded that the increase in CK was most probably induced by exercise and there was no evidence of any other contributing factors or changes in the background medications. Based on narrative review, I agree that this event is not likely attributable to study drug and was most probably induced by exercise.
- Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), a 40-year-old White male, with medical history of type 2 diabetes, diabetic retinopathy, depression, recurrent back pain and gonarthrosis. Background medication included 4 daily injections of basal and prandial insulins, citalopram 20 mg daily and codeine/paracetamol used on an as-needed basis. Beside the study medication, there were no other lipid-lowering therapies in the background therapies. The subject's baseline CK was elevated at 1.8 x ULN. On Day 180, a CK at 8752 U/L (41.7 x ULN) was documented. The event was entered as an adverse event. The inclisiran treatment was not interrupted by the investigator. The investigator obtained 2 local CK measurements, 3 and 9 days later, which showed values of 969 U/L and 390 U/L, respectively (no normal local reference range available). CKs remained elevated ranging from 1.1 to 3.4x ULN throughout the study (EOS Day 1440). There are no documented concurrent causes for the peak increase in CK levels and there are no documented reasons for the overall small chronic increase of CK over the course of the entire study. The investigator concluded that the peak increase of CK levels on Day 180 was "due to exercising". I am not sure what the cause of the peak elevation and smaller chronic elevations of CK are, but it is unlikely to be caused by active study drug based on the rapid improvement in the peak CK level despite continued drug treatment and the lack of a signal for CK elevation in placebo-controlled phase 3 data.
L222: Although a few subjects reported transient elevations in CK, an imbalance in CK was not seen in the placebo-controlled Phase 3 trials submitted with the original NDA. In these Phase 3 trials, there were 4 (0.2%) inclisiran-treated subjects and 2 (0.1%) placebo-treated subjects who had creatinine kinase &gt;10X ULN and ≤20X ULN. Four (0.2%) placebo-treated subjects had creatine kinase &gt;20 XULN as compared to none on inclisiran. No inclisiran treated subject with a clinically significant creatinine kinase increase had an associated treatment emergent adverse event related to musculoskeletal complaints. Thus, the transient CK elevations seen in ORION-3 are unlikely to represent a drug-related safety signal in the setting of continued drug exposure and the CK elevations are not corroborated by placebo-controlled data.
L223: ## - Group 2: Evolocumab/inclisiran arm
L224: As of the cut-off of May 10, 2021, 9 subjects (10%) in Group 2 had at least one clinically significant increase in chemistry lab value. One subject (1.1%) had a temporary clinically significant increase in ALT (&gt;5 and ≤10 x ULN) and one subject (1.1%) had a temporary clinically significant increase in AST (&gt;10 and ≤20 x ULN). Two subjects (2.3%) had an increase in bilirubin &gt; 2x ULN; both subjects had elevations at baseline and throughout the trial. There were no Hy's law cases. Abnormal increases in serum creatinine (≥50% increase from baseline or &gt;2 mg/dL) were reported in 6 subjects (6.9%). Review of the narratives did not reveal any cases that were likely related to study drug (e.g., one subject had chronic kidney disease, one subject had elevations in the setting of being recently hospitalized for pneumonia and coronary artery stenosis). There were no abnormal increases in creatine kinase. Additional information on notable subjects with abnormal chemistry parameters are described below:
L225: - Subject [REDACTED](b)(6): 47-year-old white female with PMH of hyperlipidemia, hypertension, CABG, current smoker with AEs of respiratory distress (Days 97, 162, 188, and 206), hemorrhoidal hemorrhage (Day 238), hyponatremia (Day 240), non-cardiac chest pain (Day 240), chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) (Days 381 and 440), laceration with report of consuming 4-8 beers per day (Day 401), SAE of hepatic enzyme increased and patient was discontinued from the study drug (Day 409) and hepatitis C (Day 438) with ALT 5.8 x ULN at Day 390, 9.1 x ULN at Day 406, and 1.8 x ULN at EOS/Day 586. AST was 9.4 x ULN at Day 390, 14.5 x ULN at Day 406, and 2.8 x ULN at EOS/Day 586. On Day 421, the patient had a gastroenterology consultation. A diagnostic work-up was initiated which included a hepatitis C panel which came back positive. The patient was diagnosed with chronic viral hepatitis C, a FibroScan image showed stage II fibrosis and stage III steatosis, and elevated liver function test. The hepatic enzyme increase is likely due to the patient's underlying liver disease and alcohol use rather than study drug.
L226: There were no clinically significant changes in hematology parameters (i.e., platelet counts ≤ 150,00 or ≥ 700,00) in Group 1 or in Group 2.
L227: ## Anti-Inclisiran Antibodies:
L228: In ORION-3, the planned last patient last visit (LPLV) date is at the end of December 2021. To date, anti-drug antibody (ADA) datasets are available for ORION-3 through the cut-off date of February 14, 2020. As ADA samples for this trial are analyzed at specific milestones, additional long-term data will be available at the completion of this ongoing trial. Using the definitions by Shankar et al.,14 the percentage of subjects with treatment-induced ADA, as well as persistent and transient ADA responses, is summarized in the table below. None of the 22 subjects with treatment-induced ADAs had an associated hypersensitivity event.

L231: * n: # of subjects with anti-drug antibody (ADA) response; m: # of subjects tested for ADA.
L232: ** Treatment Induced ADA: negative ADA sample at baseline and at least one positive ADA sample post-baseline. Persistent: negative ADA at baseline and confirmed positive ADA on 2 or more timepoints where the first and last were separated by a period of at least 16 weeks or positive ADA at the last timepoint or at a timepoint less than 16 weeks before an ADA negative last sample.
L233: Transient: treatment-induced ADA detected only 1 time point post-baseline, or 2 or more sampling time points where the first and last ADA-positive samples are separated by a period less than 16 weeks, and the subject's last sampling time point is ADA-negative.
L234: Source: Tables 2-1 and 2-2 from Novartis Responses to FDA Questions, dated November 2, 2021.
L235: In the phase 3 placebo-controlled trials (ORION-9, -10, and -11), 1830 out of a total of 1833 inclisiran-treated subjects had samples tested for anti-drug antibodies. A treatment-induced ADA response was detected in 90 (4.9%) subjects during the 18 months of treatment with inclisiran. Thirty-one (1.7%) inclisiran-treated subjects had a persistent ADA response and 41 (2.2%) had a transient ADA response. The observed frequencies, particularly for the persistent ADA response with 1.1% in ORION-3 and 1.7% in the pooled phase 3 trials, are low and similar
L236: 14 Shankar G, Arkin S, Cocca L, et al., American Association of Pharmaceutical Scientists. Assessment and reporting of the clinical immunogenicity of therapeutic proteins and peptides-harmonized terminology and tactical recommendations. AAPS J. 2014 Jul;16(4):658-73. doi: 10.1208/s12248-014-9599-2. Epub 2014 Apr 24. PMID: 24764037; PMCID: PMC4070270.
L237: between the two groups.
L238: *Reviewer's Conclusion: The overall safety profile for inclisiran in the phase 2 OLE trial ORION-3 was similar to that seen in the original NDA submission and the Day 120 safety update for ORION-3. The most common TESAEs were angina pectoris and osteoarthritis, and the most common TEAEs were nasopharyngitis, hypertension, urinary tract infection, influenza, and arthralgia. Urinary tract infection and arthralgia were identified as inclisiran-associated safety signals during the original NDA review. A drug association with angina, osteoarthritis, nasopharyngitis, hypertension, and influenza are not supported given a lack of corroboration in placebo-controlled data and the common nature of these AEs in the enrolled population, even outside of a clinical trial context. Injection site reactions that occurred throughout ORION-3 are similar in frequency and severity to what has been reported previously. No new safety concerns were identified.*
L239: ## 4.1.2. Phase 3b Trial: ORION-8
L240: ORION-8 is an ongoing long-term extension trial in 2990 subjects with ASCVD, ASCVD-risk equivalents (e.g., diabetes and familial hypercholesterolemia), HeFH, or HoFH, and elevated LDL-C despite maximum tolerated doses of statin therapy who have completed one of the phase 3 lipid-lowering trials (ORION-9, ORION-10, or ORION-11). Subjects who received placebo in one of these phase 3 trials initiated inclisiran treatment with the proposed dosage regimen (Day 1, Day 90, and then every 6 months). Subjects who received inclisiran in the previous trial continued their inclisiran treatment every 6 months (see figure below).
L241: A summary of safety data from 2967 subjects from ORION-8 were included in the Day 120 safety update in the original NDA submission (cut-off date was January 31, 2020). This current safety update includes the safety data from the trial initiation (first person first visit [FPFV] was April 16, 2019) until the cut-off date of May 10, 2021 on a total of 2990 subjects who consented to participate in this long-term extension trial and received inclisiran.

L247: Safety Population: all subjects who received at least one dose of study drug. Treatment
L248: classification was based on the actual treatment received. This was the primary population for the safety analyses.
L249: ## **Adverse Event Assessment:**
L250: - **AE Severity**1 = Mild: Discomfort noticed, but no disruption to daily activity.
- 2 = Moderate: Discomfort sufficient to reduce or affect normal daily activity.
- 3 = Severe: Inability to work or perform normal daily activity.
- New onset of diabetes in subjects with no medical history of diabetes was reported when:HbA1C becomes \ge 6.5\% and/or
- If a new concomitant medication for control of plasma glucose is added, further information to assess for a diagnosis of new onset diabetes will be collected
- 'Worsening of the glycemic control' or 'diabetic complications' in subjects with a medical history of disease (HbA1C \ge 6.5\% at baseline) was reported when:HbA1C increases from baseline &gt; 0.5\% and/or
- New concomitant medication or increase in dose of current antidiabetic therapy is initiated to improve the control of plasma glucose level.

L257: Additional information was requested from and provided by the Applicant for the subjects who discontinued for 'other' reasons. The reasons included that (1) there were 2 consecutive dosing visits missed in the trial, often because of COVID-19 issues; as a result, the subject had been without open-label inclisiran treatment for more than a year and was discontinued from the study at the sponsor's request, (2) subject's decision to not continue in the trial, some subjects were concerned that there would be too much risk of contracting COVID-19 when attending study visits at the research site, (3) study site closure, and (4) subject relocated and was no longer able to participate in the study.
L258: ## Demographics:
L259: The population in ORION-8 included 2032 (68%) male subjects, the mean age was 64 years, the median age was 65 years (min, max: 20, 90 years; 51% were age 65 and older), 93% of subjects were White, 6% were Black or African American, 0.8% were Asian, 0.2% were American Indian or Alaska Native, and 0.3% were Native Hawaiian or Other Pacific Islander. Subjects came from the US (43%), Poland (20%), UK (11%), South Africa (9%), Ukraine (3%), Hungary (3%), Spain (3%), Germany (2%), Denmark (2%), Netherlands (1%), Sweden (1%), Czech Republic (1%), and Canada (1%).
L260: ## Treatment Compliance:
L261: Subjects received SC doses of 300 mg from Day 1 through Day 810 as of the cut-off of May 10, 2021. The majority (&gt; 89%) of the injections were in the abdomen. Doses received were as follows:
L262: - Day 1 (100%; 2990/2990)
- Day 90 (98%; 2940/2990)

- Day 450 (92%, 2747/2990)
- Day 630 (74%, 2216/2990) and
- Day 810 (0%, 1/2990)
L264: ## Safety Data:
L265: The translation of investigator-reported terms for the adverse event to preferred terms was examined by this reviewer. Overall, few errors in translation were identified, and no adverse events were reclassified. Based on comparing the reported term to the dictionary-derived preferred term, this reviewer believes that AEs were generally categorized appropriately. Overall, this safety data submission, in particular the patient narratives, was high-quality, complete, and well-organized.

L271: ## Deaths:
L272: In ORION-8, seventy-eight subjects (3%) died during the trial, including 15 deaths at the Day 120 safety update and 63 deaths since the Day 120 safety update in the previous NDA review cycle (data cut-off of January 31, 2020). As shown in the following table, the cause of death was not predominated by a particular SOC or PT category, with the notable exception of deaths related
L273: to corona virus infection. There were in total 12 deaths due to COVID-19 (7 in PT COVID-19, 1 in COVID pneumonia, 3 in Pneumonia viral, 1 uncoded), confirmed by the narratives.
L274: For comparison with the placebo-controlled Phase 3 parent trials, a total of 54 deaths occurred during the 18 months of the 3 Phase 3 trials in the original NDA submission. The incidence of deaths on inclisiran and placebo was the same at 27 deaths or 1.5% on each treatment arm. The most common cause of death was in cardiac disorders and occurred at 0.7% on inclisiran as compared to 0.5% on placebo. Inclisiran did not appear to increase the risk of death from any particular cause.
L275: From the integrated summary of safety (ISS) pool of the original NDA submission, the incidence of deaths related to infections was similar in the two treatment arms, 4/1822 (0.2%) on placebo and 1/1833 (0.1%) on inclisiran. The incidence in ORION-8 (14/2990, 0.5%) was greater, likely from the higher number of deaths from infections (14 in ORION-8 vs. 1 in inclisiran-treated subjects in ISS safety pool). Twelve out of 14 deaths due to infections in ORION-8 was attributed to COVID-19.
L276: From the ISS of the original NDA, the incidence of deaths related to neoplasms was similar in the two treatment arms, 6/1822 (0.3%) on placebo and 4/1833 (0.2%) on inclisiran. The neoplasms in the placebo group, by PT, were adenocarcinoma, bladder cancer, gastroesophageal cancer, hepatocellular cancer, lung adenocarcinoma, metastases to liver, and metastatic carcinoma of the bladder. The neoplasms in the inclisiran group, by PT, were gastrointestinal lymphoma, lung neoplasm malignant, esophageal carcinoma, and metastases to liver. There was no pattern of carcinomas in the ISS that would point to a specific organ class vulnerable to inclisiran. A review of the neoplasms in Table 12, shows similar categories of malignancy, except for a small increase in pancreatic cancer.

L280: 15 Subject (b) (6): 63-year-old white male with PMH of ASCVD, DM, hyperlipidemia, hypertension, CABG, PCI. The patient received study drug on Days 1, 69 and 281. On Day 321, the patient was discharged to home following C3-C6 cervical laminectomy and fusion with the instruction of not to live alone initially. On an unspecified date, the patient had a fall in his house and was found by his brother and police twelve hours later with no movement unilaterally. On Day 325, the patient was hospitalized for treatment of SAE of rhabdomyolysis (total creatine kinase was 1286 U/L [6.4 x ULN; reference range: 30-200 U/L]). This reviewer believes the CK value is low for a diagnosis of drug-related rhabdomyolysis and that the unknown period of time on the floor after a fall, rather than study drug, contributed to the rhabdomyolysis. On Day 331, patient was obtunded, and overdose was suspected as narcotics and benzodiazepines were found at bedside. He was treated with naloxone intravenous drip. An imaging to the cervical spine showed hematoma of posterior neck so patient underwent revision laminectomy of C3-7 and hematoma evacuation. The patient then developed quadripareisis and mental status did not improve. On Day 336, the patient expired in the hospital. Although reported as a death due to rhabdomyolysis, it is unlikely that this death is drug-related.

L285: ## TESAEs:
L286: A total of 22% (658/2990) of subjects treated with inclisiran reported a TESAE, which is similar to the 20% (374/1833) of inclisiran-treated subjects in the ISS Safety Population pool of ORION-9, -10, and -11 who reported a TESAE (23% of subjects in the placebo group reported at least one SAE and the SAEs were largely balanced between the inclisiran and placebo groups).
L287: In ORION-8, the most frequent SAEs reported were in the SOC cardiac disorders (6.7%; 199/2990). The 5 most common TESAEs were in the PTs coronary artery disease (1.4%), angina pectoris (1.0%), acute myocardial infarction (0.9%), COVID-19 (0.9%), and ischemic stroke (0.7%). These events are not unexpected in an older trial population that includes patients with ASCVD, ASCVD risk equivalents, HeFH, or HoFH.

L301: A review of the subject narratives revealed some events that are likely or possibly attributable to inclisiran:
L302: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6): 79-year-old White male with PMH of hyperlipidemia, hypertension, MI, and multiple other chronic medical issues received study drug on Days 1 and 107. I agree with the investigator that this AE of ISR is likely due to study drug.

L303: study treatment and study due to the event of injection site reaction. The investigator considered the event of injection site reaction to be mild and reasonably possibly attributable to the study drug. I concur.

L307: In the ISS Safety Population pool of ORION-9, -10, and -11, the incidence of AE leading to withdrawal of study treatment was 2.5% (45/1833) on inclisiran and 1.9% (35/1822) on placebo. The most common preferred term as a reason for withdrawal of treatment was ISR
L308: (n=4, 0.2%) as compared to 0 patients on placebo. Other adverse reactions that led to withdrawal of treatment were in Neoplasms disorders and Musculoskeletal disorders with an approximately 0.2% greater incidence on inclisiran than placebo. With the exception of COVID-19 related AEs, the AEs leading to discontinuation in ORION-8 were similar to those seen in the original NDA submission.
L309: ## Common TEAEs:
L310: In ORION-8, the most common TEAEs (at least 2.0%) reported were diabetes mellitus (6.0%), arthralgia (4.7%), hypertension (4.4%), COVID-19 (4.0%), urinary tract infection (3.2%), back pain (3.1%), osteoarthritis (2.6%), injection site reaction (2.5%), upper respiratory infection (2.4%), nasopharyngitis (2.4%), angina pectoris (2.3%) and coronary artery disease (2.0%).
L311: Overall, 54% of AEs were considered mild in severity, 38% were moderate, and 8% were severe.
L312: In general, TEAEs occurred with similar incidence for both males and females in ORION-8. Notable exceptions are injection site reactions and urinary tract infections which occurred more frequently in women (see table below).

L317: In the placebo-controlled trials submitted with the initial NDA, the most common adverse reactions (\ge 3\%) in the inclisiran group, occurring more frequently than placebo, were ISR, arthralgia, urinary tract infection, diarrhea, bronchitis, pain in extremity, and dyspnea.
L318: In the Day 120 safety update for ORION-8 (cut-off date was January 31, 2020), where all subjects were on inclisiran, the most common TEAEs (at least 0.7%) were diabetes mellitus (1.9%), arthralgia (1.2%), back pain, hypertension, urinary tract infection and injection site reaction (0.9% for each PT), nasopharyngitis, coronary artery disease and osteoarthritis (0.8% for each PT), angina pectoris, injection site pain, upper respiratory tract infection and diarrhea (0.7% for each PT).
L319: With the exception of COVID-19 AEs, the PTs seen in the May 2021 cut-off date for ORION-8 are similar to what was seen at the Day 120 safety update, are not unexpected in the context of the enrolled trial population, and do not suggest additional inclisiran-associated safety signals beyond what was observed in the original NDA submission.
L320: ## AEs of Special Interest:Injection Site Reactions (ISRs)
L321: One hundred forty-six (146) out of 2990 (4.9%) subjects reported a TEAE at the injection site (using the MedDRA high-level term of injection site reactions). This incidence in ORION-8 was lower than the incidence of 8% in inclisiran-treated subjects within the ISS Safety Population (150/1833). A total of 5 (0.2%) subjects discontinued study drug due to ISRs (PTs of IS pruritus, IS rash, IS reaction).
L322: The ISRs in ORION-8 include PTs of injection site reaction (76 subjects, 2.5%), injection site erythema (20 subjects, 0.7%), injection site rash (14 subjects, 0.5%), injection site pruritus (5 subjects, 0.2%), injection site cellulitis (2 subjects, 0.1%), and injection site vesicles (1 subject, &lt;0.0%). The majority of subjects had mild AEs at the injection site (86%, 133/155); the remainder had moderate AEs (14%, 22/155). Medication was used to treat the AE at the injection site in 24% of cases. Two subjects (0.1%) had an AE of hypersensitivity.

L330: - Elevations in ALT (&gt;3 and ≤ 5x ULN) were observed in 6 (0.2%) subjects. Elevations in AST
L331: (&gt;3\times and \le 5\times ULN) were observed in 6 (0.2%) subjects and (&gt;5\times and \le 10\times ULN) was observed in 1 subject. An increase in bilirubin &gt; 2\times ULN was reported in 6 subjects (0.2%). There were no Hy's law cases.
L332: - Abnormal increases in serum creatinine (\ge 50\% increase from baseline or &gt; 2 mg/dL) were reported in 94 subjects (3.1%). For many of these subjects, they had elevated creatinine values at baseline that increased by 50% or more at Day 90; the creatinine levels decreased to some degree but did not return to baseline by Day 270. Many of these subjects have multiple underlying medical issues, such as DM, ASCVD, or hypertension, that can affect renal function over time, especially in an older population. Many had new medical issues or exacerbations of underlying medical issues that likely contributed to the increase in creatinine. Examples of some of these subjects are described in the next section.
- Abnormal increases in CK were reported in 13 subjects (0.4%) (&gt; 5\times to 10\times ULN: 12; &gt; 10\times to 20\times ULN: 1). In subjects who had CK data at a subsequent visit, all elevations normalized or significantly improved.

L334: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6), a 51-year-old male: Day 270 AST 6.7\times ULN, 15 days later was 0.5\times ULN. On Day 262, the patient was hospitalized due to a SAE of chronic pancreatitis exacerbation. This event likely contributed to the AST increase.

On Day 292, the patient was hospitalized for a diabetic ulcer that required amputation of a toe. On Day 311, the patient was hospitalized for localized foot infection and on Day 360 died from unknown causes. - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6): 61-year-old White male with a PMH of CAD, MI, hyperlipidemia, chronic kidney disease, prior implantable defibrillator insertion had a total bilirubin of 2.1 and 2.3\times ULN on Days 90 and 270, respectively. - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6): 74-year-old White male with PMH of CHF, DM, hyperlipidemia, hypertension, ischemic stroke, MI, CVD, CHD, chronic kidney disease who experienced multiple AEs during the trial including hospitalization for CHF exacerbation on Days 187 and 253, and acute kidney injury on Day 281 with creatinine of 2.5 found on routine visit. Stress test and catheterization showed worsening CAD, and the subject underwent a CABG 2 weeks later. This subject had a baseline creatinine of 1.96 mg/dL, Day 90 value of 1.81 and Day 270 value of 2.55 mg/dL. - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6): 72-year-old White female with PMH of CHF, DM, hyperlipidemia, hypertension, CABG, ischemic stroke, MI, PCI, chronic kidney disease, atrial fibrillation with pacemaker on the following medications: acetylsalicylic acid, amiodarone, amlodipine, atorvastatin calcium, bumetanide, escitalopram, glyceryl trinitrate, hydralazine, insulin glargine, levothyroxine, metoprolol tartrate, potassium chloride, and warfarin sodium.
