On Day 64, a repeat head CT revealed a SAE of small subdural hematoma. On Day 69, the patient recovered from encephalopathy, toxicity to various agents, and subdural hematoma and was discharged to an extended care facility. On Day 200, the patient was admitted with diagnosis of diarrhea (suspected C. difficile colitis) and acidosis. The patient experienced SAEs of severe septic shock, acute hypoxic respiratory failure, and urinary tract infection (UTI) and died on Day 201. She only received study drug on Days 1 and 90. Her underlying medical issues, including chronic kidney disease, and baseline medications likely contributed to the increase in creatinine.
- Subject [REDACTED](b) (6), 58-year-old male: Day 270 CK 12.7 x ULN; 9 days later CK was 0.3 x ULN. This event is unlikely to be related to study drug which was continued.
L337: There were no subjects with a clinically significant change in hematology parameters. However, as hematology and coagulation parameters were only tested at Day 1080 (\ge 90 days post final dose), few subjects (~20) in this on-going trial have data available for a Day 1080 assessment.
L338: ## **Anti-Inclisiran Antibodies:**
L339: In ORION-8, the planned LPLV date is at the end of February 2023. To date, ADA datasets are available for ORION-8 through the cut-off date of January 2020. As ADA samples for this trial are analyzed at specific milestones, additional long-term data will be available at the completion of this ongoing trial. For ORION-8, the majority of subjects had only reached the Day 90 visit in this trial at the most recent collection of ADA samples for evaluation (given the cut-off date of January 2020). Thus, the assessment of transient and persistent ADA response is not possible with only Day 90 visit data available. The percentage of subjects with treatment-induced ADA is summarized in the table below.
L340: **Table 17 Frequency (Percentage) Of Subjects with Treatment Induced ADA (ORION-8 Safety Population)**
L341: Inclisiran
(N=2967)
n/m* (%)
Treatment Induced ADA response **	45/2965 (1.5%)
Not Treatment-Induced ADA response	2920/2965 (98.5%)
L342: * n: # of subjects with anti-drug antibody (ADA) response; m: # of subjects tested for ADA.
L343: ** Treatment Induced ADA: negative ADA sample at baseline and at least one positive ADA sample post-baseline. Persistent: negative ADA at baseline and confirmed positive ADA on 2 or more timepoints where the first and last were separated by a period of at least 16 weeks or positive ADA at the last timepoint or at a timepoint less than 16 weeks before an ADA negative last sample.
L344: Transient: treatment-induced ADA detected only 1 time point post-baseline, or 2 or more sampling time points where the first and last ADA-positive samples are separated by a period less than 16 weeks, and the subject's last sampling time point is ADA-negative.
L345: Source: Tables 2-1, 2-2 and 2-3 from Novartis Responses to FDA Questions, dated November 2, 2021.
L346: Two of the 45 (4.4%) subjects with treatment-induced ADAs had an associated hypersensitivity event. The hypersensitivity events consisted of the AEs of "allergic conjunctivitis" and "eczema". Both AEs were mild in severity, not serious, not suspected to be related to study drug, and fully resolved during the study. The treatment-induced ADA response in ORION-8 (45/2965, 1.5%) is low and similar to that seen in the pooled phase 3 trials (72/1830, 3.9%).
L347: **Reviewer's Conclusion:** The overall safety profile for inclisiran in the phase 3 OLE trial ORION-8 was generally similar to that seen in the phase 3 trials included in the original NDA. No new safety concerns were identified.
L348: In ORION-8, the most frequent SAEs reported were in the SOC cardiac disorders (coronary artery disease, angina pectoris, acute myocardial infarction, and ischemic stroke) and COVID-19 infections. With the exception of COVID-19 SAEs, these SAEs were similar to those reported at
L349: *the Day 120 safety update and the original NDA submission.*
L350: *AEs that led to discontinuation included ischemic stroke, ISRs, COVID-19, myalgia, lung cancer, and dyspnea. The cases involving ISRs were considered reasonably attributable to study drug. With the exception of COVID-19 related AEs, the AEs leading to discontinuation in ORION-8 were similar to those seen in the original NDA submission.*
L351: *The most common AEs reported in ORION-8 were diabetes mellitus, arthralgia, hypertension, COVID-19, urinary tract infection, back pain, osteoarthritis, and injection site reaction. With the exception of COVID-19 AEs, these AEs were similar to those reported at the Day 120 safety update. In the placebo-controlled phase 3 trials submitted with the initial NDA, the most common adverse reactions that occurred more frequently in the inclisiran group were injection site reaction, arthralgia, urinary tract infection, diarrhea, bronchitis, pain in extremity, and dyspnea. The observed AEs were generally similar; however, diabetes mellitus was a common AE in ORION-8 but did not occur more frequently in the inclisiran group as compared to placebo in placebo-controlled data.*
L352: ## 4.1.3. Phase 3 Trial: ORION-5
L353: ORION-5 (CKJX839A12302) is an ongoing phase 3, two-part, double-blind, placebo-controlled followed by open-label, multicenter study evaluating the safety, tolerability, and efficacy of inclisiran in adult subjects with HoFH and elevated LDL-C despite maximum tolerated dose of LDL-C lowering therapies. The study has two sequential parts: part 1 (completed), a 6-month double-blind period in which subjects were randomized to inclisiran 300 mg or placebo, and part 2 (ongoing), an 18-month open-label follow-up period in which all subjects (including the placebo-treated subjects from part 1) are receiving inclisiran (see figure below).
L354: Figure 3 ORION-5: Trial Design
L355: Source: Figure 1-3 from safety_update_response and ORION-5 Study Protocol Figure 3-1
L356: FUP=follow-up; V=visit
L357: This study was initiated on February 6, 2019 and includes 56 subjects randomized 2:1 to either 300 mg inclisiran or placebo. The double-blind placebo-controlled portion of the trial (part 1) was completed in March 2020, and a total of 48 subjects are currently enrolled in the open-label part 2 of the trial with a final database lock planned in September 2021.
L358: Safety data from the completed double-blind phase (part 1) of the ORION-5 study in adults with HoFH, along with open-label data accrued prior to the cut-off date from part 2, is included in this safety update. Safety data for ORION-5, which were not submitted as part of the NDA, includes all data up to the cut-off date of May 10, 2021 and includes:
L359: - Part 1 (double-blind, placebo-controlled): Day 90 was the final dose of inclisiran or placebo, and the efficacy and safety endpoints were evaluated up to Day 180 (primary efficacy endpoint at Day 150).
- Part 2 (ongoing open-label phase): Starting on Day 180, subjects who received placebo started to receive inclisiran. Subjects who received inclisiran in part 1 continued to receive inclisiran every 6 months. Final inclisiran dosing of part 2 is on Day 630 and the last study visit is on Day 720.
L360: ## Safety Assessments:
L361: The translation of investigator-reported terms for the adverse event to preferred terms was examined by this reviewer. Overall, few errors in translation were identified, and no adverse events were reclassified. Based on comparing the reported term to the dictionary-derived preferred term, this reviewer believes that AEs were generally categorized appropriately. Overall, this safety data submission, in particular the patient narratives, was high-quality,
L362: complete, and well-organized.
L363: Refer to the Appendix for tables of study assessments for ORION-5.
L364: - Physical examination: screening and Day 720 (90 days post final dose)
- Vital signs (heart rate and blood pressure): Days 1, 90, 150, 180, 270, 450, 630, and 720 (90 days post final dose)
- ECG: screening and Day 720 (90 days post final dose)
- Hematology and coagulation: Days 1, 150, and 720 (90 days post final dose)Hemoglobin, hematocrit, erythrocytes, reticulocytes, MCH, MCV, MCHC, platelet count, white blood cell count with differential, PT, INR, and aPTT
- Full serum chemistry: Days 1 and 720 (90 days post final dose)AST, ALT, ALP, GGT, TBIL, direct and indirect bilirubin, CK, lactate, bicarbonate, uric acid, creatinine, BUN, eGFR, sodium, potassium, calcium, inorganic phosphate, chloride, albumin, total protein, glucose (fasting), HbA1C, and hsCRP
- Limited serum chemistry: screening, Days 90, 150, 180, 270, 330, 450, 510, and 690AST, ALT, ALP, GGT, TBIL, CK, creatinine, eGFR, fasting glucose, and HbA1C
- Urinalysis: screening, Days 1 and 720 (90 days post final dose)Dipstick analyses at the investigational site to include nitrite, protein, glucose, ketone, urobilinogen, bilirubin, red blood cells/erythrocytes, white blood cells/leukocytes, pH, urine sediment (microscopic examination only performed in the event of abnormalities)
- Anti-inclisiran antibodies: collected on Days 1, 90, 150, 330, and 720 (90 days post final dose)
L365: **Safety Population:** all subjects who received at least one dose of study drug. Treatment classification was based on the actual treatment received. This was the primary population for the safety analyses.
L366: **Adverse Event Assessment:**
L367: - AE Severity1 = Mild: Discomfort noticed, but no disruption to daily activity.
- 2 = Moderate: Discomfort sufficient to reduce or affect normal daily activity.
- 3 = Severe: Inability to work or perform normal daily activity.
- New onset of diabetes in subjects with no medical history of diabetes was reported when:HbA1C becomes \ge 6.5\% and/or
- Two consecutive values of fasting plasma glucose that are \ge 126 mg/dL
- If a new concomitant medication for control of plasma glucose is added, further
L368: information to assess for a diagnosis of new onset diabetes will be collected

- 'Worsening of the glycemic control' or 'diabetic complications' in subjects with a medical history of disease (\text{HbA1C} \ge 6.5\% at baseline) was reported when:\text{HbA1C} increases from baseline &gt;0.5\% and/or
- New concomitant medication or increase in dose of current antidiabetic therapy is initiated to improve the control of plasma glucose level.
L369: ## Exposure and Disposition:
L370: - Part 1: The median duration in the trial was 191 days (~0.5 years) for the inclisiran group and 188 days (~0.5 years) for the placebo group in ORION-5.
- Part 2: The median duration in the trial was 456 days (~1.4 years) for the inclisiran→inclisiran group and 440 days (~1.4 years) for the placebo→inclisiran group in ORION-5.
L371: A total of 56 subjects with HoFH were randomized in part 1 of ORION-5 and a total of 48 subjects are currently in the ongoing open-label part 2 of the trial. Duration on study and subject dispositions for part 1 and part 2 for the ORION-5 trial are summarized in the following tables.

L385: ## Demographics:
L386: The population studied in ORION-5 consisted of 61% female subjects, the mean age was 43 years, and 86% of subjects were White (see table below). Baseline demographics were reasonably well balanced between the inclisiran and placebo group, considering the small sample size for both groups.

L390: ## Treatment Compliance:
L391: In part 1 of the trial, most subjects received inclisiran on both scheduled treatment days through Day 180 as follows: on Day 1 (100%; 37/37) and Day 90 (94.6%; 35/37). Two subjects (3.6%) missed one dose in part 1 of the trial; 5 subjects (8.9%) missed one dose and 1 subject (1.8%) missed two doses in part 2 of the trial. Overall, a total of 9 doses were missed over the entire duration of the trial. The dose administered was 300 mg in all the subjects. The majority
L392: (≥ 96.4%) of the injections were in the abdomen.
L393: ## **Safety Data:**
L394: The adverse events observed in parts 1 and 2 are summarized in the following tables. For subjects exposed to inclisiran in parts 1 and 2, 55% reported at least one AE and 20% reported at least one SAE. Two deaths occurred in part 2 of the trial when all subjects were on inclisiran. No subject discontinued study drug due to a TEAE in either parts 1 or 2 of the trial.

L405: ## Deaths:
L406: Two inclisiran→inclisiran treated subjects died during part 2 of the trial. The first subject was a 66-year-old Asian male with a prior medical history of smoking, angina pectoris, myocardial infarction, and PCI. The cause of death was reported as cardiogenic sudden death. I agree with the investigator that the event is likely not related to the study medication and is related to underlying coronary artery disease. The second case was a 54-year-old White female with coronary heart disease, who had experienced SAEs of colon cancer, endocarditis, and urinary tract infection in the previous year, who was admitted to the intensive care unit in multiple organ failure and bacterial pneumonia. Eight days after admission, cardiopulmonary resuscitation was initiated after a cardiac arrest; the subject did not recover. This event was also not reasonably attributable to inclisiran based on the subject's underlying medical condition.

L421: In subjects treated with inclisiran in parts 1 and 2, 11 (20%) subjects reported a TESAE. L434: In part 2, angina pectoris was reported in 3 subjects who received inclisiran in part 1 and part 2 (inclisiran-&gt;inclisiran) vs. none in subjects who only received inclisiran in part 2 (placebo-&gt;inclisiran). As shown in the table below, aortic arteriosclerosis, aortic valve stenosis, diarrhea, pyrexia, and rhinorrhea were reported with a difference of 2 subjects (2 subjects vs. none in inclisiran-inclisiran vs. placebo-&gt;inclisiran). In general, the subjects in the 'inclisiran-inclisiran' group had 6 months greater duration of exposure to inclisiran than the 'transitioning from placebo to inclisiran' group. While diarrhea was also seen as a common TEAE in the placebo-controlled phase 3 trials and the placebo-controlled part 1 of ORION-5, some of the other AEs, such as angina pectoris, aortic arteriosclerosis, and aortic valve stenosis, more likely reflect the underlying high risk for CV events in this HoFH population. Imbalances in the AEs were not observed in placebo-controlled phase 3 data included with the original NDA submission.

L442: ## AEs of Special Interest:
L443: - Injection Site Reactions (ISRs): In part 1, there were no AEs at the injection site. In Part 2, one subject in the inclisiran→ inclisiran group reported injection site erythema, mild in severity.
L444: ## Chemistry Abnormalities:
L445: In part 1, there was a greater percentage of subjects with at least one abnormal chemistry value in the placebo group (47%) compared to the inclisiran group (38%). There was no evidence of an increase in new-onset DM in the inclisiran treated group as compared to placebo (9% vs 11%).
L446: When combining part 1 (inclisiran-treated only group) and part 2 (all subjects on inclisiran), 2 subjects had &gt;3 and ≤5 x ULN increases in ALT, 1 subject had a &gt;3 and ≤5 x ULN increase in AST, and 2 subjects had a ≥50% increase in serum creatinine from baseline (not &gt;2mg/dL). Based on the prompt resolution of these elevations as well as concurrent events, this reviewer does not believe the lab abnormalities are likely to be primarily related to study drug.
L447: ## For ALT/AST elevations:
L448: - Subject (b) (6) (28-year-old Asian male with HoFH on rosuvastatin 20 mg and ezetimibe): ALT was 1.3 x ULN on Day 1. ALT was 3 x ULN at Day 510 but was 1.7 x ULN by next visit, Day 630. The subject was on treatment on Day 510 (drug was not stopped
L449: as a result of the ALT elevation). Abnormal liver function test (mild) was recorded as an AE on Day 512, but no other AEs were recorded within a +/- 3-day window around the onset of the event. The investigator considered the AE to not be related to the study medication.
L450: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) (59-year-old White male with HoFH, HTN, CHF, prior MI, chronic cholecystitis and pancreatitis on aspirin, carvedilol, ramipril, rosuvastatin 40 mg, and triamterene): ALT was 2 x ULN on Day 1. ALT was 4.2 x ULN at Day 180 and 3.3 x ULN 2 weeks later. ALT was 1.3 x ULN at ~ Day 230. This subject also had AST 2.3 x ULN at baseline, 3.2 x ULN at Day 180 and 2 weeks after. AST was 1.1 x ULN at ~ Day 230.
L451: For creatinine elevations:
L452: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6): 41-year-old white female, baseline creatinine was 0.68 mg/dL, increased to 1.04 mg/dL at Day 270 and returned to baseline with 0.64 mg/dL at next visit, Day 450. Other notable events at that time were at Day 256, the patient was hospitalized due to a serious adverse event of angina unstable, underwent a coronarography which showed aortic valve stenosis, moderate aortic valve incompetence, and 85% stenosis and occlusion of the main left and right coronary artery. Approximately 2 months later, the subject was hospitalized for open-heart surgery because of stenotic coronary atherosclerosis and severe aortic valve stenosis.
- [REDACTED](b) (6): baseline creatinine was 0.65 mg/dL, increased to 1.03 mg/dL at Day 630, which was the last study visit prior to the DLP. Other notable events around that time were at Day 546, the patient was hospitalized due to a serious adverse event of degenerative aortic valve stenosis. The next day, transcatheter aortic valve implantation (TAVI) was performed. The procedure was complicated by development of a serious adverse event of pulmonary oedema. The patient was treated with sulzoncef, furosemide, propofol and methylprednisolone.

L469: ## Hematology Abnormalities:
L470: There were no subjects with a clinically significant change in hematology parameters in Part 1 or 2.
L471: In ORION-5, INR was only assessed at the screening visit, V1 (baseline), V3 (Day 150) and V11/EOS (Day 720). A total of 22 randomized subjects had at least one INR value &gt;1 \times ULN in the ORION-5 trial. Of these, nine subjects were on a vitamin K antagonist (such as warfarin) during the trial. Eight subjects had mild increase in INR values (sometimes at screening or at baseline) but these values all returned to normal by the end of study. Five subjects had INR values that remained elevated during the trial; additional information is provided on these 5 subjects:
L472: - 2 subjects had an elevated INR at baseline that remained elevated throughout the trial (Subjects [REDACTED](b) (6) and [REDACTED](b) (6)). Neither one of the subjects was on a vitamin K antagonist. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6), was on inclisiran in parts 1 and 2, had a screening INR of 1.6 and values of 1.3 on Day 1, 1.8 on Day 150 (subject had an apheresis session 3 days prior to this assessment) and 1.6 on Day 720. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6), was on placebo in part 1 and inclisiran in part 2, had a screening INR of 1.3 and values of 1.4 on Day 1, 1.3 on Day 150 and 1.5 on Day 720.
- 2 apheresis-treated subjects had fluctuating increases in INR during the trial (Subjects [REDACTED](b) (6) and [REDACTED](b) (6)). Their highest INR values coincided with the start of apheresis treatments. Parameters of the coagulation system may be altered during apheresis by the mandatory use of anticoagulants (heparin) as well as the type of LDL apheresis columns used.16,17
- Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) had an INR of 1.1 at Day 1 and 1.2 at Day 150. She had an abnormal INR value of 1.4 at Day 180 which was approximately 6 months following a single inclisiran injection in the entire study period. This subject, also discussed in the disposition section, had decided to withdraw from the trial approximately 2 months after a miscarriage.
L473: ## Anti-Inclisiran Antibodies:
L474: The ORION-5 trial had a recent database lock on October 22, 2021 with final and complete ADA datasets available for assessment. No subject in ORION-5 developed a treatment induced ADA response. This information represents the complete ADA data for this trial.

L477: *Reviewer's Conclusion: The overall safety profile for inclisiran in ORION-5 was generally similar to that seen in the phase 3 trials of the original NDA. No new safety issues were identified.*
L478: ## 4.1.4. Phase 3 Trial: ORION-4
L479: ORION-4 (CKJ839B12301): "Double-blind, Randomized, Placebo-controlled Trial Assessing the Effects of Inclisiran on Clinical Outcomes Among People with Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease"
L480: ## Open Session DMC Meeting Minutes for ORION-4 (CKJ839B12301):
L481: - August 13, 2019: 1570 patients randomized.The main reason for individuals failing screening is that total cholesterol (from desktop analyzer) is too low.
- In the UK, there are plenty of eligible individuals that have been identified and clinic space/people are being expanding.
- In the US, it has been difficult to identify those with high enough lipids. Sites will try to focus on individuals with more recent cholesterol measurements at pre-screening (as it is less likely they will have been started on LDL-C lowering therapy in the interim).
- February 4, 2020: 6400 patients randomized, 5300 in the UK and 1100 in the US.Continued issues with many screening failures due to patients not meeting the lipid threshold.
- There has been a low rate of dropout for intolerance during run-in and no SUSARs.
- FDA requested that 70% of participants be on high-intensity LDL-C lowering therapy. Currently have 43% on high-intensity LDL-C lowering therapy. It is hoped that this requirement will be relaxed if a subgroup analysis by level of LDL-C lowering therapy is conducted.
- August 6, 2020: 6400 patients randomized.Currently have 44% on high-intensity LDL-C lowering at baseline. A subgroup analysis by level of LDL-C lowering therapy (high-intensity, moderate-, low-intensity, or no statin) will be conducted (which would have 90% power in each of the categories).
- Level of LDL control is the same in those on high-intensity LDL-C lowering therapy as those on medium- or low-intensity.
- Impact of COVID-19:Recruitment was paused in mid-March and face-to-face follow-up visits were suspended in both the UK and US.
- Follow-up has continued remotely, but participants have been unable to receive study medication.
L482: - Adjudication issue: receiving hospital documents with appropriate handling (redacting information etc.) when nurses are not in the hospital and not able to access records remotely.
- February 11, 2021: 7951 patients randomized.Impact of COVID-19:As of October 2020, there were around 4000 participants that had not been dosed in the last 6 months, but this has now been reduced to about 2100 participants.
- New procedure for home administration of injections has been developed to allow all participants in UK to be able to receive study treatment.
- IT infrastructure is now ready, but adjudication has not yet started due to the difficulty with getting access to records during the pandemic.
L483: *Reviewer's Conclusion: Inclusion criteria for the ORION-4 CVOT includes a baseline LDL-C of 100 mg/dL or greater threshold. The DMC meetings state that there are a large number of screening failures due to patients not meeting the lipid threshold. This is particularly true for the US (less of an issue in the UK). In addition, they are struggling to meet the requirement for 70% of participants to be on high-intensity LDL-C lowering therapy, as requested by FDA. As of August 2020, only 44% of enrolled subjects were on high-intensity LDL-C lowering therapy at baseline. Both issues (high numbers of screening failures and a reduced number of participants on high-intensity statin therapy may have been alleviated by having the baseline LDL-C threshold set at 70 mg/dL. However, the sponsor chose an LDL-C of 100 mg/dL in order to enroll subjects at higher baseline CV risk.*
L484: *There are issues with enrollment, dosing, and a delay in adjudication due to COVID-19. However, there does not appear to be any notable safety issues due to inclisiran administration. Of note, based on the company's response to an FDA query, 19 (0.2%) subjects from October 30, 2018 to September 19, 2021 from ORION-4 discontinued study treatment due to use of other inhibitors of PCSK9, based on 8,185 randomized subjects to date. Six (0.1%) subjects are from the UK and 13 (0.8%) are from the US.*
L485: ## 4.2. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L486: ## 4.2.1. Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L487: The Applicant reports that the post-marketing safety reports received prior to the cut-off date of this submission (May 10, 2021) are very limited and do not reveal any new significant safety information. Nine non-serious post-marketing case reports were received (7 from Germany and 2 from Austria), and the adverse events reported in these nine cases are reportedly consistent with the experience in the phase 3 trials. None of the cases resulted in treatment
L488: discontinuation.
L489: ## 4.2.2. Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L490: The use and administration of inclisiran in the postmarket setting is anticipated to be generally similar to its use and administration in clinical trials. Inclisiran was well-tolerated, and the most important safety issue observed in phase 3 trials was injection site reactions. Inclisiran will be administered by a health care provider in the clinic setting, as it was during the phase 3 trials. No additional risk management activities in the postmarket setting, e.g., REMS, are anticipated.
L491: ## 4.3. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L492: ORION-3, -8, and -5 were well-executed and largely adhered to the protocol. The protocol amendments were reasonable and unlikely to have had a negative impact on the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the results. There were no potential issues concerning the submitted data quality or integrity that raised questions about the safety results. Thus, these trials have generated data that are interpretable and supportive of the known safety profile of inclisiran.
L493: Treatment emergent deaths and SAEs, with the exception of COVID-19-related SAEs, were similar to those reported in the original NDA and in the Day 120 safety update. Cardiovascular-related, neoplasms, and infection-related (COVID-19, pneumonia) SAEs were the most common. AEs that led to study drug discontinuation included ISRs, ischemic stroke, COVID-19-related, and malignancy-related AEs. The cases involving ISRs were likely attributable to inclisiran. Other causes of death, SAEs, and AEs leading to drug discontinuation (i.e., cardiovascular and malignancy) are not unexpected in a trial population of older subjects with established ASCVD or ASCVD risk equivalents.
L494: Common AEs included diabetes mellitus, nasopharyngitis, COVID-19, hypertension, urinary tract infection, influenza, back pain, and arthralgia. An association with inclisiran was only established for urinary tract infection and arthralgia based on placebo-controlled phase 3 data. Other observed common AEs were not corroborated by placebo-controlled data, occur frequently even outside of clinical trials (e.g., back pain, nasopharyngitis), and are common for the enrolled trial population (e.g., diabetes mellitus, hypertension).
L495: Injection site reactions were reported in 13.2% of ORION-3 subjects (49/371) and 4.9% of ORION-8 subjects (146/2990), compared to 8.2% of inclisiran-treated subjects (150/1833) in the safety pool of ORION-9, -10 and -11 (Safety Population of the original NDA; 1.8% (33/1822) of placebo-treated subjects reported ISRs). The higher incidence of ISRs in ORION-3 may be due to its longer study duration. Only one AE at the injection site (1/56, 1.8%) was reported in ORION-
L496: 5 in the inclisiran-inclisiran treatment group. These AEs at the injection site in ORION-3, -8 and -5 were localized, predominantly mild or occasionally moderate, and transient in nature.
L497: Clinically significant changes in laboratory parameters were observed at low frequency in the submitted open-label data but were mostly related to underlying disease and not corroborated by placebo-controlled data in the original NDA. There were no Hy's law cases.
L498: In the phase 3 trials (ORION-9, -10, and -11), the incidence of subjects with a treatment-induced ADA response was 3.9% and titers were generally low. Approximately 2.2% of subjects had transient ADA, while 1.7% had persistent ADA. ADA presence did not appear to be associated with hypersensitivity events. The available data from ORION-3, -8, and -5 are consistent with those findings. There does not appear to be an association between the presence of transient or persistent ADA and hypersensitivity events. The observed frequencies for persistent ADA in ORION-3 and -5 and transient ADA in ORION-3, -8, and -5 are low and similar to those reported in the Safety Pool submitted in the original NDA.
L499: Inclisiran was generally well-tolerated, and the most important safety issue was injection site reactions in the phase 2 and phase 3 trials. Few inclisiran treated subjects withdrew from treatment due to adverse reactions at the injection site.
L500: Based on my review of new and updated safety data, the safety profile of inclisiran in subjects with ASCVD or HeFH and elevated LDL-C is consistent with that previously reported in the original NDA submission. Inclisiran continues to have an acceptable safety profile for its intended use in adults with ASCVD or HeFH.
L501: ## 5. Labeling Recommendations

L503: The majority of labeling decisions were conveyed to the Applicant during the initial NDA review cycle. Refer to the initial primary clinical review document for this information (in DARRTS, dated November 6, 2020).

- adding "none" to the Contraindications in highlights and Section 4,
- inclusion of additional requested information on immunogenicity in Section 6.2, and
- revision of information in Section 11 that clarifies the classification text that inclisiran sodium is a small interfering RNA (siRNA) (b) (4) PCSK9 (proprotein convertase subtilisin kexin type 9) mRNA.
L506: ## 6. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L507: ## Postmarketing Requirements
L508: - Conduct an efficacy and safety study evaluating inclisiran in patients with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) aged 12 years to less than 18 years. The Phase 3 study will be a randomized, double-blind, placebo controlled, parallel group, 12-month, multicenter efficacy and safety study in 150 patients (randomized 2:1 to inclisiran and placebo), followed by a 12-month open-label extension with all patients assigned to inclisiran in pediatric patients aged 12 years to less than 18 years with HeFH on maximally tolerated, stable lipid-modifying therapy with LDL-C \ge130 mg/dL. Reference is made to the FDA Agreed initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP) dated November 28, 2018 and the FDA Written Request for inclisiran dated November 1, 2019 (DARRTS Reference ID: 4514598).

Final protocol for ORION-16 (CKJX839C12301) was submitted to FDA July 22, 2020. DDLO stated the final protocol was acceptable on January 5, 2021.
- Study Completion: June 2025
- Final Report Submission: December 2025
- Conduct an efficacy and safety study evaluating inclisiran in patients with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) aged 6-7 if on plasma apheresis and 8 to &lt;12 years of age. The Phase 3 study will be a randomized, double-blind, placebo controlled, parallel group, 12-month, multicenter efficacy and safety study (b) (4), followed by a 12-month open-label extension with all patients assigned to inclisiran in pediatric patients aged 6-7 (if on plasma apheresis or meet eligibility criteria for lipid apheresis from their home country) and 8 years to less than 12 years with HeFH on maximally tolerated, stable lipid-modifying therapy (b) (4).

Draft Protocol Submission: December 2023
- Final Protocol Submission: June 2024
- Study Completion: June 2027
- Final Report Submission: December 2027The Applicant submitted new proposed milestone dates because of recruitment delays due to the ongoing COVID-19 pandemic in post-marketing study 1 which is currently ongoing in adolescent patients 12 to &lt; 18 years of age. The FDA Written Request for the pediatric HeFH studies requests 1-year interim data from the ongoing adolescent study to better inform the dosing and safety in the younger pediatric patients, 8 to &lt; 12 years of age. Due to the recruitment challenges in the adolescent study, 1-year interim data will be delayed impacting the lower pediatric age group study timelines.
L509: 3. Conduct a worldwide, descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to inclisiran during pregnancy to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant. Infant outcomes will be assessed through at least the first year of life. The minimum number of patients will be specified in the protocol.

- Protocol Submission including first interim report: March 2022
- Interim Study Report: March 2022 and annually
- Study Completion: December 2030
- Final Study Report: July 2031The Applicant submitted new proposed milestone dates to reflect the ongoing status of the study globally. The study protocol has been finalized with other global health authorities to support commitments linked to inclisiran approval in other countries. Novartis has already initiated data collection from the countries where inclisiran has been approved. Novartis states that the revised timetable will allow them to keep the same timetable of annual interim report submissions already agreed to with other global health authorities worldwide.
L510: ## Postmarketing Commitments
L511: - Validate the sensitivity and all other validation parameters except cutpoints for the ELISA method used to detect anti-inclisiran-reactive IgG/IgM antibodies in human serum, employing an affinity-purified anti-inclisiran antibody positive control (i.e., Rabbit Anti- KLH-TTRSC-2017 antiserum) to ensure that the assay is sensitive, specific, and selective for measurement of ADA responses. Validate the assay in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry entitled "Immunogenicity Testing of Therapeutic Protein Products —Developing and Validating Assays for Anti-Drug Antibody Detection".Study Completion: August 2022
- Final Report Submission: October 2022
- Re-evaluate anti-inclisiran-reactive IgG/IgM antibodies in all clinical samples using the appropriately validated assay. Re-assess the impact of anti-inclisiran-reactive IgG/IgM antibodies on pharmacokinetics, efficacy, and safety.Study Completion: July 2023
- Final Report Submission: August 2023The Applicant submitted new proposed milestone dates for these two PMCs. The Applicant states since inclisiran is a novel siRNA, there are many complexities to produce new antibody positive control (PC) reference material. The monoclonal antibodies production will likely be ready in the last quarter of 2021, however, the corresponding polyclonal antibodies positive control will not be available until the second half of
L512: 2022. Due to the availability of the PC, the previously provided timelines are outdated and Novartis is requesting an extension.

L546: ADA = anti-drug antibodies; AE = adverse event; ECG = electrocardiogram; FU=follow-up; EOS = end of study; HbA1c= glycated hemoglobin A1C; hsCRP=high sensitivity C-reactive protein; IL6=interleukin 6; IFN-\gamma=interferon gamma; LDL-C = low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; PCSK9 = proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9; S = Stored sample; SAE = serious adverse event; TNF-\alpha=tumor necrosis factor alpha.
L547: a Observation visits every 30 days (\pm7 days) until study drug is available.
L548: b Vital signs: blood pressure and heart rate will be measured prior to injection. On Day 360 (transition visit), vital signs will also be measured at 4 hours after injection.
L549: c ECG should be performed prior to the injection.
L550: d Hematology, chemistry (including glucose, liver and renal function, hsCRP, IL6, IFN-\gamma, and TNF-\alpha), and coagulation testing (of which some will be collected and stored for future analyses). Blood samples for determination of laboratory values will be performed prior to study drug injection. All laboratory testing will be performed with subjects in a fasted state. See Section 7.1.6 for a list of laboratory parameters. Lab tests performed by study's designated Central Lab facility from entry into the study to EOS. On days when inclisiran is injected, if indicated, the investigator will need to collect a blood sample for tryptase within 30 minutes of the onset of anaphylaxis (or as soon as logistically possible).
L551: e Physical and neurological examinations, weight, and ECG performed once yearly.
L552: f Visit windows are \pm 2 days for the Day 30 visit, and \pm 7 days from Day 90 onward.
L553: g Urinalysis collection is prior to the injection on Day 1; supplies from the Central Lab must be used to perform the urinalysis.
L554: h Urine pregnancy test performed and results available prior to the injection on Day 1; supplies from the Central Lab must be used to perform the test.
L555: i Women of childbearing potential will have a pregnancy test at each visit.
L556: j On Day 360 two ADA samples will be drawn: one before the injection and one 4 hours after the injection.
L557: k Plasma glucose and lactate will be collected at intervals throughout the study and stored for future analyses. See Section 7.1.8 for details. Additional blood samples will be collected every 180 days and aliquots of plasma and serum stored for future analyses.
L558: l Diary cards should be turned in to study site coordinator at follow-up visits on Day 30, 90, 180, 270 and 360.
L559: m Efficacy parameters will include LDL-C, total cholesterol, triglycerides, HDL-C, non-HDL-C, very low-density lipoprotein (VLDL-C), C-reactive protein (CRP), and PCSK9. A standard lipid panel will be done at all visits (Day 1 to EOS) however some PCSK9 samples will be stored for future analyses and ultracentrifugation is to be done only at Day 1, Day 210, Day 360, Day 510, Day 720 and EOS.
L560: n Day 1 in clinic; Days 14 through 336 to be self-injected according to label; for subjects in Transition 2; at Visit Day 360 in the clinic.
L561: o Until study drug is available, subjects will attend a basic observation visit at 30 day intervals. These visits will include safety labs (hematology, chemistry, coagulation) and recording of AEs, SAEs, and concomitant medications.
L562: p Only subjects in Transition 2 will receive a final dose of evolocumab at Visit Day 360 with the first dose of inclisiran.
L563: q HbA1c assessed every 270 days. Therefore, HbA1C to be assessed at follow-up visits on Days 270, 540, 810, 1080, and 1350.
L564: r Subjects will be observed in the clinic for at least 4 hours post their first inclisiran injection before being discharged
L565: s EOS will occur 90 days following the final administration of study drug. However, if the study ends early or if the subject discontinues the study, the EOS visit should occur at least 30 days following the final administration of study drug.
L566: t There should be a minimum of 7 days between Study Entry or last observation visit and Day 1.

L582: 1. EOS visit should occur at least 90 days following the last inclisiran dose once a decision is made to end the study (either by the subject, investigator or sponsor). For subjects prematurely and permanently discontinued from study treatment, who are not willing to return within the 90 day timeframe, the EOS visit will be scheduled as soon as possible, or if decision to discontinue and not return is made at a specific visit, this visit will become the EOS visit and EOS visit procedures should be followed.
L583: 2. Day 1 visit is the same as EOS visit in the previous qualifying ORION study. Aside from informed consent and eligibility, procedures will not be repeated but will be those captured during feeder study EOS visit except for subjects enrolled in Sweden. See Section 3.1 and Section 6.3 Sweden Specific Protocol Amendment 1 (to Global Amendment 1 version), dated 1 Nov 2018.
L584: 3. Dosing visits will occur every 180 days until the study ends for each respective subject. Visit windows have been extended to \pm2 months due to COVID-19.
L585: - Vital signs: blood pressure and heart rate will be measured prior to injection on dosing days. When available, an automated BP device is recommended for collection of BP and the result recorded to the nearest mmHg. The subject should be sitting at rest for at least 5 minutes prior to these assessments. One assessment for each vital sign (HR, BP) is required per applicable visit.
- See Section 7.1.6.3 for details of specific tests to be analyzed
- See Section 7.1.6.1 and Section 7.1.6.2 for details of specific tests to be analyzed
- Urinalysis collection is prior to the injection; supplies from the central laboratory must be used to perform the urinalysis
- Only in women of childbearing potential (performed locally, prior to any dosing, using central laboratory kit supplies and with results prior to injection; urine pregnancy test). Results of the pregnancy test from Day 540 of the feeder study will be used on Day 1 in ORION-8
- ADA samples will be drawn prior to injection
- Subjects who received blinded placebo in the feeder study will receive blinded inclisiran and subjects who received blinded inclisiran in the feeder study will receive blinded placebo on Day 1 in ORION-8. Subjects moving over from the ORION-3 trial will not receive any treatment at Day 1; all subjects were on inclisiran in the feeder trial and will receive their first dose of inclisiran in this study at day 90
- Subjects will be observed in the clinic for 30 minutes post injection in order to have additional vital and/or laboratory assessments completed if needed. For any suspected episode of anaphylaxis, the investigator will need to collect a blood sample for tryptase within 30 minutes of an onset of anaphylaxis (or as soon as logically possible).
- Concomitant medications (relevant) and AE/SAE collection for this study will begin at time of consent and continue through EOS. Ongoing concomitant medications and AEs/SAEs from the feeder study will continue to be followed in this study until resolution.

L604: Application Type	NDA
Application Number(s)	214012
Priority or Standard	Standard
Submit Date(s)	23 December 2019
Received Date(s)	23 December 2019
PDUFA Goal Date	23 December 2020
Division/Office	DDLO/OCHEN/CDER
Reviewer Name(s)	Eileen Craig, MD (efficacy) and Iffat Chowdhury, MD (safety)
Review Completion Date	4 November 2020
Established/Proper Name	Inclisiran
(Proposed) Trade Name	Leqvio
Applicant	The Medicines Company/Novartis Pharmaceuticals Co.
Dosage Form(s)	Subcutaneous (SC) injection
Applicant Proposed Dosing Regimen(s)	284 mg administered on Day 1, again at 3 months, then every 6 months.
Applicant Proposed Indication(s)/Population(s)	An adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with primary hyperlipidemia (including heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia) to reduce low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C)
Recommendation on Regulatory Action	Approval
Recommended Indication(s)/Population(s) (if applicable)	As an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) or clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (CVD), who require additional lowering of low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C).

L624: ## 1. Executive Summary
L625: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L626: Inclisiran (proposed proprietary name of LEOVIO), a new molecular entity (NME), is a double-stranded short interfering ribonucleic acid (siRNA), which inhibits hepatic production of proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9 (PCSK9). The reduction in intrahepatic PCSK9 levels leads to increased recycling and expression of the LDL-C receptor on the hepatocyte cell surface. This leads to increased LDL-C uptake and lowering of LDL-C levels in the circulation.
L627: Inclisiran was developed as a single use prefilled syringe (300 mg inclisiran sodium in 1.5 mL aqueous solution containing 284 mg of inclisiran) for administration by a healthcare professional. Inclisiran is administered as a subcutaneous injection given at Day 1, again at 3 months, then every 6 months.
L628: The applicant proposes the following indication: INCLISIRAN is indicated as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with primary hyperlipidemia (including heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia) to reduce low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C).
L629: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L630: This application contains substantial evidence that the drug is effective for its intended use. LDL-C is a valid surrogate endpoint for evaluating risk reduction of cardiovascular disease (CVD). Based on data showing robust LDL-C reductions and an acceptable safety profile, this reviewer recommends approval for the following indication:
L631: - INCLISIRAN is indicated as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) or clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (CVD), who require additional lowering of low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C).
L632: It is important to note that the effect of inclisiran on cardiovascular morbidity and mortality has not been determined. Therefore, inclisiran is not indicated for the treatment of patients with primary hyperlipidemia without established clinical atherosclerotic CVD.
L633: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment
L634: ## Benefit-Risk Integrated Assessment
L635: Inclisiran (proposed proprietary name of LEQVIO) is a double-stranded short interfering ribonucleic acid (siRNA) that lowers LDL-C levels in the circulation. The applicant proposes that inclisiran is indicated as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with primary hyperlipidemia (including heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia) to reduce low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C). Based on data showing robust LDL-C reductions and an acceptable safety profile with inclisiran, this reviewer recommends approval of inclisiran as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) or clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (CVD), who require additional lowering of LDL-C.
L636: Hyperlipidemia, specifically an increase in LDL-C levels, is a major modifiable risk factor for the development of atherosclerosis and atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD). ASCVD continues to be the leading cause of death and disability worldwide. The goal of lipid-lowering therapy is to reduce the risk for cardiovascular disease. Patients with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HoFH), a rare and life-threatening disorder, comprise a subpopulation whose disease is particularly severe and difficult to treat. This submission does not address the treatment of HoFH.
L637: Current treatment guidelines emphasize reducing the risk of ASCVD through lipid management, based on an individualized approach which considers a patient's overall risk for a CV event. Moderate- or high-intensity statins are considered first-line therapy for all patients who require LDL-C reduction, depending on their risk category. Ezetimibe and/or PCSK9 inhibitors are considered as second-line, add-on therapies to a background of maximally tolerated statin therapy in patients at highest CV risk (secondary prevention and HeFH) who require additional LDL-C reduction.
L638: The phase 3 trials, ORION-9, ORION-10 and ORION-11, included a population of approximately 3500 patients with HeFH or ASCVD with elevated LDL-C despite maximally tolerated statin therapy who were treated with inclisiran sodium 300 mg administered subcutaneously at Day 1, Day 90, and every 6 months afterward for a duration of 18 months. The study population included relevant comorbidities such as increased age, renal impairment, hepatic impairment, hypertension and diabetes. The data from these 3 trials demonstrated durable, clinically meaningful LDL-C reduction (48-52%) with supportive results in total cholesterol, non-HDL-C and apo-B endpoints. The majority of subjects in the inclisiran group experienced a sizable LDL-C reduction at Day 510 compared to the placebo group. Consistent, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percent changes from baseline to Day 510 were seen across various subpopulations defined by baseline demographic characteristics and disease comorbidities.
L639: Inclisiran has a prolonged pharmacodynamic effect on LDL-C levels which allows for infrequent dosing, which may be preferable to some patients. It is possible, but not proven, that less frequent dosing with inclisiran may improve a patient's adherence. However, unlike alirocumab, evolocumab and statins, we do not have CV outcome data with inclisiran that confirms a reduction in CV morbidity and mortality, nor do we have the long-term safety data assessment that is possible from a CVOT that is several years in duration and enrolls thousands of patients. The lack of this data will limit the scope of the indication and thus supports a narrower indication than the applicant's proposed indication.
L640: Patients that have defective LDL-C receptors and consequent diminution in LDL receptor activity, particularly patients with HoFH who are LDL receptor-negative, have a decreased LDL-C lowering response to drugs like statins and PCSK9 inhibitors that work through the LDL-C receptor. Inclisiran is therefore unlikely to show a sizable effect in these patients who have failed to show a meaningful LDL-C response to statins and PCSK9 inhibitor monoclonal antibodies.
L641: Review of safety data identified few issues; these findings—either individually or collectively—do not outweigh efficacy in the intended population. More patients assigned to inclisiran reported and discontinued study drug due to injection site reactions compared with patients assigned to placebo, but no serious or severe reactions occurred. Other events with small imbalances greater in the inclisiran group included arthralgia and increases in blood glucose in patients with diabetes/impaired fasting glucose. The incidence of adverse events of concern, including hypersensitivity, neurocognitive events, renal events, increases in liver transaminase and ophthalmological events, were similar between the inclisiran and placebo groups. The incidence of persistent anti-drug antibodies was low and was not associated with attenuation of LDL-C lowering.
L642: The benefit-risk analysis for inclisiran is favorable for an indication as an adjunct to maximally tolerated statin therapy in high-risk patients with clinical CVD or HeFH, who require additional LDL-C lowering. The data support approval for these conditions of use. The effect of inclisiran on cardiovascular morbidity and mortality has not been determined. Therefore, inclisiran is not indicated for the treatment of patients with non-familial hyperlipidemia without established clinical atherosclerotic CVD. The applicant will be issued postmarketing requirements (PMRs) to conduct phase 3 efficacy and safety studies evaluating inclisiran in patients with HeFH ages 8 years (or ≥6 years of age and on apheresis) to less than 18 years. In addition, the applicant will be issued a postmarketing commitment (PMC) to complete the ongoing randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled multicenter trial in approximately 15,000 patients (randomized 1:1 to inclisiran and placebo) designed to assess the effects of inclisiran on the occurrence of major cardiovascular events.
L643: ## Benefit-Risk Dimensions
L644: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	
Large burden of cardiovascular (CV) disease worldwide and in the US:
CVD prevalence in United States excluding hypertension (CHD, HF, and stroke only) is 9% overall (24.3 million in 2016)1
Leading cause of death in United States
$351 billion annual cost of CVD and stroke in US (2015)1
LDL-C reduction with statins and PCSK9 inhibitors is associated with improved CV outcomes:
Meta-analysis of statin trials demonstrated that an absolute reduction of 38.7 mg/dL (1 mmol/L) with statins is associated with a 22% relative risk reduction in 5-year incidence of major vascular events (non-fatal myocardial infarction, coronary death, ischemic stroke, or coronary revascularization).2
CV outcomes trials (CVOTs) of the two approved PCSK9 inhibitors, alirocumab and evolocumab, demonstrated that reducing LDL-C levels led to a reduction in risk of CV events.3,4
LDL-C reduction with other agents is used in high-risk patients who require additional LDL-C lowering:
LDL-C reduction with therapies targeting upregulation of the LDL-C receptor is the cornerstone of preventive treatment for CV disease.
Statins are considered first-line, standard-of-care for lowering LDL-C with the goal of reducing CV risk.
CVOTs of statins and monoclonal antibody PCSK9 inhibitors demonstrated that reducing LDL-C levels with these agents led to a reduction in risk of CV events.

Benefit	
In patients with established ASCVD who take inclisiran for secondary prevention as add-on to background statin therapy, inclisiran lowers LDL compared to placebo:
At Day 510, -51% to -52% reduction
In patients with HeFH who take inclisiran as add-on to background statin therapy, inclisiran lowers LDL compared to placebo:
At Day 510, -48% reduction
A cardiovascular outcomes trial to evaluate the benefit of inclisiran as an adjunct to statin therapy is ongoing.
Inclisiran has a robust LDL-C lowering treatment effect. The LDL-C lowering treatment effect of inclisiran is similar to that achieved with alirocumab and evolocumab; however, alirocumab and evolocumab have both demonstrated CV risk reduction in outcomes trials. The LDL-C lowering treatment effect of inclisiran is greater than that of ezetimibe, bempedoic acid, and low-intensity statins; ezetimibe and low-intensity statins have been evaluated in CV outcomes trials.
In the absence of outcomes data, the clinical benefit is uncertain. Nonetheless, inclisiran provides an additional option for patients who

L659: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Risk and Risk Management	
In phase 3 trials, inclisiran was associated with:
Injections site reactions (8.2% of patients) compared to placebo (1.8%)
Arthralgia (5.0% versus 4.0% in placebo)
Urinary tract infections (4.4% versus 3.6% in placebo)
Diarrhea (3.9% versus 3.5% in placebo)
Bronchitis (4.3% versus 2.7% in placebo)
require LDL-C lowering despite optimized available therapies.
Inclisiran is associated with injection site reactions (ISR) that were mild to moderate in severity and transient in nature. The risk for ISR is monitorable, and can be adequately addressed through labeling.
Other adverse events with imbalances were infrequent and included small increases in blood glucose in patients with diabetes/ impaired glucose tolerance. However, new onset diabetes incidence was similar to placebo.
Adverse events of special interest, including hypersensitivity, hepatic events, renal events, neurological/ neurocognitive events, and ophthalmological events, demonstrated rates similar to placebo.

L663: ## 2. Therapeutic Context
L664: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L665: Hypercholesterolemia, specifically an increase in low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) levels, is a major modifiable risk factor for the development of atherosclerosis and atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD). ASCVD comprises a variety of diseases with similar pathophysiology (atherosclerosis) of the heart and blood vessels, including coronary heart disease (CHD), acute coronary syndrome (ACS), and stroke.
L666: ASCVD continues to be the leading cause of death and disability worldwide.12 On the basis of NHANES 2013 to 2016 data, the prevalence of CVD (comprising CHD, HF, stroke, and hypertension) in adults \ge20 years of age is 48% overall (121.5 million in 2016) and increases with age in both males and females. CVD prevalence excluding hypertension (CHD, HF, and stroke only) is 9% overall (24.3 million in 2016).1 In the United States, heart disease is the leading cause of death for men and women, including most racial and ethnic groups.13 Approximately 647,000 Americans die from heart disease each year.14 The average annual direct and indirect cost of CVD and stroke in the United States was an estimated $351.2 billion in 2014 to 2015.15
L667: Many large-scale, randomized trials have shown that reducing LDL-C levels with statins reduces the risk of CHD, with a direct relationship between LDL-C levels and CHD events. One meta-analysis concluded that lowering LDL-C by 1 mmol/L (38.7 mg/dL) for 4 to 5 years reduces the risk of major vascular events (non-fatal myocardial infarction, coronary death, ischemic stroke, or coronary revascularization) by 22%.16 Several trials have shown that statin regimens using higher doses or more-potent agents, which both yield greater reductions in LDL-C, reduce the risk of vascular events more than less-intensive
L668: 12 Foreman KJ, Marquez N, Dolgert A et al. Forecasting life expectancy, years of life lost, and all-cause and cause-specific mortality for 250 causes of death: reference and alternative scenarios for 2016-40 for 195 countries and territories. Lancet. 2018; 392: 2052-90.
L669: 13 Heron, M. Deaths: Leading causes for 2017. National Vital Statistics Reports;68(6). Accessed May 1, 2020.
L670: 14 https://www.cdc.gov/heartdisease/facts.htm. Accessed May 1, 2020.
L671: 15 Benjamin EJ, Muntner P, Alonso A, Bittencourt MS, Callaway CW, Carson AP, et al. Heart disease and stroke statistics—2019 update: a report from the American Heart Association. Circulation. 2019;139(10):e56-528.
L672: 16 Cholesterol Treatment Trialists' (CTT) Collaboration. Efficacy and safety of more intensive lowering of LDL cholesterol: a meta-analysis of data from 170000 participants in 26 randomised trials. Lancet 2010;376:1670-1681.
L673: statin regimens in patients at very high cardiovascular risk.17,18,19,20 More recently, CV outcomes trials of the two approved monoclonal antibody PCSK9 inhibitors, alirocumab and evolocumab, also demonstrated that reducing LDL-C levels with these agents led to a reduction in risk of CV events.21,22
L674: A CVOT with ezetimibe demonstrated incremental benefit (6% relative risk reduction) with moderate LDL-C lowering23. (b) (4)
L675: Other agents, namely colesevelam and bempedoic acid, may be used as adjuncts to statins, PCSK9 inhibitors, and ezetimibe, but the incremental effects on CV outcomes are uncertain.
L676: The goal of lipid-lowering therapy is to reduce the risk for cardiovascular disease. For LDL-C lowering therapies, in the absence of cardiovascular outcomes data, contemporary decisions to approve novel LDL-lowering therapies are influenced by the direction and magnitude of drug-induced changes in LDL-C, the mechanism of action of the novel agent, as well as the effects of the drug on other lipid parameters, markers of cardiometabolic risk and any evidence for off-target toxicity. In the case of the monoclonal antibody PCSK9 inhibitors, evolocumab and alirocumab, approval was granted in 2015, prior to the completion of their cardiovascular outcomes trials (CVOTs). However, the initial indication was restricted to a higher CV risk population, namely as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) or clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (CVD), who require additional lowering of LDL-C. The indication was broadened after the successful completion of their CVOTs, which incorporated
L677: 17 Cannon CP, Braunwald E, McCabe CH, Rader DJ, Rouleau JL, Belder R et al., for the Pravastatin or Atorvastatin Evaluation and Infection Therapy–Thrombolysis in Myocardial Infarction 22 Investigators*. Intensive versus Moderate Lipid Lowering with Statins after Acute Coronary Syndromes. N Engl J Med 2004; 350(15): 1495-504.
L678: 18 LaRosa JC, Grundy SM, Waters DD, Shear C, Barter P, Fruchart JC et al; Treating to New Targets (TNT) Investigators. Intensive lipid lowering with atorvastatin in patients with stable coronary disease. N Engl J Med 2005; 352:1425-35.
L679: 19 Pedersen TR, Faergeman O, Kastelein JJ, Olsson AG, Tikkanen MJ, Holme I et al., on behalf of the Incremental Decrease in End Points through Aggressive Lipid Lowering Study Group. High-dose atorvastatin vs usual-dose simvastatin for secondary prevention after myocardial infarction. The IDEAL study: a randomized controlled trial. JAMA 2005; 294:2437-45. Erratum in: JAMA. 2005 Dec 28;294(24):3092.
L680: 20 Cannon CP, Steinberg BA, Murphy SA, Mega JL, Braunwald E. Meta-analysis of cardiovascular outcomes trials comparing intensive versus moderate statin therapy. J Am Coll Cardiol. 2006;48(3):438-45.
L681: 21 Schwartz GG, Steg PG, Szarek M, et al. Alirocumab and Cardiovascular Outcomes after Acute Coronary Syndrome. N Engl J Med 2018; 379: 2097-107.

L683: 23 Cannon, CP, Blazing, MA, Giugliano, RP, et al. Ezetimibe added to statin therapy after acute coronary syndromes. N Engl J Med. 2015;372:2387-2397.
L684: important safety assessments, demonstrating CV benefit associated with LDL-C reduction, and reassuring long-term safety data.
L685: ORION-4 (Study CTSU-MDCO-PCS-17-01), titled "A Double-blind, Randomized, Placebo-controlled Trial Assessing the Effects of Inclisiran on Clinical Outcomes Among People with Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease", an ongoing cardiovascular outcome trial with inclisiran, is being conducted in the hyperlipidemic (non-HoFH) population but results will not be known for several years. The phase 3 HPS-4/TIMI 65/ORION-4 trial (NCT03705234) will be conducted at approximately 180 clinical sites in the UK and the US. Approximately 15,000 participants aged 55 years or older with atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease will be randomized between inclisiran sodium 300 mg and matching placebo (given by subcutaneous injection on the day of randomization, at 3 months and then every 6-months) in a 1:1 ratio for a planned median duration of about 5 years. The US centers are planned to enroll approximately 3,000 of the 15,000 subjects in this trial. All participants will be followed until death, withdrawal of consent for follow-up, or the scheduled end of the study (even if they have a study outcome or discontinue study treatment for any reason). The estimated primary completion date is December 2024,24 with results expected by 2025.
L686: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L687: Current treatment guidelines emphasize reducing the risk of atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD) through lipid management, based on an individualized approach which considers a patient's overall risk for a CV event. In primary prevention, this is based on an ASCVD risk score and additional risk factors.
L688: Moderate- or high-intensity statins are considered first-line therapy for all patients who require LDL-C reduction, depending on risk category. Low-intensity statins are not recommended for any population. Ezetimibe and/or PCSK9 inhibitors are considered as second-line, add-on therapies to a background of maximally tolerated statin therapy in patients at highest CV risk (secondary prevention and HeFH) who require additional LDL-C reduction.
L689: Statins reduce LDL-C by 30% to 60% depending on the statin intensity. PCSK9 inhibitors lower LDL-C by an additional 50% to 70%. Ezetimibe lowers LDL-C by an additional 15% to 20%. Other drug categories, such as fibrates or niacin, are not considered mainstays of LDL-C lowering therapy due to mild LDL-lowering ability and clinical trial data that shows no benefit in reducing
L690: 24 https://clinicaltrials.gov/ct2/show/NCT03705234
L691: CV morbidity or mortality among patients already treated with a statin.25,26
L692: The following drugs are currently approved for patients with primary hyperlipidemia or HeFH:
L693: - Statins (HMG-CoA reductase inhibitors) and statin fixed-dose combinations (rosuvastatin, atorvastatin, simvastatin, pitavastatin, lovastatin, fluvastatin, pravastatin, atorvastatin/ezetimibe, simvastatin/ezetimibe);
- Cholesterol absorption inhibitor (ezetimibe);
- Peroxisome proliferator receptor alpha (PPAR-\alpha) activators (fenofibric acid, fenofibrate, choline fenofibrate)
- Bile acid sequestrants (cholestyramine, colestipol, colesevelam);
- Niacin extended-release; and
- PCSK9 (Proprotein Convertase Subtilisin Kexin Type 9) inhibitors (alirocumab and evolocumab)
- Adenosine triphosphate-citrate lyase (ACL) inhibitor (bempedoic acid and bempedoic acid/ezetimibe)
L694: Statins are considered the standard-of-care for lowering LDL-C with the goal of reducing CV risk. The FDA-approved LDL-C-lowering therapies that are indicated to treat patients with hyperlipidemia when administered in addition to background statin therapy include colesevelam, ezetimibe, simvastatin/ezetimibe, atorvastatin/ezetimibe, evolocumab, alirocumab, bempedoic acid and bempedoic acid/ezetimibe (designated by bold font in table below).
L695: 25 AIM-HIGH Investigators, W.E. Boden, J.L. Probstfield, T. Anderson, et al. Niacin in patients with low HDL cholesterol levels receiving intensive statin therapy. N Engl J Med, 365 (2011), pp. 2255-2267
L696: 26 The FIELD study investigators. Effects of long-term fenofibrate therapy on cardiovascular events in 9795 people with type 2 diabetes mellitus (the FIELD study): randomised controlled trial. Lancet. 2005;366:1849-1861.

L719: ## 3. Regulatory Background
L720: ## 3.1. U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L721: Inclisiran is a new molecular entity (NME) and it is not currently marketed in the U.S.
L722: ## 3.2. Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity
L723: Excerpts of Pre-submission Regulatory History:
L724: ## December 17, 2015: Pre-IND Meeting:
L725: The clinical team provided comments regarding safety monitoring for future trials, such as: assess for any potential relatedness of new-onset diabetes to ALN-PCSSC use; evaluate for the incidence and severity of injection site reactions, hypersensitivity, and immunogenicity; subjects should be on maximally tolerated statin therapy; subjects who achieve two consecutive LDL-C values &lt; 25 mg/dL should be monitored more closely; neurological examinations should be incorporated into the protocol for all subjects; all subjects need to be followed until the drug is no longer significantly pharmacodynamically active; and include older subjects in the trials. (b) (4)
L726: ## October 7, 2016: FDA Request for Information regarding revusiran
L727: FDA request for information regarding the relevance of the findings from the Alnylam revusiran program. Alnylam Pharmaceuticals, Inc. had recently announced discontinuation of development of revusiran, another investigational RNA interference (RNAi) therapeutic that was being developed for the treatment of hereditary ATTR amyloidosis with cardiomyopathy (hATTR-CM). According to their press release, an imbalance of mortality and peripheral neuropathy were noted in the Phase 3 program. FDA requested updated safety data for ALN-PCSSc, including an assessment for neuropathy, lactic acidosis, myopathy, liver-related adverse events/chemistries as well as any potential safety signals identified to date. Response with the requested information was submitted on October 24, 2016 (S-0022).
L728: ## April 24, 2017: FDA EOPII Meeting minutes
L729: FDA provided written feedback following the April 6, 2017 EOPII meeting. The meeting topics centered on alignment of the key aspects of the proposed Phase III program. Some clinical recommendations included:
L730: - For the populations the applicant has identified, the majority of subjects should be on (b) (4)
L731: high-intensity statin therapy, such as atorvastatin 40 or 80 mg or rosuvastatin 20 or 40 mg.
L732: - Eligibility criteria should be as inclusive as possible. Recommend including subjects with history of hemorrhagic stroke; liberalize AST/ALT cut-offs; include patients with stable heart failure, especially those with ischemic cardiomyopathy; if the data from the renal impairment study (ORION-7) support it, include patients with eGFR &lt;30 mL/min/1.73\text{m}^2 into all Phase III trials.
- The eligibility criteria included a history of ASCVD or ASCVD-risk equivalents. We advised the applicant that with our current regulatory approach to LDL-C-lowering drugs, the (b) (4) indication for inclisiran would likely be similar to that granted for evolocumab and alirocumab (i.e., as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with HeFH or clinical ASCVD, who require additional lowering of LDL-C) unless there are positive results from a CVOT. (b) (4)
- Assessments and pre-specified definitions for new-onset diabetes or worsening of pre-existing diabetes mellitus should be detailed in the protocols.
- The neurologic exams should be comprehensive and include peripheral sensory and motor evaluation, and an assessment of gait, pain, position, strength and reflexes.
- Size of the safety database at the time of NDA submission: At least 1500 patients should be exposed to inclisiran for approximately 18 months, in order to include the safety data from at least 4 injections. The vast majority of these subjects should be exposed to the to-be-marketed doses and formulation of inclisiran in double-blind, controlled trials. The long-term experience in controlled trials needs to include a heterogeneous population with respect to demographics, high- and moderately-high risk for CVD, concomitant high- and moderate-high intensity statin therapy, diabetes, established cardiovascular disease, and CHF or ischemic cardiomyopathy to ensure sufficient representation from the types of patients expected to use inclisiran if approved.Of note, the applicant has submitted in the Controlled Phase III Studies pool, 1822 subjects treated with placebo and 1833 with inclisiran, with 1666 receiving all 4 doses of inclisiran. This meets and exceeds what the FDA requested at the EOP II meeting.
- Detailed recommendations were given regarding the (b) (4) ORION-4 CVOT, which are not relevant for this submission.
L733: October 26, 2017: FDA feedback on Phase III protocols
L734: FDA provided feedback on the draft Phase III protocols, SAP and dose regimen rationale. Response and revised protocols addressing FDA feedback submitted on November 21, 2017 (S-0086) and November 30, 2017 (S-0087). Some of the issues raised include:
L735: - In ORION-9 and ORION-10, in addition to being on a maximally tolerated dose of statin, the majority of subjects with HeFH should also be on chronically administered ezetimibe.
- Subjects with New York Heart Association (NYHA) class III heart failure are excluded from the trial; recommend including these patients in the phase 3 trials and CVOT, since patients with NYHA class III ischemic cardiomyopathy would likely be treated with LDL-C-lowering therapy.
- In ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11, one of the secondary endpoints is “mean maximum reduction in LDL-C levels”. [REDACTED] (b) (4) providing an average of maximum reductions would not accurately reflect the effect of the drug throughout the dosing interval.
- The percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 is the primary endpoint in this study. The time adjusted % change in LDL-C from baseline between Day 90 and Day 540 will be considered a secondary endpoint.
- For serious adverse events and adverse events of special interest, FDA recommends prospectively planned analyses that compare treatment groups with respect to risk, e.g., with a rate ratio, risk difference, hazard ratio, or relative risk, along with a confidence interval for the chosen metric to help quantify the uncertainty in the treatment comparison (no hypothesis testing is necessary).
L736: ## December 8, 2017: FDA feedback on ORION-10 final protocol
L737: FDA recommended that if patients discontinue study medication to initiate a protocol-prohibited lipid-lowering-therapy (e.g., an approved PCSK9 inhibitor), collect the reason that the new therapy was initiated. Response and feedback incorporated into the ORION-10 protocol amendment #1 submitted on January 16, 2018 (S- 0100)
L738: ## April 19, 2019: FDA feedback on format and content of the NDA
L739: FDA recommendations included:
L740: - In the ISS, Adverse Event (AE) tables should provide analyses by multiple levels of the MedDRA hierarchy, i.e., System Organ Class (SOC), High Level Group Term (HLGT), High Level Term (HLT), and preferred term (PT).
- Include data tables of adverse events of special interest (AESIs), including injection site reactions, hypersensitivity reactions, immune-mediated reactions, new onset diabetes mellitus, and cancer.
- ISRs: In the safety tables, provide a row that includes the number and percentage of subjects that experienced an injection site reaction (ISR) using the MedDRA High Level Term: 'Injection site reaction'; also breakdown the HLT by the relevant PTs so that no potential ISRs are missed.
L741: - Provided feedback on what should be included in the narrative summaries for deaths and adverse events leading to drug or study discontinuation.
- Provide the minutes of all DSMB and steering committee meetings. Include a chronology of prior substantive communications with FDA and copies of official meeting/telecom minutes.
