L745: ## 4. Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
L746: ## 4.1. Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)
L747: According to Dr. Cynthia Kleppinger's Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI) clinical inspection summary (in DARRTS dated 9/24/2020; Reference ID: 4675108), the inspection for this NDA consisted of two domestic clinical investigator sites and one foreign clinical investigator site in addition to the NDA sponsor (Novartis Pharmaceuticals Corporation in East Hanover, NJ). As Dr. Kleppinger notes in her review, the COVID-19 pandemic significantly limited the Office of Regulatory Affairs' ability to conduct onsite good clinical practice (GCP) inspections; planned foreign onsite inspections could not be completed. At the inspection of Novartis Pharmaceuticals, the FDA inspector observed that there were 4 subjects in ORION-9, 23 subjects in ORION-10, and 34 subjects in ORION-11 that did not have an in-person EOS visit, and are presumed to have not been given the terminal EOS ECG. This finding is not expected to change the overall results of this application as ECG findings were not an issue with inclisiran and the number of subjects potentially missing the EOS ECG across the three trials, involving a total of 3660 subjects, is small. In conclusion, based on the inspections of the two domestic clinical sites, the sponsor, and the remote regulatory assessment of one foreign site, the inspectional findings support the validity of data as reported by the sponsor under this NDA.
L748: Reviewer Note: One investigator (Dr Captain; Site [REDACTED](b) (6)) was terminated from the study due to protocol and GCP non-compliance. This site randomized [REDACTED](b) (6) in ORION-9; the results for [REDACTED](b) (6) are not expected to impact the overall results.
L749: ## 4.2. Product Quality
L750: The drug substance, inclisiran sodium, is a synthetic, chemically modified double-stranded small interfering ribonucleic acid (siRNA) oligonucleotide, covalently connected to a ligand containing three N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residues (to facilitate uptake by hepatocytes). The sense strand contains 21 nucleotides and the antisense strand contains 23 nucleotides; it was designed to match the human messenger ribonucleic acid transcripts for proprotein convertase subtilisin kexin type 9 (PCSK9). The manufacturer of the drug substance is [REDACTED](b) (4). FDA concluded that the drug substance information is adequate to control the identity, purity, strength, and quality of the drug substance used for manufacturing the drug product. The proposed drug substance specifications are consistent with batches used in clinical, nonclinical, and registration stability studies (for additional details, refer to Dr. Jensen's CMC review dated 7/16/2020 in Panorama).
L751: The inclisiran injection drug product is a single dose sterile, preservative-free, clear, and colorless to pale yellow solution in a prefilled syringe (PFS). Each PFS unit contains 284 mg inclisiran (equivalent to 300 mg inclisiran sodium salt) in 1.5 mL water for injection. It may include sodium hydroxide and/or phosphoric acid for pH adjustment to target 7. FDA concluded that the proposed specification is adequate to support the quality of the proposed product (for additional details, refer to Dr. Carver's drug product review dated 9/4/2020 in Panorama).
L752: The composition of inclisiran injection used throughout all clinical development was the same and the applicant proposes the same for marketing. Initially, a 2 mL vial (batches manufactured at [REDACTED](b) (4)) was filled to deliver either 100 mg (0.5 mL) of inclisiran sodium or 300 mg (1.5 mL) of inclisiran sodium. Subsequently, a prefilled syringe (batches manufactured at [REDACTED](b) (4)) delivering the identical 300 mg of inclisiran sodium (1.5 mL) was developed as the commercial presentation and was used in the second half (doses 3 and 4) of one of the Phase III clinical trials (ORION-10). The drug product used in the phase 3 clinical trials is the same as the 'to be marketed' product.
L753: The applicant proposes a prefilled syringe containing 300 mg inclisiran sodium solution, transported and stored at room temperature, and administered by a healthcare professional, as the to-be-marketed presentation.
L754: The OPO recommends a complete response (CR) action due to Office of Pharmaceutical Manufacturing Assessment (OPMA) recommending a 'withhold approval' because of deficiencies related to the [REDACTED](b) (4) drug product manufacturing site in [REDACTED](b) (4) (refer to CDER Clinical Review Template
L755: Version date: September 6, 2017 for all NDAs and BLAs
L756: review by Dr. Ramaswamy, in DARRTS dated 10/1/20; Reference ID: 4679598 and Dr. Janoria's facility review in Panorama dated 9/30/2020). This CR action will allow for [REDACTED] (b) (4) and Novartis to complete studies and provide updated accurate information to the NDA for assessment.
L757: Dr Ramaswamy concludes that there currently are no outstanding deficiencies related to the drug substance, drug product, process, microbiology, environmental analysis, and container/carton label sections of the NDA. Due to outstanding manufacturing facility related deficiencies, OPQ overall recommendation for NDA 214012 is a complete response action.
L758: ## 4.3. Clinical Microbiology
L759: The applicant states that Inclisiran Injection is a preservative-free sterile solution presented in a 2.25 mL prefilled syringe for single use. Inclisiran Injection is tested for endotoxin and container closure integrity on stability throughout the shelf-life, which assures the microbiological quality of the drug product.
L760: ## 4.4. Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L761: Inclisiran was administered via SC injection in all toxicology studies, consistent with the clinical route of administration. The pivotal toxicology studies involved once monthly dosing which is more frequent than the phase 3 clinical trials where dosing was on Day 1, Day 90, and then every 6 months. The nonclinical toxicology repeat dose studies were 4 weeks, 15 weeks and 29 weeks duration in rats and 4 weeks, 15 weeks and 40 weeks duration in monkeys. As described in the applicant's submission, these studies included analysis of inclisiran in plasma and in tissues including the liver which is the target organ of effect, the kidney which is the main organ of elimination of inclisiran from plasma, and the heart, a non-target tissue. The pharmacology/toxicology team (refer to review by Dr. Braithwaite, in DARRTS dated 9/30/20; Reference ID: 4678631) confirms that inclisiran was well-tolerated in repeat dose studies up to 29-weeks of duration in rats and 40-weeks of duration in monkeys.
L762: Because inclisiran will be co-administered with statins in the real world setting and in clinical trials, a study was conducted in monkeys to determine the potential toxicity of administering inclisiran in combination with atorvastatin. When both atorvastatin and inclisiran were administered together, further decreases (compared to monotherapy of either agent) in cholesterol, LDL and triglycerides were seen, but were less than additive. Maximal reduction of PCSK9 was seen in monkeys treated with inclisiran alone. This may be due to the finding that statins cause an increase in PCSK9 levels when compared to pretreatment values and likely accounts for the reduced degree of PCSK9 lowering compared to inclisiran treatment alone. The FDA pharmacology/toxicology team confirms that no new or exacerbated toxicity were observed when inclisiran was administered in combination with atorvastatin for 85 days. The NOAEL in the pivotal rat and monkey studies were 250 mg/kg/28 days (8- fold the 300 mg
L763: recommended clinical dose based on BSA comparison/dose) and 300 mg/kg/28 days (19-fold the 300 mg recommended clinical dose based on BSA comparison/dose), respectively.
L764: The development of anti-drug antibodies (ADA) was evaluated in repeat dose studies in rats and monkeys. Inclisiran did not stimulate proinflammatory cytokines in mice or monkeys, or activate complement in monkeys. There were some incidences of the development of ADA, but they were generally low titer responses in a small number of animals, were not dose related, and did not impact PCSK9 or LDL-C parameters. Dr. Braithwaite notes that delayed or absent IgM immune response to KLH antigen were observed in monkeys at doses \ge 100 mg/kg/28 days (16-fold the 300 mg recommended clinical dose based on BSA comparison/dose). Reduced IgG response to KLH antigen was observed several days after immunization at the 300 mg/kg/28-day dose (19-fold the 300 mg recommended clinical dose based on BSA comparison/dose). However, these findings were reversible.
L765: Development and reproductive toxicity studies were conducted in rats and rabbits. Inclisiran did not show positive signals for selective reproductive toxicity in fertility and early embryonic development studies conducted in rats, embryo-fetal development studies conducted in rats and rabbits, and pre- and postnatal development study conducted in rats. Dr. Braithwaite confirms that inclisiran did not have an adverse impact on reproduction or development in rats and rabbits.
L766: Genotoxicity studies were conducted including a bacterial mutagenicity assay, an in vitro chromosome aberration assay using human peripheral lymphocytes, and an in vivo bone marrow micronucleus assay in rats. Dr. Braithwaite confirms that inclisiran tested negatively for genotoxicity in a standard battery of OECD-compliant tests and did not promote neoplastic transformations in TgRasH2 mice or Sprague Dawley rats.
L767: The applicant's off-target analysis involved a bioinformatics search against the human transcriptome and was used to identify a set of transcripts based on partial sequence homology to PCSK9 that would potentially be inhibited by inclisiran. Twenty genes were identified whose expression may be impacted by inclisiran. To measure off-target inhibition, the response of endogenously expressed transcripts to inclisiran was determined by quantitative polymerase chain reaction in a dose response study in Hep3B cells. The results showed a \ge 45-fold difference between the "on-target" inhibition of PCSK9 and the inhibition of any of the predicted off-target transcripts, supporting the specificity of inclisiran for PCSK9.
L768: The pharmacology/toxicology team recommends approval of inclisiran for the indicated population.
L769: ## 4.5. Clinical Pharmacology
L770: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology/Division of Cardiometabolic and Endocrine Pharmacology (OCP/DCEP) has reviewed the clinical pharmacology data submitted under NDA 214012 and recommends approval (refer to the Clinical Pharmacology review by Sang Chung, Ph.D., Justin Earp, Ph.D., and Jayabharathi Vaidyanathan, Ph.D., in DARRTS dated 10/13/2020; Reference ID: 4684702). Important recommendations and comments from their review are summarized below:
L771: ## Dosing in patient subgroups
L772: There was an increase in inclisiran exposure in patients with severe renal impairment (2.5-fold) or moderate hepatic impairment (2.1-fold). However, the pharmacodynamic markers including PCSK9 and LDL-C changes in these patients were comparable to those with normal renal or hepatic function.
L773: - Renal impairment: No dose adjustment is necessary in patients with mild, moderate or severe renal impairment. Inclisiran was not studied in patients with end stage renal disease.
- Hepatic impairment: No dose adjustment is needed in patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment. Inclisiran was not studied in patients with severe hepatic impairment.
- Other demographics: No dose adjustments are needed for differences in age, body weight, sex, or race.
L774: The to-be-marketed drug product (formulation and device) was used in the pivotal and supportive Phase 3 trials.
L775: ## Mechanism of action
L776: Inclisiran is a chemically modified double-stranded 21-23mer small interfering RNA, conjugated on the sense strand with triantennary GalNAc to facilitate uptake by hepatocytes. In hepatocytes, the antisense strand is incorporated in the RNA-induced silencing complex (RISC) and directs catalytic breakdown of mRNA for PCSK9, thereby inhibiting translation of PCSK9 protein. The reduction of intrahepatic PCSK9 levels increases LDL-C receptor recycling and expression on the hepatocyte cell surface, leading to an increase in LDL-C uptake and lowering LDL-C levels in the systemic circulation.
L777: ## Pharmacokinetics
L778: ## Absorption
L779: At the proposed inclisiran dose of 284 mg, the T_{max} was approximately 4 hours post dose with a mean C_{max} of 509 ng/mL. Concentrations reached undetectable levels after 24 to 48 hours post dosing.
L780: ## Distribution
L781: Inclisiran is 87% bound to human plasma proteins at clinically relevant plasma concentrations
L782: (0.5 µg/mL, mean C_{max} following 300 mg SC injection). As shown in nonclinical studies, inclisiran distributes primarily into the liver, which is the target organ of effect, followed by the kidney. In animals, elimination of radioactivity from liver was slow, and the half-life for all tissues were greater than 190 hours.
L783: ## Metabolism
L784: Inclisiran is extensively metabolized by non-specific nucleases to shorter nucleotides of varying length.
L785: ## Elimination
L786: The clin-pharm team states that the apparent terminal half-life is approximately 7 hours (range 5-7 h) following 300 mg dose. Approximately 17% (range 9% - 31%) of dose is eliminated as unchanged inclisiran in urine. Renal clearance of inclisiran is reduced with decreasing renal function.
L787: ## Drug-drug interactions
L788: Inclisiran is not a substrate for and is not an inducer or inhibitor of CYP450 enzymes or common drug transporters. Thus, inclisiran exposure is unlikely to be influenced by other drugs. In addition, inclisiran does not influence drug metabolizing enzymes or transporters, and is unlikely to be the source of drug interactions.
L789: - Inclisiran is not an in vitro inhibitor or inducer of cytochrome P450 (CYP450) enzymes. The applicant tested inclisiran's ability to inhibit CYP450 enzymes using human liver microsomes. Inclisiran reportedly did not exhibit direct or time dependent inhibition towards CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4/5, with half maximal inhibitory concentrations. No induction of CYP450 enzymes was observed at inclisiran concentrations up to 37-fold above the clinical 300 mg dose C_{max}.
- Inclisiran is not an in vitro substrate or inhibitor of common drug transporters including organic anion transporters (OAT1, OAT3), organic cation transporters (OCT1, OCT2, OCT3), organic anion transporting polypeptides (OATP1B1, OATP1B3), multi-drug and toxin extrusion proteins (MATE1, MATE2k), multi-drug resistance 1 (MDR1), bile salt export pump (BSEP) and the breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) transporter.
L790: Inclisiran PK was not significantly impacted by the co-administration of statin drugs in one small study. In study ALN-PCCS-001, inclisiran PK was compared between groups of subjects who were treated with inclisiran alone (N = 3 to 5), and inclisiran in combination with statins (N=6). Subjects on statins had similar inclisiran exposures as subjects not on statins, both on the first and last dose of inclisiran administration.
L791: Statin PK (atorvastatin and rosuvastatin) was not significantly impacted by the co-administration of inclisiran based on sparse statin PK data collected in ORION-10. The PK of statins in subjects in the inclisiran group was similar to the PK of statins in the placebo group.
L792: In conclusion, there is a low likelihood of clinically significant drug-drug interactions with inclisiran.
L793: ## Pharmacodynamics
L794: Inclisiran is cleared from plasma relatively quickly but has a prolonged PD effect on LDL-C and PCSK9 levels. As PK and PD profiles were not temporally linked, dose selection was based on PD findings and PD modeling, rather than PK. Refer to Section 7.1.4 for additional details.
L795: ## Hepatic Impairment
L796: ORION-6 was a Phase I, open-label study to evaluate the PK, PD, safety, and tolerability of a single dose of inclisiran administered SC in subjects with mild to moderate hepatic impairment (based on Child-Pugh assessment) compared with matched subjects with normal hepatic function. Subjects with Child-Pugh Class A (5 to 6 points) and Class B (7 to 9 points) were classified as mild and moderate hepatic impairment, respectively. Subjects with severe hepatic impairment were not studied.
L797: Twenty-eight subjects were enrolled in the study: 10 subjects with mild hepatic impairment, 6 subjects with moderate hepatic impairment, and 12 subjects with normal hepatic function.
L798: Pharmacokinetics: Inclisiran plasma exposures increased in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment. Inclisiran plasma concentrations reached maximum concentrations approximately 5 hours to 6 hours after dosing, followed by declining concentrations until undetectable levels were observed after 48 hours in the majority of subjects. The C_{max} geometric mean ratios (90% CI) between subjects with mild and moderate hepatic impairment compared to subjects with normal HF were 1.1 (0.8, 1.5) and 2.1 (1.5, 3.0), respectively. The AUC_{0-inf} geometric mean ratios (90% CI) were 1.3 (1.1, 1.7) and 2.0 (1.6, 2.6) for subjects with mild and moderate hepatic impairment, respectively.
L799: The mean baseline LDL-C levels were 133, 97, and 160 mg/dL in subjects with normal HF, mild and moderate hepatic impairment, respectively. All groups had decreases in LDL-C compared to baseline. The greatest decreases were observed in subjects with mild hepatic impairment and normal hepatic function. LDL-C percentage changes from baseline on Day 60 were 52%, 53%, and 40% in subjects with normal HF, mild and moderate hepatic impairment, respectively. On Day 180, LDL-C levels were reduced by 40%, 37%, and 24% for subjects with normal HF, mild and moderate hepatic impairment, respectively. The applicant proposes the following labeling:
L800: ## “8.7 Hepatic Impairment
L801: No dose adjustment is necessary in patients with mild to moderate hepatic impairment. INCLISIRAN has not been studied in patients with severe hepatic impairment [see *Clinical Pharmacology (12.3)*].”
L802: ## Renal Impairment
L803: ORION-7 was a Phase I, single-dose, open-label study to evaluate the safety, tolerability, PK, and PD of a single dose of 300 mg inclisiran sodium administered SC to subjects with renal impairment (mild, moderate, severe) compared with matched subjects with normal renal function. The effect of ESRD on inclisiran PK was not evaluated. The effect of hemodialysis on inclisiran was not investigated during inclisiran development. The applicant states that as inclisiran is eliminated renally, hemodialysis is anticipated to remove inclisiran from plasma.
L804: Thirty-one subjects were enrolled and received inclisiran: 8 subjects each with normal, mildly impaired, and moderately impaired renal function and 7 subjects with severely impaired renal function. Median CrCl was 103 mL/min in subjects with normal renal function and 69 mL/min, 33 mL/min, and 20 mL/min in subjects with mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment, respectively.
L805: Pharmacokinetics: Inclisiran plasma exposures increased with renal impairment. Inclisiran plasma concentrations reached maximum concentrations around 4 hours to 8 hours after dosing and were at undetectable levels between 48 and 96 hours. The C_{max} geometric mean ratios (90% confidence interval [CI]) between renal impairment groups and healthy subjects were 2.3 (1.5, 3.6), 2.0 (1.3, 3.1), and 3.3 (2.1, 5.2) for mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment, respectively. The AUC from time 0 to 48 hours geometric mean ratios (90% CI) were 1.6 (1.2, 2.1), 1.8 (1.4, 2.4), and 2.3 (1.8, 3.1) for mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment, respectively.
L806: The mean baseline LDL-C levels were 136, 139, 113, and 95 mg/dL in subjects with normal renal function and in subjects with mild, moderate, and severe renal function impairment, respectively. Maximum LDL-C percentage changes from baseline of 58%, 35%, 53%, and 51% were observed from Day 30 to Day 60 in the normal renal function group and the mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment groups, respectively. The applicant proposes the following labeling:
L807: ## “8.6 Renal Impairment
L808: No dose adjustments are necessary for patients with mild, moderate, severe renal impairment(b) (4) [see *Clinical Pharmacology (12.3)*].(b) (4)
L809: Reviewer comment:
L810: *The C_{max} geometric mean ratio (90% confidence interval [CI]) between healthy subjects and patients with severe renal impairment was 3.3 (2.1, 5.2). The AUC from time 0 to 48 hours geometric mean ratios (90% CI) was 2.3 (1.8, 3.1) for severe renal impairment.*
L811: *In the QT study, at the inclisiran mean C_{max} observed in this study (2888 ng/mL), the model-predicted \Delta\Delta QTcF was 1.9 msec (90% CI 0.6, 3.2 msec). At the maximum individual inclisiran concentration observed in this study (8430 ng/mL), the model-predicted \Delta\Delta QTcF was 5.7 msec (90% CI 1.1, 10.3 msec). At the mean C_{max} observed in ORION-7 at the therapeutic dose of 300 mg in severe renal impairment (1760 ng/mL), the model-predicted \Delta\Delta QTcF was 1.1 msec (90% CI 0.3, 1.9 msec). The applicant concludes that inclisiran had no clinically significant effect on QTcF at systemic exposures that significantly exceeded those likely to be obtained at therapeutic doses.*
L812: *The 900 mg dose in the QTc study is a similar exposure increase to that of severe renal impairment.*
L813: ## QT Study
L814: ORION-12 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo- and positive-controlled, 3-way crossover study in 48 healthy subjects. Each subject received a single SC injection of inclisiran sodium 900 mg (3-times the recommended dose), placebo, and a single oral dose of moxifloxacin 400 mg (active control) at various timepoints.
L815: The QT consultation review by Dr Begum (in DARRTS dated 5/13/20; Reference ID: 4607664) notes that the mean peak concentrations of 2890 \pm 1360 ng/mL (T_{max} ~ 4 h) were observed following a 900 mg inclisiran injection. The peak concentrations of inclisiran observed in the study were higher than those observed with therapeutic doses (300 mg) in healthy subjects (C_{max}: 509 ng/mL) and in subjects with severe renal impairment (C_{max}: 1760 ng/mL; Study ORION-7). None of the subjects experienced QTcF values greater than 480 msec in the inclisiran 900 mg group. Similarly, none of the subjects experienced \Delta QTcF values greater than 30 msec in the inclisiran 900 mg group. The applicant's by-time analysis, which the FDA agreed with, showed that inclisiran 900 mg excluded the 10 msec threshold at the supratherapeutic dose level for \Delta\Delta QTcF [C_{max} at 4 hours: FDA analysis: 2.9 msec (90% CI 0.5, 5.2 msec); applicant analysis: 2.5 msec (90% CI 0.6, 4.5 msec)].
L816: Thus, at a single dose of 900 mg, inclisiran had no clinically significant effects on QTcF or other ECG variable at systemic exposures that significantly exceed those likely to be achieved with a therapeutic dose.

Section 8 presents the safety review which focused on the pooled data (Safety Pool) from ORION -9, -10, and -11. The applicant's analysis was verified and supplemented with the clinical reviewer's analysis, where applicable.
L831: The clinical reviewers collaborated with reviewers from the Office of Biotechnology Products (OBP), Division of Biotechnology Review and Research IV (DBRRIV) on the review of the antibody assay validation and inclisiran immunogenicity (refer to the review by Dr. Takahashi in DARRTS dated 8/14/2020; Reference ID: 4656320).

L833: ## 6. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L834: ## 6.1. ORION-9 (MDCO-PCS-17-03)
L835: ## 6.1.1. Study Design
L836: ## Overview and Objective
L837: Title of Study: A placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized trial to evaluate the effect of 300 mg of inclisiran sodium given as subcutaneous injections in subjects with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) and elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C)
L838: The primary objective was to evaluate the effect of inclisiran treatment on LDL-C levels at Day 510. Other objectives included an assessment of the safety and tolerability profile of inclisiran.
L839: ## Trial Design
L840: ORION-9 was a Phase III, placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized trial in approximately 400 subjects with HeFH and elevated LDL-C, despite maximum tolerated dose of LDL-C lowering therapies. The purpose of the trial was to evaluate the efficacy, safety, and tolerability of 4 subcutaneous inclisiran injections over a period of 18 months. Screening occurred approximately 2 weeks prior to randomization. Subjects were randomized 1:1 to inclisiran sodium 300 mg or placebo. Treatment allocation was stratified by country and by current use of statins or other lipid-modifying therapies. Each subject would receive four subcutaneous injections of blinded inclisiran or placebo on Day 1, Day 90, Day 270, and Day 450. The end of study (EOS) visit would be conducted on Day 540. Subjects who withdrew from study drug treatment were asked to continue follow-up for safety and efficacy using the protocol-specified visit schedule and procedures.
L841: Pharmacodynamic assessments were scheduled at various visits and included LDL-C levels as well as other lipids and lipoproteins (e.g., total cholesterol, triglycerides, HDL-C, non-HDL-C, very low-density lipoprotein cholesterol [VLDL-C], apolipoprotein A1 [Apo-A1], apolipoprotein B [ApoB], lipoprotein(a) [Lp(a)], high sensitivity C-reactive protein [hsCRP], and PCSK9). All subjects were offered to have pharmacogenetic analyses done, unless underlying causal mutations of HeFH were well documented by a validated specialized laboratory.
L842: ## LDL-C Assessment for Efficacy
L843: Subjects were in a fasted state for all efficacy laboratory assessments of lipids/lipoproteins/biomarkers. LDL-C using ultracentrifugation was done additionally at baseline and at Day 510. Calculated LDL-C used the Friedewald calculation (LDL-C = TC - HDL-C - TG/5), which is typically used in clinical practice and is generally considered reliable within &lt;0.5 mg/dL of directly-measured LDL. The Friedewald equation loses accuracy in subjects with elevated triglycerides or low LDL-C values. Thus, the primary endpoints used a reflexive LDL-C approach: either calculated LDL-C (based on the Friedewald formula) was used, or if the calculated LDL-C was less than 40 mg/dL or triglycerides were greater than 400 mg/dL, or calculated LDL-C was missing, directly measured (using ultracentrifugation) LDL-C was used, if it was available (non-missing).
L844: Safety assessments including adverse events (AEs), serious adverse events (SAEs), electrocardiograms (ECGs), concomitant medications, and safety laboratory parameters were collected during the study. Formation of anti-drug antibodies (ADA) and further characterization of ADA were evaluated.
L845: The independent Data Monitoring Committee (IDMC) reviewed safety data after the first 40 subjects received the first SC injection of inclisiran or placebo and had completed 1 month follow-up. Thereafter the IDMC reviewed safety data every 3 months until the end of study (EOS) unless requested otherwise by the IDMC.
L846: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria:**
L847: - Male or female subjects \ge 18 years of age.
- History of HeFH with a diagnosis of HeFH by genetic testing; and/or a documented history of untreated LDL-C of &gt;190 mg/dL, and a family history of FH, elevated cholesterol or early heart disease that may indicate FH (Simon Broome Diagnostic criteria for HeFH27)
- Serum LDL-C \ge 100 mg/dL at screening
- Fasting triglyceride &lt;400 mg/dL at screening.
- Calculated glomerular filtration rate &gt;30 mL/min by estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) using standardized local clinical methodology.
- Subjects on statins should be receiving a maximally tolerated dose. Maximum tolerated dose is defined as the maximum dose of statin that can be taken on a regular basis without intolerable adverse events. Intolerance to any dose of any statin must be documented as historical AEs attributed to the statin in question in the source documentation and on the Medical History page of the electronic case report form (eCRF).
- Subjects not receiving statins must have documented evidence of intolerance to all doses of at least two different statins: one statin at the lowest available daily dose AND another statin at any dose. A subject may participate in the study with documentation of intolerance to only one statin if there is a documented history of rhabdomyolysis attributed to that statin.
- Subjects on lipid-lower therapies (such as a statin and/or ezetimibe) should be on a stable dose for \ge 30 days before screening with no planned medication or dose change during study participation.
L848: ## **Key Exclusion Criteria:**
L849: - Any uncontrolled or serious disease, or any medical or surgical condition, that may either interfere with participation in the clinical study, and/or put the subject at significant risk or might interfere with interpretation of the clinical study results.
- New York Heart Association (NYHA) class IV heart failure or last known left ventricular ejection fraction &lt;25\%.
- Cardiac arrhythmia within 3 months prior to randomization that is not controlled by medication or via ablation.
- Major adverse cardiovascular event within 3 months prior to randomization.
- Uncontrolled severe hypertension: systolic blood pressure &gt;180 mmHg or diastolic blood pressure &gt;110 mmHg prior to randomization despite antihypertensive therapy.
L850: 27 Austin, MA, Hunter CM, Zimmern RL, Humphries SE. Genetic causes of monogenic heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia: A HuGE prevalence review. American Journal of Epidemiology. 2004;160:407-420.
L851: - Active liver disease defined as any known current infectious, neoplastic, or metabolic pathology of the liver or unexplained elevations in alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), &gt;3\times the upper limit of normal (ULN), or total bilirubin &gt;2\times ULN at screening confirmed by a repeat abnormal measurement at least 1 week apart.
- History of malignancy that required surgery (excluding local and wide-local excision), radiation therapy and/or systemic therapy during the three years prior to randomization.
- Females who are pregnant or nursing, or who are of childbearing potential and unwilling to use at least two methods of highly effective contraception for the entire duration of the study.
- Males who are unwilling to use an acceptable method of birth control during the entire study period (ie, condom with spermicide).
- Known history of alcohol and/or drug abuse within the last 5 years.
- Treatment with other investigational products or devices within 30 days or five half-lives of the screening visit, whichever is longer.
- Planned use of other investigational products or devices during the course of the study
- Treatment (within 90 days of screening) with monoclonal antibodies directed towards PCSK9.
L852: ## Dose Rationale
L853: Previous studies28 have shown that a 300 mg dose of inclisiran sodium is reasonably well tolerated and provides maximum efficacy (i.e., doses higher than 300 mg did not provide additional efficacy in LDL-C lowering). The applicant used modelling and simulation studies to further explore this dose and dosing regimen to confirm that it will provide sustained reductions in PCSK9 and LDL-C.
L854: **Test Product, Dose and Mode of Administration:** Inclisiran sodium 300 mg (inclisiran sodium includes 284 mg inclisiran and 16 mg sodium) will be administered as a single SC injection on Day 1, Day 90, Day 270, and Day 450.
L855: **Reference Therapy, Dose, and Mode of Administration:** Placebo will be administered as SC injections of saline solution. Placebo volume will be matched to test product volume within each dose and injection; the 300 mg dose will be administered as 1.5 mL of placebo.
L856: 28 ALN-PCSSC-001: Doses up to 800 mg as a single dose and two doses or more doses up to 500 mg were administered. ORION-1: Up to 500 mg as a single dose on Day 1 or two doses on Day 1 and Day 90 (100 mg, 200 mg or 300 mg) were administered.
L857: The study drug should be administered under aseptic conditions by clinical study site staff. The site of injection was the abdomen, alternating sides for each injection.
L858: ## Prohibited Concomitant Medications
L859: - Medications prescribed to lower LDL-C (e.g., statins, ezetimibe, lomitapide, mipomersen, niacin, colesevelam, bile acid absorption inhibitors, monoclonal antibodies directed towards PCSK9).
- Any medication taken for the purpose of lipid lowering, including over-the-counter or herbal therapies.
L860: ## Permitted Concomitant Medications
L861: - Hormone replacement therapy
- Lipid-lower medications; subjects already on a stable (\ge 30 days before screening) lipid-lower medications (such as statins and/or ezetimibe) should remain on the dose that they have received during participation in the original protocol unless clinically indicated
- Prescription medications prescribed to treat preexisting medical conditions such as diabetes and hypertension
- Prescription or nonprescription medications, when necessary to treat an AE, and at the discretion of the investigator
L862: Figure 1. Study Design of ORION-9
L863: Source: Applicant's Figure 1, ORION-9 Protocol Amendment #4
L864: ## Study Endpoints
L865: ## Primary Efficacy Endpoints:
L866: - Percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510
L867: - Time adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540. This is the average percentage change in LDL-C from baseline over the period after Day 90 and up to Day 540. (The applicant states that this analysis assesses the effect on LDL-C levels seen with a more chronic dosing regimen. The Day 90 dose is the start of the 6-monthly dosing regimen.)
L868: ## **Key Secondary Efficacy Endpoints:**
L869: - Absolute change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510
- Time adjusted absolute change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540
- Percentage change from baseline to Day 510 in PCSK9, total cholesterol, ApoB, and non-HDL-C
L870: **Safety Endpoints:** Adverse event, SAEs, vital signs, clinical laboratory values (hematology, coagulation testing, chemistry, and urinalysis), ADA testing and electrocardiograms (ECGs) will be collected at specified visits through the EOS visit (Day 540).
L871: **Blinded Study:** This was a double-blind, placebo-controlled study. Study medication was blinded prior to distribution to the site. Inclisiran may be visually distinguishable from placebo; therefore, blinded syringes were provided to all study sites and used to maintain the blind. Each investigational product vial, inclisiran or placebo, was covered in a yellow shroud to blind the vial. Randomization via automated interactive response technology (IRT) was used to assign subject to blinded investigational product kits.
L872: **Reviewer Comments:** *The design of this trial was similar to other recent trials for agents that are intended to be used as an add-on to statin in higher CV risk populations. One feature that was different was the 18-month duration of the phase III trials rather than the more typical 12 months. Because inclisiran is administered only 3 times in the first year and 2 times per year afterward, this 18-month duration allowed for subjects to be exposed to 4 injections of inclisiran, which is important to more completely assess the safety profile, particularly injection site reactions (ISRs) and other hypersensitivity reactions. The infrequent administration of inclisiran also impacted when the assessment for the primary endpoint for efficacy would be taken. For lipid-lowering agents that are administered more frequently and steady-state lipid lowering is reached within a few weeks, the primary endpoint for LDL-C reduction is usually done around Week 12. For inclisiran, the primary endpoint for percentage change in LDL-C from baseline was assessed at Day 510. While this gives one a good assessment of steady-state and durable LDL-C reduction, one would anticipate that there may be more missing data with an endpoint assessed 17 months into the trial rather than 3 months.*
L873: *The primary endpoint was percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510. Reduction of LDL-C is a surrogate endpoint for CV risk reduction and has been used as the basis for approval in previous trials of lipid-lowering drugs.*
L874: *Key secondary endpoints that are relevant to clinical practice included percent change in non-high-density lipoprotein cholesterol (non-HDL-C), total cholesterol (TC) and apolipoprotein B (Apo-B) from baseline to Day 510. The Hochberg procedure was applied to control the family-wise type I error rate at a 2-sided significance level of \alpha=0.05 for the key secondary endpoints.*
L875: *Other endpoints of interest to practicing clinicians were changes from baseline in triglycerides (TG), HDL, lipoprotein (a) [Lp(a)], and hsCRP. The applicant did not include these biomarkers as prespecified secondary endpoints; however, the review team evaluated them as exploratory endpoints.*
L876: *The applicant made reasonable efforts to blind study medication from subjects and investigators. However, if a patient wanted to try and unblind their treatment assignment and, for example, had an LDL-C assessment done at a health fair that showed a significant decrease (such as &gt; 40%) in LDL-C, they may have been able to guess their treatment assignment to inclisiran.*
L877: *The FDA clinical team encouraged the applicant to make the eligibility criteria as inclusive as possible, such as including older subjects (the applicant removed the upper age limit); including those with a history of hemorrhagic stroke or stable heart failure, especially those with ischemic cardiomyopathy; liberalizing AST/ALT cut-offs; and, if the data from the renal impairment study supported it, including patients with eGFR &lt;30 mL/min/1.73m2 into all Phase III trials. The applicant did agree to broaden many of the eligibility criteria. The subjects in this trial should thus represent patients that will be treated in real world conditions.*
L878: *This trial was placebo-controlled, which was justified as subjects were receiving a background of maximum tolerated statin therapy (ideally high-intensity statin) and/or other lipid-lowering therapies, such as ezetimibe. At the time these trials were conducted, PCSK9 monoclonal antibody inhibitors (i.e., alirocumab, evolocumab) were not widely available to patients.*
L879: *The FDA clinical team made extensive recommendations regarding the baseline lipid-lowering regimen that patients should be on, specific safety assessments, the size of the safety database, the duration of the trials, data collected if patients discontinue study medication, what should be included in the safety analyses, etc. The applicant agreed to the majority of FDA requests in a timely, thoughtful and thorough manner.*
L880: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L881: **Sample Size and Power:** The sample size calculation was performed with the assumption (which was based on the observed results from a Phase II study) that the difference in change from
L882: baseline between the active dose group and the placebo group for LDL-C would be no less than 30 mg/dL, with a standard deviation of 20 mg/dL. A sample size of at least 380 evaluable subjects would provide more than 90% power to detect a 30% reduction of LDL-C levels in the inclisiran group compared to the placebo group at one-sided significance level of 0.025.
L883: **Primary Endpoint Analysis:** The family-wise type I error rate was controlled at one-sided significance level of \alpha=0.025 by using a nested testing procedure. The percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 was tested first. If the null hypothesis were rejected at one-sided significance level of \alpha=0.025 and superiority of inclisiran over placebo were claimed, then the time adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540 would be tested, also at one-sided significance level of \alpha=0.025. Mixed-effect models for repeated measures (MMRM) would be performed on the percent change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 to test the superiority of inclisiran over placebo. The model included fixed effect for treatment, visits, baseline value, interaction between treatment and visits, and current use of statins or other lipid-modifying therapies.
L884: Time adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540 were calculated from the MMRM with imputed data. Linear combinations of the estimated means after Day 90 and up to Day 540 were used to compare treatments. Results were combined using Rubin's method.
L885: **Key Secondary Endpoint Analysis:** The secondary efficacy endpoints would not be tested if either one of the co-primary efficacy endpoints' null hypothesis failed to be rejected. The Hochberg procedure was applied to control the family-wise type I error rate at one-sided significance level of \alpha=0.025 for the key secondary endpoints. The key secondary endpoints were analyzed using the same methods as in the primary efficacy analysis.
L886: **Missing Data:** The applicant attested that they would diligently follow up with each subject during the study through Day 540 \pm 14 days on all efficacy laboratory assessments of lipids/lipoproteins/biomarkers to keep missing data to a minimum regardless of whether the subject remained on study treatment, used ancillary therapies, experienced an adverse event, or adhered to the protocol. However, if missing data, defined as data not available from either scheduled (within the protocol defined visit window) or unscheduled visits, occurred for the primary or any of key secondary efficacy endpoints then that data would be imputed.
L887: Primary and key secondary efficacy endpoints had missing data imputed using multiple imputation techniques (a primary method and sensitivity/exploratory methods).
L888: A multiple imputation washout model was utilized for the primary efficacy analysis of the percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 endpoint. The washout model, a modified control-based Pattern-Mixture Model (PMM), was used to explore the possibility of data
L889: missing not at random (MNAR) for subjects who discontinued the study early. For subjects who discontinued the study early, their missing values were imputed under the assumption that their outcome would be similar to those in the placebo group with similar background characteristics. For subjects in the inclisiran group only missing Day 510 values were imputed. For subjects in the placebo group their missing values over all visits after early termination were imputed based on the missing at random (MAR) assumption. Multiple imputation was used to account for uncertainty in the imputation process and results from the imputed datasets were combined using Rubin's method.
L890: One sensitivity technique included using control-based pattern mixture models which utilize placebo data for monotone missing inclisiran sodium 300 mg treatment data. Additional sensitivity data imputation techniques were utilized. Retrieved data were analyzed in all analyses where possible.
L891: Interim Analysis: No interim analysis were performed in this study.
L892: ## Analysis Populations
L893: - **Intent-to-Treat (ITT) Population**
The intent-to-treat (ITT) population comprises all subjects randomized into the study. Treatment classification was based on the randomized treatment. The ITT population was used for analysis of the primary and secondary endpoints.
- **Full Analysis Set (FAS)**
The FAS comprises all subjects who are randomized into the study, take any study medication and have at least one post treatment lipid data measured. Treatment classification was based on the randomized treatment.
- **Modified Intent-to-Treat (mITT) Population**
The mITT population comprises all randomized subjects who receive at least one dose of investigational product and have both the baseline and the 510-day follow-up LDL-C assessment. Treatment classification was based on the randomized treatment.
- **Safety Population**
All subjects who received at least one dose of investigational product. Treatment classification was based on the actual treatment received. This is the primary population for the safety analyses.

On 9/13/2019, final SAPs for ORION-9 and ORION-10 were submitted. On 9/27/2019, addendums to the SAPs for

L903: Hematology blood draws include:
L904: - Hemoglobin, hematocrit, erythrocytes, reticulocytes, mean cell hemoglobin (MCH), mean corpuscular volume (MCV), mean corpuscular hemoglobin concentration (MCHC), platelet count, white blood cell count with differential.
L905: Coagulation blood draws include:

L907: Chemistry blood draws vary based on visit day as follows:
L908: - Full serum chemistry - Baseline (Day 1) and EOS (Day 540): AST, ALT, alkaline phosphatase (ALP), gamma glutamyl transferase (GGT), total bilirubin (TBIL), direct and indirect bilirubin, creatine phosphokinase (CPK), lactate, bicarbonate, uric acid, creatinine, urea (BUN), estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR), sodium, potassium, calcium, inorganic phosphate, chloride, albumin, total protein, glucose (fasting), glycated hemoglobin A1C (HbA1C), and tryptase (as required).
- Limited serum chemistry - Screening, Days 90, 150, 270, 330, 450 and 510: ONLY: AST, ALT, ALP, GGT, TBIL, CPK, creatinine, eGFR, fasting glucose, HbA1C (not at Day 150, 330 and 510) and tryptase (as required).
L909: Fasted lipid profile/biomarkers include:
L910: - Total cholesterol (TC), triglycerides, LDL-C, HDL-C, non-HDL-C, VLDL-C, Apo-A1, ApoB, lipoprotein (a) [Lp(a)], hsCRP, and PCSK9.
L911: ## Protocol Amendments
L912: Protocol Amendment #1 (dated 21 Nov 2017): Changes were made to address comments received from an FDA review of the study. These clarifications were related to exclusion criterion #3, site training requirements and statistics: All sites were conducted under the US IND; FDA requested to allow NYHA class III heart failure subjects into the trial; participating study sites would be trained on the importance of completing study follow-up procedures and collecting the documentation required for missing data values; appendix detailing full neurological examination was added; clarification of HbA1C analyses; and sensitivity analysis would be performed on the primary and key secondary efficacy endpoints to assess the impact of missing values.
L913: Protocol Amendment #2 (dated 08 Jan 2018): Changes were made to the Withdrawal criteria: i.e. collect data if patients withdraw because they have initiated protocol-prohibited lipid-lowering therapy (e.g., an approved PCSK9 inhibitor); the amendment also clarified standards for vital sign measurements based on FDA comments: an automated BP device was recommended for collection of BP and the result recorded to the nearest mmHg. The revision specified that a subject should be sitting at rest for at least 5 minutes prior to these assessments. One assessment for each vital sign (HR, BP) was required per applicable visit.
L914: Protocol Amendment #3 (dated 30 Aug 2018): Administrative changes and minor edits.
L915: Protocol Amendment #4 (dated 30 Jan 2019): Changes to include refinement in the description of time adjusted analyses of LDL-C, clarify the plan to test for ADA and to align the approach to missing data handling with Health Authority feedback.
L916: *Reviewer comment: The clinical team agreed with the applicant's protocol amendments and do not believe that these modifications had a negative impact on the integrity of the trial or our interpretation of the results*

L928: ## Patient Disposition
L929: The study was conducted in 47 centers in 8 countries: Canada (3 centers), Czech Republic (3 centers), Denmark (6 centers), Netherlands (5 centers), South Africa (9 centers), Spain (6 centers), Sweden (3 centers), and USA (12 centers).
L930: A total of 617 subjects were screened and 482 were randomized. In ORION-9, approximately 22% of screened subjects were screen failures. The most common inclusion/exclusion criteria not met was the triglycerides inclusion criterion (fasting triglyceride &lt;400 mg/dL at screening) in 15% (93/617) of subjects. The second most common inclusion/exclusion criteria not met was the statin intolerance inclusion criterion (subjects not receiving statins must have documented evidence of intolerance to all doses of at least two different statins...) in 2% (13/617) of subjects.
L931: The 482 randomized subjects were enrolled in 46 sites from 8 countries (no subjects were enrolled from 1 site in the USA). South Africa enrolled the most subjects (177, 37%), followed by Spain (84, 17%), US (65, 13%) and Denmark (49, 10%).
L932: 29 See [web address].
L933: As shown in the table below, at Day 510 approximately 98% of the ITT Population were still in the study and at Day 540 (EOS Visit) approximately 97% were still in the study. A total of 3.8% (9/240) of placebo-treated subjects discontinued the study. The most common reason for discontinuation from the study was withdrawal of consent (1.7%; 4/240). There was 1 discontinuation from the study due to death in both treatment groups. A total of 2.9% (7/242) of inclisiran-treated subjects discontinued the study. The most common reason for discontinuation from the study was other (2.1%; 5/242); other reasons included subject moved to a new house and screen failure (accidentally randomized).

L953: *Reviewer comment: The demographic and baseline characteristics were balanced between the two treatment groups, especially for sex, age, race and region. The study primarily enrolled white subjects so there is very limited representation of non-white and Hispanic or Latino patients. The study population included appropriate representations of female sex and relevant comorbidities such as renal impairment, hypertension and, to a lesser degree, diabetes. There were small imbalances between treatment groups in some comorbid conditions such as ASCVD, diabetes and smoking status that should not impact the interpretation of efficacy.*
L954: Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)
L955: In ORION-9, 432 subjects (89.6%) consented to genetic testing. Fifty-nine percent (256 of 432) of these subjects, who consented to genetic testing, had single low-density lipoprotein receptor (LDLR) mutation. Of these 256 subjects, 168 (65.6%) had LDLR negative mutations, 28 (10.9%) subjects had LDLR defective mutations and 60 (23.4%) had LDLR mutations that were unclassified. Twenty-three of these 432 subjects (5.3%), who consented to genetic testing, had mutations in the APOB gene and one subject (0.2%) had a PCSK9 gain of function mutation. In 37 out of 432 subjects (8.6%), who consented to genetic testing, 2 or more mutations were identified. According to the applicant, the subjects with LDLR negative mutations had the highest baseline LDL-C (167 mg/dL). The table below presents this data for the entire ITT population.

L967: *Reviewer comment: The majority of participants were on background high-intensity statin therapy, which is appropriate, as inclisiran, if approved, would be most clinically relevant in patients with HeFH or at higher CV risk not adequately controlled by maximally tolerated statin therapy. The enrolled population adequately reflects the intended clinical population.*
L968: Approximately 25% (122/482; 26.7% placebo:24.0% inclisiran) of subjects reported either partial or complete intolerance to statins. The most common symptoms of statin intolerance (partial or complete) were muscle ache (75%; 91/122), other symptoms (36%; 44/122), and cramping (22%; 27/122). Approximately 12% (14/122) of these subjects had at least one documented abnormal lab (7% CK level, 3% hepatic [mainly ALT], 2% other lab).
L969: ## Treatment Compliance and Concomitant Medications
L970: ## Treatment Compliance
L971: All study drugs were administered subcutaneously by clinical study site staff, primarily in the abdominal region (doses on Day 1, Day 90, Day 270, and Day 450). Overall, 96.7% (232/240) of placebo-treated subjects and 95.4% (230/241) of inclisiran-treated subjects received all 4 planned SC doses. Approximately 97% of placebo-treated and inclisiran-treated subjects received 3 of the 4 planned SC doses.
L972: *Reviewer comment: Over 95% of study subjects received all 4 doses of study drug. This high level of treatment compliance will allow for a good assessment of steady-state and durable LDL-C reduction.*
L973: ## Changes in Lipid-Modifying Therapy (LMT) During Trial
L974: Six percent (14/240) of subjects in the placebo group and 3% (8/242) in the inclisiran group had changes in LMT use during the trial. One subject (0.2%) in the placebo group began therapy with a PCSK9 monoclonal antibody (evolocumab); no subject in the inclisiran group began therapy with a PCSK9 monoclonal antibody.
L975: Shifts from baseline in statin intensity were minimal in both treatment groups. Most subjects remained on their statin intensity category (high, moderate, low or none) throughout the trial.
L976: New or changed concomitant medications during the study were similar between the placebo and inclisiran treatment groups.
L977: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L978: The applicant's co-primary endpoints for this trial are:
L979: - Percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510.
L980: - Time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540. The applicant states that this provides the average LDL-C between the dosing intervals and reflects LDL-C effect at a steady state. The Day 90 dose is the start of the every 6-month dosing regimen.
L981: ## Percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510:
L982: The placebo-adjusted change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 using observed values was -49.5% (p&lt;0.0001). The primary analysis used a prespecified washout model to account for missing data.30 The placebo-adjusted change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 using the imputed values was -47.9% (p&lt;0.0001).

L988: ## Sensitivity Analyses
L989: Three additional, prespecified sensitivity analyses (PMM, MMRM, ANCOVA with country and current use of statins or other LMT as fixed effects) for handling missing values were used to
L990: 30 A multiple imputation washout model was used for missing data imputation with 100 total imputed datasets. Treatment effects from the 100 analyses were combined using Rubin's Method.

L995: * A control-based pattern mixture model (PMM) was used for missing data imputation with 100 total imputed datasets. A mixed-effects model for repeated measures (MMRM) on each of the 100 datasets was performed by including fixed effects for treatment, visit, interaction between treatment and visit, and baseline LDL-C as a covariate. A linear contrast at Day 510 was used to compare treatment groups. Treatment effects from the 100 analyses were combined using Rubin's Method.
L996: ** A MMRM analysis that assumes missing data are MAR was performed. The model included fixed effects for treatment, visit, interaction between treatment and visit, and baseline LDL-C as a covariate. The Restricted Maximum Likelihood (REML) estimation approach was used with the covariance structure set as Unstructured. A linear contrast at Day 510 was used to compare treatment groups.
L997: *** A multiple imputation washout model was used for missing data imputation with 100 total imputed datasets. This modified model assumed missing Day 510 MAR for Inclisiran subjects if they received all 4 doses and had data observed at Day 540. Analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) on each of the 100 datasets was performed by including fixed effects for treatment, current use of statins or other lipid-modifying therapies(y/n), country, interaction between treatment and country, and baseline LDL-C as a covariate. Treatment effects from the 100 analyses were combined using Rubin's Method.

L1016: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1017: The study was well executed and largely adhered to the protocol. The protocol amendments were reasonable and unlikely to have had a negative impact on the integrity of the trial or our interpretation of the results. Protocol deviations were similar between the treatment groups and were not believed to have a negative impact on study results. Subject retention was excellent (97%). The applicant made reasonable efforts to blind study medication from subjects and investigators. There were no notable financial conflicts of interest. Datasets were complete, generally straightforward to navigate, and of high quality. There were no potential issues concerning the submitted data quality or integrity that raise questions about the
L1018: purported efficacy results. Thus, the study has generated data that are robust, interpretable, and persuasive in supporting the proposed indication.
L1019: ## Efficacy Results – Key Secondary Endpoints

L1021: - Absolute change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510.The placebo-adjusted absolute change from baseline to Day 510 in LDL-C levels was -68.9 mg/dL (p&lt;0.0001).
- Time-adjusted absolute change in LDL-C from Baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540.Compared to placebo, the time-adjusted absolute change from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540 was -62.7 mg/dL (p&lt;0.0001).

- The placebo-adjusted percentage change in TC from baseline to Day 510 was -31.8% (p&lt;0.0001).
- The placebo-adjusted percentage change in Apo-B from baseline to Day 510 was -36.1% (p&lt;0.0001).
- The placebo-adjusted percentage change in non-HDL-C from baseline to Day 510 was -42.4% (p&lt;0.0001).

L1028: *Reviewer comment: The results of the key secondary efficacy endpoints are consistent and supportive of the LDL-C reduction that was shown in the primary efficacy endpoint. This is expected as LDL-C is a major component of TC and non-HDL-C, and apolipoprotein B is the primary apolipoprotein of LDL-C. In addition, a reduction in PCSK9 levels is the mechanism of action through which inclisiran lowers LDL-C.*
L1029: Other secondary endpoints of interest include:
L1030: - Individual responsiveness defined as the number of subjects reaching on-treatment LDL-C levels of &lt;25 mg/dL, &lt;50 mg/dL, &lt;70 mg/dL, and &lt;100 mg/dL at Day 510 (see table below).
- Proportion of subjects in each group with greater or equal to 50% LDL-C reduction from Baseline.At any time during the study, 3.8% (9/239) of placebo-treated subjects had ≥50% LDL-C reduction from baseline compared with 66.0% (159/241) of inclisiran-treated subjects.
- At Day 510, 0.9% (2/229) of placebo-treated subjects had ≥50% LDL-C reduction from baseline compared with 39.8% (92/231) of inclisiran-treated subjects.
- Proportion of subjects in each group who attain global lipid targets for their level of ASCVD risk.
L1031: - At any visit, 18.4% (44/239) of placebo-treated subjects achieved their corresponding LDL-C targets compared with 77.2% (186/241) of inclisiran-treated subjects.
- At Day 510, 1.4% (1/71) of placebo-treated subjects with ASCVD achieved their LDL-C target of &lt;70 mg/dL compared to 52.5% (31/59) of inclisiran-treated subjects with ASCVD

L1038: ## Exploratory Endpoints
L1039: Additional endpoints explored by this reviewer include percentage change from Baseline in triglycerides, HDL-C, and lipoprotein (a) [Lp(a)] (ITT population).
L1040: - The placebo-adjusted percentage change in TG from baseline to Day 510 was -12% (p&lt;0.003).
- The placebo-adjusted percentage change in HDL-C from baseline to Day 510 was +3% (p&lt;0.05).
L1041: - The placebo-adjusted percentage change in Lp(a) from baseline to Day 540 was -20% (p&lt;0.0001).
L1042: Inclisiran lowered TG by approximately 12% and Lp(a) by 20%. HDL-C increased slightly with inclisiran exposure. These changes are not considered adverse, but the clinical significance is unclear.

L1057: ## 6.2. ORION-10 (MDCO-PCS-17-04)
L1058: ## 6.2.1. Study Design
L1059: ## Overview and Objective
L1060: Title of Study: A placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized trial to evaluate the effect of 300 mg of inclisiran sodium given as subcutaneous injections in subjects with atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD) and elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C)
L1061: The primary objective was to evaluate the effect of inclisiran treatment on LDL-C levels at Day 510. Other objectives included an assessment of the safety and tolerability profile of inclisiran.
L1062: ## Trial Design
L1063: The three pivotal Phase III trials, ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11, evaluated the efficacy and safety of inclisiran sodium 300 mg in subjects with HeFH (ORION-9; 482 subjects), ASCVD (ORION-10; 1561 subjects) and ASCVD and ASCVD Risk Equivalent (ORION-11; 1617 subjects). The design of these 3 trials were nearly identical, the main difference was the study population enrolled into the trial. In this section, only relevant differences in ORION-10 as compared to ORION-9 will be described.
L1064: Key Inclusion Criteria that are unique to ORION-10 as compared to ORION-9:
L1065: - History of ASCVD (CHD, CVD or PAD)31
- Serum LDL-C \ge 70 mg/dL at screening
- No current or planned renal dialysis or renal transplantation.
L1066: 31 Coronary heart disease (CHD):
L1067: - Prior myocardial infarction
- Prior coronary revascularization (PCI or CABG)
- Angiographic or CT-imaging (eg, MDCT/CTA) evidence of coronary atherosclerosis (&gt;70\% stenosis in at least one major epicardial coronary artery)
L1068: ## Cerebrovascular disease (CVD):
L1069: - Prior ischemic stroke confirmed by a brain imaging study – CT or MRI; thought not to be caused by atrial fibrillation, valvular heart disease or mural thrombus
- Carotid artery stenosis &gt;70\% on prior angiography or ultrasound
- History of prior percutaneous or surgical carotid artery revascularization
L1070: ## Peripheral arterial disease (PAD):
L1071: - Prior documentation of a resting ankle-brachial index \le 0.85
- History of prior percutaneous or surgical revascularization of an iliac, femoral, or popliteal artery
- Prior non-traumatic amputation of a lower extremity due to peripheral artery disease
L1072: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L1073: **Sample Size and Power:** The sample size calculation was performed with the assumption (which was based on the observed results from a Phase II study) that the difference in change from baseline between the active dose group and the placebo group for LDL-C would be no less than 30 mg/dL, with a standard deviation of 20 mg/dL. Assuming about a 5% drop out rate, the sample size would be approximately 1425 subjects that were evaluable for efficacy across the placebo and inclisiran dose groups. This sample size of at least 1425 evaluable subjects, would provide more than 90% power to detect a 30% reduction of LDL-C levels in the inclisiran group compared to the placebo group at one-sided significance level of 0.025.
L1074: ## Protocol Amendments
L1075: ## Amendment 1, dated 16 Jan 2018:
L1076: - The criterion for renally impaired subjects was revised based on a recently completed clinical pharmacology study. Calculated glomerular filtration rate &gt;30 mL/min by eGFR was removed from inclusion criterion #5. Text was added to provide background of the new Phase I clinical data that supported expansion of use in subjects with renal impairment to include severe renal impairment.
- Initiation of protocol-prohibited lipid-lowering therapy (e.g., an approved PCSK9 inhibitor) was added as a possible reason that a subject may have discontinued their participation in the study.
- Clarified standards for vital sign measurements based on FDA comments. When available, an automated blood pressure device was recommended for collection of blood pressure and the result recorded to the nearest mmHg.
L1077: ## Amendment 2, dated 12 Jul 2018:
L1078: - Changes were made to the primary endpoint analysis as it related to how missing data were handled for the analyses. Missing data for the primary and key secondary endpoints were imputed according to the algorithm specified in the SAP.
- Changes were made to add the prefilled syringe as a drug product presentation. Blinding of the vials and syringes were described.
- Changes were made to clarify the ADA testing plan.
L1079: ## Amendment 3, dated 10 Sep 2018:
L1080: - Changes were made to the Procedures in Case of Emergency section of the protocol to replace [REDACTED] (b) (4) with The Medicines Company as the contact representative.
L1081: - Changes were made to remove the month, year and/or edition associated with the Investigator's Brochure as investigative sites should always refer to the latest edition.
L1082: Amendment 4, dated 31 Jan 2019:
L1083: - Changes were made to account for refinement in the description of time-adjusted analyses of LDL-C: "After Day 90 and up to Day 540" (versus between Day 90 and Day 540).
- Changes were made to clarify the plan to test for ADA. Serum samples for analysis were collected at every visit but assessment of ADA only occurred for samples collected at the following visits: Randomization (prior to injection), Day 30, Day 150, Day 330, and Day 510.
- Changes were made to Schedule of Assessments to align with Health Authority feedback for wider visit windows (\pm 30 days versus \pm 14 days) and to clarify that analysis windows were defined to maximize the amount of data that were included in the analysis models.
- Changes were made to account for the addition of a new analysis set: the FAS included all subjects who were randomized into the study, took any study drug, and had at least 1 post treatment lipid data measured.
- Changes were made regarding the handling of missing data per Health Authority feedback. Additional methods to impute and analyze data were utilized as sensitivity/exploratory analyses for the co-primary endpoints. An MMRM without any imputation (assuming data are MAR) was also performed.
- Changes were made to add analyses for clinically significant (CS) laboratory parameters: Analyses were performed for each laboratory parameter by treatment group for incidence rates of both potentially clinically significant (PCS)/CS values for subjects without PCS/CS value at baseline.
L1084: *Reviewer comment: The clinical team agreed with the applicant's protocol amendments and do not believe that these modifications had a negative impact on the integrity of the trial or our interpretation of the results.*
L1085: ## 6.2.2. Study Results
L1086: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L1087: The applicant attests that the study was conducted in compliance with the protocol, The Medicines Company's standard operating procedures and/or guidelines and any vendor partners where applicable, the US FDA regulations, the International Conference on Harmonisation (ICH) Good Clinical Practice (GCP) guidelines, the Declaration of Helsinki, and
L1088: other local regulations, in operation at the time of the study.
L1089: ## Financial Disclosure
L1090: Refer to Section 13.2 Financial Disclosure for additional information.
L1091: ## Patient Disposition
L1092: The study was conducted entirely in the United States at 146 sites (81 of these sites enrolled &gt;7 subjects); subjects were randomized at 145 sites. A total of 2329 subjects were screened and 1561 were randomized. In ORION-10, approximately 33% (768/2329) of screened subjects were screen failures. The 3 most common inclusion/exclusion criteria not met was the serum LDL-C \ge 1.8 mmol/L (70 mg/dL) inclusion criterion in 20% (453/2329), the “any condition that interferes with the study” exclusion criterion in 2.5% (58/2329), and the triglycerides inclusion criterion (fasting triglyceride &lt;400 mg/dL at screening) in 2.4% (56/2329) of screened subjects.
L1093: As shown in the table below, at Day 510 approximately 93% (1444/1561) of the ITT Population were still in the study; 92% (715/780) of placebo-treated subjects and 93% (729/781) of inclisiran-treated subjects. At Day 540 (EOS Visit) approximately 91% (1415/1561) completed the study; 89% (694/780) of placebo-treated subjects and 92% (721/781) of inclisiran-treated subjects. A total of 11% (86/780) of placebo-treated subjects discontinued the study. The most common reason for discontinuation from the study was withdrawal of consent (4.4%; 34/780). A total of 8% (60/781) of inclisiran-treated subjects discontinued the study. Similarly, the most common reason for discontinuation from the study was withdrawal of consent (3.1%; 24/781).
L1094: The disposition of subjects was reasonably balanced between the treatment groups. All but 2 of the 1561 randomized subjects were treated (778 with placebo and 781 with inclisiran) and included in the Safety Population. The Safety Population excludes 2 subjects assigned to inclisiran who were not dosed and 2 placebo subjects who were dosed instead with inclisiran.

L1114: *Reviewer comment: The demographic and baseline characteristics are largely balanced between the two treatment groups. The study enrolled 86% of subjects who are White and 13% who are Black or African American. There is very limited representation of Asian, American Indian or other groups. Fourteen percent of subjects identified as Hispanic or Latino. Two-thirds (69%) of the study population is male and the mean age is 66 years old. The study population included relevant comorbidities such as ASCVD (100%, primarily CHD), renal impairment (23% [356/1561] had a GFR between 15 and 60 mL/min/1.73m), hypertension (91%) and diabetes (45%).*
L1115: Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)
L1116: At baseline, use of lipid modifying therapy (LMT) was reasonably balanced between the treatment groups (see table below). Overall, 95% (1478/1561) of subjects received LMT, 89% (1393/1561) of subjects received statin therapy, 31% (489/1561) received other LMT and 5% (85/1561) received other LMT and no statin. Approximately 5% (83/1561) were not taking any LMT at baseline.
L1117: Overall, 69% (1084/1561) were on a high intensity statin, 19% (292/1561) were on a moderate intensity statin and 1% (12/1561) were on a low intensity statin at baseline. Approximately 10% (153/1561) of subjects were treated with ezetimibe.

The enrolled population adequately reflects the intended clinical population.*
L1126: ## Treatment Compliance and Concomitant Medications
L1127: ## Treatment Compliance
L1128: All study drugs were administered subcutaneously by clinical study site staff, primarily in the abdominal region (doses on Day 1, Day 90, Day 270, and Day 450). Overall, 86.9% (676/778) of placebo-treated subjects and 89.1% (696/781) of inclisiran-treated subjects received all 4 planned SC doses.
L1129: *Reviewer comment: Approximately, 87% of placebo-treated subjects and 89% of inclisiran-treated subjects received all 4 planned SC doses. This level of treatment compliance is adequate to allow for a good assessment of steady-state and durable LDL-C reduction.*
L1130: ## Changes in Lipid-Modifying Therapy (LMT) During Trial
L1131: Approximately 90% (1407/1561) of subjects had no new LMT added or changes to LMT usage during the study. Ten percent (81/780) of subjects in the placebo group and 9% (73/781) in the inclisiran group had changes in LMT use during the trial. Nine subjects (1%) in the placebo group began therapy with a PCSK9 monoclonal antibody and were discontinued; no subject in the inclisiran group began therapy with a PCSK9 monoclonal antibody.
L1132: Shifts from baseline in statin intensity were minimal in both treatment groups. Most subjects remained on their statin intensity category (high, moderate, low or none) throughout the trial.
L1133: New or changed concomitant medications during the study were similar between the placebo and inclisiran treatment groups.
L1134: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L1135: The applicant's co-primary endpoints for this trial are:
L1136: - Percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510.
- Time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540.
L1137: ## Percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510:
L1138: The placebo-adjusted change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 using observed values was -57.6% (p&lt;0.0001). The primary analysis used the same prespecified washout model as described in ORION-9 to account for missing data. The placebo-adjusted change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 using the imputed values was -52.2% (p&lt;0.0001).

L1153: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1154: The study was well executed and largely adhered to the protocol. The protocol amendments were reasonable and unlikely to have had a negative impact on the integrity of the trial or our interpretation of the results. Protocol deviations were similar between the treatment groups and were not believed to have a negative impact on study results. Subject retention was very good (91%). The applicant made reasonable efforts to blind study medication from subjects and investigators. There were no notable financial conflicts of interest. Datasets were complete,
L1155: generally straightforward to navigate, and of high quality. There were no potential issues concerning the submitted data quality or integrity that raise questions about the purported efficacy results. Thus, the study has generated data that are robust, interpretable, and persuasive in supporting the proposed indication.

L1164: *Reviewer comment: The results of the key secondary efficacy endpoints are consistent and supportive of the LDL-C reduction that was shown in the primary efficacy endpoint. This is expected as LDL-C is a major component of TC and non-HDL-C, and apolipoprotein B is the primary apolipoprotein of LDL-C. In addition, a reduction in PCSK9 levels is the mechanism of action through which inclisiran lowers LDL-C.*
L1165: Other secondary endpoints of interest include:

- At Day 510, 2.6% (17/666) of placebo-treated subjects had \ge50% LDL-C reduction from baseline compared with 72.8% (503/691) of inclisiran-treated subjects.
- Proportion of subjects in each group who attain global lipid targets for their level of ASCVD risk.At any visit, 36.1% (277/767) of placebo-treated subjects achieved their corresponding LDL-C targets compared with 94.1% (722/767) of inclisiran-treated subjects.
- At Day 510, 17.9% (119/666) of placebo-treated subjects with ASCVD achieved their LDL-C target of &lt;70 mg/dL compared to 84.1% (581/691) of inclisiran-treated subjects with ASCVD

L1187: ## Subgroup Analyses of Efficacy
L1188: Subgroup analyses for efficacy were performed based on multiple categories using MMRM to account for missing data (see figure below). In all subgroups, inclisiran lowered LDL-C more than placebo. The estimate of the treatment effect was generally similar across subgroups, although there was a trend toward increased magnitude of treatment effect with lower baseline LDL-C.
