L1191: Reviewer comment: In this trial of patients with ASCVD, inclisiran demonstrated a clinically meaningful reduction in LDL-C relative to placebo.
L1192: ## 6.3. ORION-11 (MDCO-PCS-17-08)
L1193: ## 6.3.1. Study Design
L1194: ## Overview and Objective
L1195: Title of Study: A placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized trial to evaluate the effect of 300 mg of inclisiran sodium given as subcutaneous injections in subjects with atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD) or ASCVD-risk equivalents and elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C)
L1196: ORION-11 was an international (non-US), multi-center study conducted (i.e., screened potential study subjects) in 72 centers in 8 countries: Czech Republic, Germany, Hungary, Netherlands, Poland, South Africa, Ukraine, and United Kingdom. The primary objective was to evaluate the effect of inclisiran treatment on LDL-C levels at Day 510. Other objectives included an assessment of the safety and tolerability profile of inclisiran.
L1197: ## Trial Design
L1198: The three pivotal Phase III trials, ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11, tested the efficacy and safety of inclisiran sodium 300 mg in subjects with HeFH (ORION-9; 482 subjects), ASCVD (ORION-10; 1561 subjects) and ASCVD and ASCVD Risk Equivalent (ORION-11; 1617 subjects). The design of these 3 trials were nearly identical, the main difference was the study population enrolled into the trial. In this section, only relevant differences in ORION-11 as compared to ORION-9 and ORION-10 will be described.
L1199: Key Inclusion Criteria that are unique to ORION-11 as compared to ORION-10:
L1200: - History of ASCVD (CHD, CVD or PAD) or ASCVD-risk equivalent32
- Serum LDL-C \ge 70 mg/dL at screening for ASCVD subjects or \ge 100 mg/dL for ASCVD-risk equivalent subjects at screening.
- Calculated glomerular filtration rate &gt; 30 mL/min by estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) using standardized clinical methodology.
L1201: Reviewer comment: As was discussed at the End-of-Phase 2 meeting on April 6, 2017 and in the FDA letter to the applicant on 9/13/2019, our current regulatory approach to LDL-C-lowering drugs is that the (b) (4) indication would likely be similar to that granted for evolocumab and alirocumab (i.e., as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy
L1202: 32 ASCVD risk equivalent is defined as those subjects with type 2 diabetes mellitus, FH, or 10-year risk of 20% or greater of having a CV event assessed by Framingham Risk Score or equivalent (target LDL-C of &lt; 100 mg/dL).
L1203: Clinical Review E. Craig and I. Chowdhury NDA 214012 LEQVIO™ (inclisiran)
L1204: for the treatment of adults with HeFH or clinical ASCVD, who require additional lowering of LDL
C) unless the applicant can provide positive results from a CVOT. The applicant was informed
(b) (4)
that they could include patients with ASCVD-risk equivalents in their phase 3 trials,





(b) (4)








The safety data should represent the entire trial
population (both with ASCVD and those with only ASCVD-risk equivalents) so changes would not
need to be made in reporting adverse event statistics.

Thus, in ORION-11, the presentation of the efficacy data will be slightly different than in ORION
10 (all ASCVD patients) and ORION-9 (all HeFH patients) in that the ASCVD-risk equivalent
(b) (4)
patients will be removed from the final efficacy findings
L1205: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L1206: Amendment 1 (05 Jan 2018) included both clinical and administrative amendments.  Clarifications were made to the protocol synopsis and main body to address comments  received from an FDA review of the study protocol and correct typographical errors noted since  finalization of the original version of the protocol. In this amendment, a U.S. IND No. (127589)  was added to the title page, as all sites were conducted under the US IND. Exclusion criteria  were modified to allow NYHA class III heart failure subjects and last known left ventricular  ejection fraction was changed from &lt;30% to &lt;25% based on FDA feedback. Additional details  were provided within the Efficacy Analysis section to clarify that the secondary efficacy  endpoints would not be tested if 1 of the co-primary efficacy endpoints null hypothesis failed to  be rejected. Withdrawal criteria were updated to clarify training requirements and the data  collected for subjects that terminated the study early. Also, initiation of protocol-prohibited  lipid-lowering therapy (e.g., an approved PCSK9 inhibitor) was added to the eCRF as one of the  pre-identified reasons for a subject to discontinue from participating in the study.
L1207: CDER Clinical Review Template 104
L1208: *Version date: September 6, 2017 for all NDAs and BLAs*
L1209: Text was updated in footnote 1 and 7 in the Schedule of Assessments table to clarify standards for vital sign measurements. The Screening Period (Day -14 to -1) section was updated to clarify the laboratory results required for Randomization, a reference was added, and duplicated text was removed. The Chemistry section was updated to clarify that HbA1C would not be performed on Days 150 and 330. Based on FDA request, updates and clarifications were made to the SAP sections of the protocol: The ITT population would be the primary population for the efficacy analysis. Efficacy analysis would also be performed for the mITT population as supportive analysis. Sensitivity analyses would be performed on the primary and key secondary efficacy endpoints to assess the impact of missing values. Sensitivity analyses to explore the dropout pattern and its possible impact on treatment comparisons would be performed. Pattern-Mixture Model (PMM) would be used to explore the possibility of data missing not at random (MNAR) for subjects who discontinued study treatment. A control-based pattern-mixture model would be utilized. Multiple imputation would be used to account for uncertainty in the imputation process and results from the imputed datasets would be combined using Rubin's method. Furthermore, a tipping point analysis would be performed to search for the tipping point that reverses the study conclusion.
L1210: Amendment 2 (31 Jan 2019) included only clinical amendments. In this amendment, the description of the co-primary endpoint was clarified to be "time adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540". Monitoring of ADA serum samples were updated to clarify the distinction between timing of sampling for ADA and which samples were to be assessed for ADA. Based on health authority feedback, a wider visit window (\pm 30) was added to the Schedule of Assessment table to give subjects greater flexibility to make visit windows and ensure more data captured. The modified windows were also aligned with the rules of protocol deviation. Based on FDA, updates and clarifications were made to the SAP sections of the protocol.
L1211: *Reviewer comment: The clinical team agreed with the applicant's protocol amendments and do not believe that these modifications had a negative impact on the integrity of the trial or our interpretation of the results.*
L1212: ## Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) Changes
L1213: An addendum to the SAP (dated 26 Sept 2019) incorporated changes to align the statistical analyses of this study to the SAPs of subsequent Phase III studies and integrated analyses. Key changes in the statistical analyses are listed below.
L1214: ## - Statin/LMT Use at Baseline
L1215: Most subjects (94.7%) were currently using statins or other LMT at baseline. The applicant states that due to the default method of equal weighting the effects in the models, the small number of subjects who were not currently using statins or other LMT at baseline were skewing the least squares means estimates of the treatment groups. Therefore, additional analyses
L1216: were performed excluding this effect from the efficacy models. All statistical models that included the statin or other LMT use at baseline effect were rerun with that effect removed ("a" tables in applicant's document).
L1217: • Modified Multiple Imputation Washout Model ("b" tables in applicant's document)
The multiple imputation washout model was modified such that subjects in the inclisiran group who received all 4 doses of study drug, had the Day 510 value missing, and had evaluable data at Day 540, had their intermittent missing Day 510 values imputed assuming the missing data were MAR. This washout model was pre-specified in the SAPs of subsequent Phase III studies and integrated analyses as the primary method of analysis for the primary endpoint of percentage change in LDL-C from baseline at Day 510. The applicant believes that this method is the most appropriate method for imputation of missing data as it provides the best estimate of what the values of missing data points would have been, had they been measured. All statistical models that used the multiple imputation washout model were rerun for the primary efficacy endpoint (percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510) using the modified multiple imputation washout model imputed data.
L1218: ## 6.3.2. Study Results
L1219: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L1220: The applicant attests that the study was conducted in compliance with the protocol, The Medicines Company's standard operating procedures and/or guidelines and any vendor partners where applicable, the US FDA regulations, the International Conference on Harmonisation (ICH) Good Clinical Practice (GCP) guidelines, the Declaration of Helsinki, and other local regulations, in operation at the time of the study.
L1221: ## Financial Disclosure
L1222: Refer to Section 13.2 Financial Disclosure for additional information.
L1223: ## Patient Disposition
L1224: The study was conducted in 72 centers in 8 countries: Czech Republic (2 centers), Germany (5 centers), Hungary (4 centers), Netherlands (1 center), Poland (19 centers), South Africa (8 centers), Ukraine (9 centers), and United Kingdom (24 centers).
L1225: A total of 2381 subjects were screened and 1617 were randomized. In ORION-11, approximately 32% of screened subjects were screen failures. The most common inclusion/exclusion criterion not met was the subject not meeting appropriate serum LDL-C
L1226: inclusion criterion of LDL-C \ge 70 mg/dL (19.5%; 465/2381 subjects). The second most common criterion not met was the exclusion criterion of any condition that interfered with the study in 3% (68/2381) of subjects.
L1227: The 1617 randomized subjects were enrolled in 71 sites from 7 countries (no subjects were enrolled from 1 site in Netherlands). Forty-seven of 71 sites had more than 10 subjects enrolled. Poland enrolled the most subjects (649, 44%), followed by United Kingdom (419, 29%), Ukraine (102, 7%) and Hungary (97, 7%).
L1228: As shown in the table below, at Day 510 approximately 97% (1561/1617) of the ITT Population were still in the study; 97% (779/807) of placebo-treated subjects and 97% (782/810) of inclisiran-treated subjects. At Day 540 (EOS Visit) approximately 95% (1542/1617) completed the study; 95% (770/807) of placebo-treated subjects and 95% (772/810) of inclisiran-treated subjects. A total of 5% (37/807) of placebo-treated subjects discontinued the study. The most common reason for discontinuation from the study was withdrawal of consent (2%; 17/807), followed by death (2%; 15/807). Similarly, a total of 5% (38/810) of inclisiran-treated subjects discontinued the study. The most common reason for discontinuation from the study was death (2%; 14/810) followed by withdrawal of consent (2%; 13/810).
L1229: The disposition of subjects was reasonably balanced between the treatment groups. All 1617 randomized subjects were included in the ITT Population (the primary efficacy analysis Population). All but 2 of the 1617 randomized subjects were treated (805 with placebo and 810 with inclisiran) and included in the Safety Population. Two subjects (both randomized to placebo) were excluded from the Safety Population because they did not receive any study drug. Another subject (Subject (b)(6)) was incorrectly administered inclisiran instead of placebo on Day 450. This subject is included in the inclisiran Safety Population but included in the placebo ITT Population.

L1254: Reviewer comment: The demographic and baseline characteristics are largely balanced between the two treatment groups. The study enrolled 98% of subjects who are White; there is minimal representation of non-white and Hispanic or Latino patients. Seventy-two percent of the study population is male and the mean age is 65 years old. The study population included relevant comorbidities such as ASCVD (87%, primarily CHD), renal impairment (13% had a GFR between 30 and 60 and 58% had a GFR between 60 and 90 mL/min/1.73m2), hypertension (81%) and diabetes (35%).
L1255: Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)
L1256: At baseline, use of lipid modifying therapy (LMT) was reasonably balanced between the treatment groups (see table below). Overall, 97% (1565/1617) of subjects received LMT, 95% (1532/1617) of subjects received statin therapy, 12% (191/1617) received other LMT and 2% (33/1617) received other LMT and no statin. Approximately 3% (52/1617) were not taking any LMT at baseline.
L1257: Overall, 78% (1261/1617) were on a high intensity statin, 16% (251/1617) were on a moderate intensity statin and 0.4% (6/1617) were on a low intensity statin at baseline. Approximately 7% (114/1617) of subjects were treated with ezetimibe.

L1264: ## ASCVD Only Patients
L1265: At baseline, 80.2% (563/702) of placebo-treated subjects with ASCVD were on a high intensity
L1266: statin at baseline and 80.5% (573/712) of inclisiran-treated subjects with ASCVD were on a high intensity statin at baseline. At baseline, 15% (106/702) of placebo-treated subjects and 15% (105/712) of inclisiran-treated subjects with ASCVD were on a moderate intensity statin at baseline. At baseline, 4% in each group were not on a statin and 2% in each group were on no lipid-modifying therapy.
L1267: *Reviewer comment: The majority (~78%) of participants in ORION-11 were on background high-intensity statin therapy, which is appropriate, as inclisiran, if approved, would be most clinically relevant in patients with HeFH or at higher CV risk not adequately controlled by maximally tolerated statin therapy. In terms of background lipid-lowering therapy, especially high-intensity statin use, the enrolled population adequately reflects the intended clinical population.*
L1268: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L1269: ## Treatment Compliance
L1270: All study drugs were administered subcutaneously by clinical study site staff, primarily in the abdominal region (doses on Day 1, Day 90, Day 270, and Day 450). Overall, 92% (748/804) of placebo-treated subjects and 91% (747/811) of inclisiran-treated subjects received all 4 planned SC doses.
L1271: *Reviewer comment: Over 91% of study subjects received all 4 doses of study drug. This level of treatment compliance is acceptable and will allow for a good assessment of steady-state and durable LDL-C reduction.*
L1272: ## Changes in Lipid-Modifying Therapy (LMT) During Trial
L1273: Nine percent (73/807) of subjects in the placebo group and 7% (54/810) in the inclisiran group had changes in LMT use during the trial. No subjects in either group began therapy with a PCSK9 monoclonal antibody.
L1274: Shifts from baseline in statin intensity were minimal in both treatment groups. Most subjects remained on their statin intensity category (high, moderate, low or none) throughout the trial.
L1275: New or changed concomitant medications during the study were similar between the placebo and inclisiran treatment groups.
L1276: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L1277: *Reviewer comment: The following sections of the efficacy evaluation focuses on the ITT Population of 1617 subjects but will also include a separate evaluation of efficacy in ASCVD subjects only; this evaluation excludes the 203 ASCVD-risk-equivalent subjects.*
L1278: The applicant's co-primary endpoints for this trial are:
L1279: - Percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510.
- Time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540.
L1280: ## Percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510:
L1281: The primary analysis used the same prespecified washout model as described in ORION-10 to account for missing data. The placebo-adjusted change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 using the imputed values was -47.8% (p&lt;0.0001).

L1286: * A multiple imputation washout model was used for missing data imputation with 100 total imputed datasets. Analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) on each of the 100 datasets was performed by including fixed effects for treatment and baseline LDL-C as a covariate. Treatment effects from the 100 analyses were combined using Rubin's Method.
L1287: Three additional, prespecified sensitivity analyses (PMM, MMRM, ANCOVA with country as a covariate) for handling missing values were used to analyze the percentage change from baseline at Day 510 primary endpoint. Similar (-51.8%, -52.7% and -49.9%) and statistically significant (p&lt;0.0001), placebo-adjusted differences were demonstrated for this primary endpoint, regardless of sensitivity analysis used to handle missing values.
L1288: *Reviewer comment: In a population with ASCVD and ASCVD-risk-equivalents, inclisiran lowers LDL-C to a clinically meaningful degree: -44% from the inclisiran group's baseline and -48% from baseline as compared to the placebo group.*

L1308: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1309: The study was well executed and largely adhered to the protocol. The protocol amendments were reasonable and unlikely to have had a negative impact on the integrity of the trial or our interpretation of the results. Protocol deviations were similar between the treatment groups and were not believed to have a negative impact on study results. Subject retention was excellent (95% over 18 months). The applicant made reasonable efforts to blind study medication from subjects and investigators. There were no notable financial conflicts of interest. Datasets were complete, generally straightforward to navigate, and of high quality. There were no potential issues concerning the submitted data quality or integrity that raise questions about the purported efficacy results. Thus, the study has generated data that are robust, interpretable, and persuasive in supporting the proposed indication.

L1343: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L1344: ## Subgroup Analyses of Efficacy
L1345: Subgroup analyses for efficacy were performed based on multiple categories using MMRM to account for missing data (see figure below). In all subgroups, inclisiran lowered LDL-C more than placebo. The estimate of the treatment effect was generally similar across subgroups, although there was a trend toward increased magnitude of treatment effect with lower baseline LDL-C, similar to that observed in ORION-10. In this trial, there was also a possible trend towards greater treatment effect in patients on statin compared to patients not on statin, and in patients on higher-intensity statin compared to lower intensity statin. These trends related to background lipid management were not apparent in subgroup analyses of ORION-10.
L1346: Figure 7. ORION-11: Forest Plot of Treatment Differences in Percentage Change from Baseline in LDL-C at Day 510 - Subgroup Analyses - ITT Population
L1347: Source: FDA Information Request to Applicant dated 5/26/20, ORION-11 FP Day 510 CSR v2.1 (corrected)
L1348: Reviewer comment: In this trial of patients with ASCVD and ASCVD-risk-equivalents, inclisiran demonstrated a clinically meaningful reduction in LDL-C relative to placebo.
L1349: ## 7. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L1350: ## 7.1. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1351: ## 7.1.1. Primary Endpoints
L1352: The focus of Section 7 will be pooled analyses or comparisons for the three phase 3 trials, ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11.
L1353: - ORION-9: 482 subjects with HeFH
- ORION-10: 1561 subjects with ASCVD, and
- ORION-11: 1617 subjects with ASCVD or ASCVD risk equivalents.
L1354: All 3 trials were placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized, multicenter, 18-month trials of nearly identical design which provided data from a total of 3,660 subjects at CV risk (3457 with HeFH or ASCVD) with elevated LDL-C levels, despite receiving the maximally tolerated dose of LDL-C lowering therapies. The trials used the same study endpoints, the same inclisiran dose (300 mg) and dosing regimen, and the same visit schedule; details of this are described in Section 6.
L1355: The applicant's co-primary efficacy endpoints were:
L1356: - Percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510
- Time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540
L1357: As discussed in Section 6 in more detail, the family-wise type I error rate was controlled at a 2-sided significance level of \alpha=0.05 by using a nested testing procedure. The percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 was tested first. If the null hypothesis was rejected at a 2-sided significance level of \alpha=0.05 and superiority of inclisiran over placebo was claimed, then the time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540 was to be tested, also at a 2-sided significance level of \alpha=0.05. The primary endpoints used a reflexive LDL-C approach: either calculated LDL-C (based on the Friedewald formula) was used, or if the calculated LDL-C was &lt; 40 mg/dL or triglycerides were &gt; 400 mg/dL, or calculated LDL-C was missing, directly measured (using ultracentrifugation) LDL-C was used if it was available. For percentage change in LDL-C, missing values were imputed for LDL-C after a reflexive approach using the multiple imputation (100 total imputed datasets) washout model. For time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C, a control-based PMM was used to explore the possibility of missing data being missing not at random (MNAR) for subjects who discontinued
L1358: the study.
L1359: The difference between investigational study drug and placebo in mean percent change in LDL-C from baseline to study endpoint is the regulatory standard to support approval of LDL-C lowering therapies. The strong evidence that has established LDL-C as a causal factor in the development of ASCVD and that robust and sustained lowering of LDL-C can improve CV outcomes is discussed in Section 2.1.
L1360: The second co-primary endpoint of time-adjusted percentage change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540 provides an estimate of LDL-C changes over time. The data demonstrate a consistency with the Day 510 results and confirms how we expect the treatment to work over time. (b) (4)
L1361: as it is important to demonstrate that LDL-C lowering is consistently maintained over time, FDA labels of recently approved LDL-C lowering therapies do contain figures that show the mean percent change from baseline in LDL-C over the duration of the trial (usually ~ 1 year) in patients treated with placebo and patients treated with study drug.
L1362: Reviewer comment:
L1363: *I do recommend including figures that show the mean percent change from baseline in LDL-C over the duration of the 18-month trial in patients in the Phase 3 trials.* (b) (4)
L1364: Patient Disposition in Phase 3 Trials: Overall, 93.5% (3422/3660) of subjects completed the Phase III trials (92.8% [1695/1827] of placebo-treated subjects and 94.3% [1728/1833] of inclisiran-treated subjects). The most common reasons for discontinuing the study were withdrew consent (placebo-treated subjects 55 [3%], inclisiran-treated subjects 37 [2%]); death (placebo-treated subjects 27 [1.5%], inclisiran-treated subjects 27 [1.5%]); and lost to follow-up (placebo-treated subjects 29 [1.6%], inclisiran-treated subjects 17 [0.9%]).
L1365: ## Lipid-Modifying Therapies (LMT)
L1366: LMT usage at baseline was similar in the inclisiran and placebo groups in the individual trials and the pooled population. Looking at all 3 trials together, almost all subjects (94% [3441/3660]) were on statins at study entry. High-intensity statins were prescribed to 74% (2701/3660), moderate-intensity statins were prescribed to 17% (613/3660) and 14% (513/3660) were taking ezetimibe. Nine placebo-treated subjects and no inclisiran-treated subjects started a PCSK9 monoclonal antibody during the Phase III trials.

L1375: ## ADAs analysis
L1376: Assessment of ADA occurred at Randomization (prior to injection), Day 30, Day 150, Day 330, and Day 510. At other study visits, samples were collected and stored. If a subject had a positive sample in the screening assay, all stored samples for that subject were also analyzed in the screening assay in order to provide a complete time course of the ADA profile for that subject. Each positive sample in the screening assay was subsequently analyzed in the confirmatory assay and if positive in the confirmatory assay then the sample was analyzed in the titration assay to determine the ADA titer.
L1377: The three phase 3 trials of 18-months duration included approximately 9,100 ADA assays from 1830 subjects treated with inclisiran sodium 300mg. There were 1740 subjects with no confirmed positive ADA, 49 subjects with confirmed post-baseline positive ADA with a maximum titer level of 50 and 100, and 41 subjects with a confirmed post-baseline positive ADA with a maximum titer level of 200, 400, and 800.
L1378: In response to an FDA information request, the applicant provided figures of observed LDL-C and PCSK9 level over time according to ADA maximum titer levels in patients in the inclisiran treatment arm for the Safety Pool. The figures show that ADA confirmed positive samples by titer level were not associated with a loss of effect for either LDL-C or PCSK9 levels over time.
L1379: In response to an FDA information request, the applicant provided waterfall plots of percent change from Baseline in LDL-C at Last Value during the treatment period according to ADA status33 in patients on inclisiran in the Safety Pool. The pattern of the individual subject responses in these 3 groups is similar and does not show that a positive ADA response was associated with a reduction in LDL-C lowering with inclisiran.
L1380: *Reviewer comment: Across the phase 3 trials, the LDL-C reduction ranges from -48 to -52%. The majority of subjects in the inclisiran group experienced a sizable LDL-C reduction at Day 510 compared to the placebo group. These results are consistent, statistically significant and clinically meaningful across trials. The limitation to this evidence of effectiveness in LDL-C reduction is that the effect of inclisiran on cardiovascular morbidity and mortality has not been determined.*
L1381: ## 7.1.2. Secondary and Other Endpoints

- Time adjusted absolute change in LDL-C from baseline after Day 90 and up to Day 540
- Percentage change from baseline to Day 510 in PCSK9, total cholesterol, ApoB, and non-HDL-C
L1384: The Hochberg procedure was applied to control the family-wise type I error rate at a 2-sided significance level of \alpha=0.05 for the key secondary endpoints.
L1385: *Reviewer comment: Key secondary endpoints that are relevant to clinical practice include percent change in non-HDL-C, TC and Apo-B from baseline to Day 510. The applicant is seeking labeling claims based on these pre-specified secondary endpoints and I agree that they should be presented in the product labeling.*

* A control-based pattern mixture model (PMM) was used for missing data imputation with 100 total imputed datasets. A mixed-effects model for repeated measures (MMRM) on each of the 100 datasets was performed by including fixed effects for treatment, visit, interaction between treatment and visit, and baseline LDL-C as a covariate. A linear contrast at Day 510 was used to compare treatment groups. Treatment effects from the 100 analyses were combined using Rubin's Method.
Source: modified by reviewer from Applicant's ISE Table 21
L1393: ## Reviewer comment:
L1394: Across the phase 3 trials, the TC reduction ranges from -30 to -33%; the Apo-B reduction ranges from -36 to -43%; and the non-HDL-C reduction ranges from -42 to -47%, compared to placebo. The results of the key secondary efficacy endpoints are consistent, statistically significant, clinically meaningful and supportive of the LDL-C reduction that was shown in the primary efficacy endpoint. This is expected as LDL-C is a major component of TC and non-HDL-C; in addition, apolipoprotein B is the primary apolipoprotein of LDL-C.
L1395: Across the phase 3 trials, the TG reduction ranges from -9 to -12%; the HDL-C increase ranges from +3 to +6%; and the Lp(a) reduction ranges from -19 to -32%, compared with placebo. Although epidemiological studies suggest that Lp(a) levels are independently associated with atherosclerotic disease, it is unclear if modifying Lp(a), per se, with inclisiran would reduce cardiovascular risk among patients with well-controlled LDL-C but elevated Lp(a). In addition, it is unclear whether the modest changes in TG and HDL-C observed are clinically meaningful.

L1402: * 3655 subjects (481, 1559, and 1615 subjects in ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11, respectively), were included in the Safety Population. In ORION-10, the Safety Population excludes 2 subjects assigned to inclisiran who were not dosed and 2 placebo subjects who were dosed instead with inclisiran. In ORION-11, two subjects (both randomized to placebo) were excluded from the Safety Population because they did not receive any study drug. Another subject was incorrectly administered inclisiran instead of placebo on Day 450. This subject is included in the inclisiran Safety Population but included in the placebo ITT Population.

L1403: *Reviewer comment: The demographic and baseline characteristics are largely balanced between the two treatment groups. The study enrolled 92% of subjects who are White, 6% who are Black or African American and &lt;1% from other racial groups. Thus, there is limited representation of Black groups and inadequate representation of Asian, American Indian or other groups. Six percent of subjects identified as Hispanic or Latino. Two-thirds (68%) of the study population is male and the mean age is 64 years old. Of the 1833 patients treated with inclisiran in the Phase 3 trials, approximately 980 (54%) patients were ≥ 65 years and 240 (13%) patients were ≥ 75 years. The study population included relevant comorbidities such as ASCVD (85%, primarily CHD), renal impairment (mild [56%, 2,046/3660], moderate [16%, 582/3660], or severe [0.5%, 20/3660]) as assessed by the central laboratory at screening using eGFR, hypertension (80%) and diabetes (36%). Thus, with the limitations of underrepresentation of non-white groups and only 32% women, participants studied in the Phase III program adequately represent the intended target population for inclisiran treatment for patients with ASCVD and/or HeFH as an adjunct to maximally tolerated statin therapy.*
L1404: ## Placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 for subgroups
L1405: Treatment differences in percentage change from baseline in LDL-C at Day 510 in the pooled ORION-9, -10 and -11 trials by subgroup – ITT Population is shown in Figure 9 and described in more detail below.
L1406: Sex: There was no meaningful difference in the estimate of the treatment effect by sex. In the 3 Phase III trials, 67% (2470/3660) of subjects were male and 33% (1190/3660) were female. In the pooled group, as in the individual trials, similar and statistically significant placebo-adjusted
L1407: LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were observed (males: -54%; females: -55%).
L1408: **Age:** There was no difference or trend in the estimate of the treatment effect by age. In the 3 Phase III trials, 47% (1737/3660) of subjects were &lt;65 years and 53% (1923/3660) were ≥65 years; 87% (3168/3660) of subjects were &lt;75 years and 13% (492/3660) were ≥75 years. In the pooled group, as in the individual trials, similar and statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were observed: &lt;65 years: -54%; ≥65 years: -54%; &lt;75 years: -54%; ≥75 years: -55%.
L1409: ## Body Mass Index
L1410: Overall, 50% (1830/3660) of subjects in the Phase 3 trials had a baseline BMI less than the median value (29.7 kg/m2) and 50% (1828/3660) had a baseline BMI greater than the median value. In the pooled group, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were observed in both subgroups. As was seen in the individual trials, a slightly larger percentage change was noted in subjects with a BMI higher than the median (&gt;29.74 kg/m2: -57%) compared with subjects with a BMI lower than the median (≤29.74 kg/m2: -52%).
L1411: ## Race
L1412: There was no meaningful difference in the estimated treatment effect by race. In the 3 Phase III trials, 92% (3378/3660) of subjects were White and 6% (232/3660) of subjects were Black/African-American and &lt;2% (50/3660) were of other races. In the pooled group, similar and statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were seen in Whites (-54%), Blacks/African Americans (-54%), and subjects of other races (-50%).
L1413: ## Ethnicity
L1414: In the 3 Phase III trials, 94% (3424/3660) of subjects were not Hispanic or Latino and 6% (236/3660) were Hispanic or Latino. In the pooled group, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were observed in both subgroups. A larger percentage change was noted for subjects who were not Hispanic or Latino (-55%) compared with subjects with the ethnicity of Hispanic or Latino (-44%).
L1415: *Reviewer comment: Whether this is due to the smaller sample size and increased variability of the result or inter-individual differences or is a true reflection of ethnic differences is not known; further investigation with a larger sample size of Hispanic/Latino individuals would be needed to clarify this.*
L1416: ## Study Center Region
L1417: Overall, 45% (1649/3660) of subjects in the Phase III trials were from North America, 47%
L1418: (1713/3660) from Europe, and 8% (298/3660) from South Africa. In the pooled group, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were observed in all subgroups. A larger percentage change was noted for subjects from North America (-56%) and South Africa (-58%) compared with subjects from Europe (-51%).
L1419: ## Baseline Statin Use
L1420: In the Phase III trials, 92% (3361/3660) were receiving a statin at baseline and 8% (299/3660) were not receiving statins at baseline. In the pooled group, as in the individual trials, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were observed in subjects on or off statins; however, a larger percentage change was noted for subjects on statins (-55%) compared with subjects off statins (-49%). As the group not receiving a statin was small compared to the group taking a statin, the difference could be due to chance. It is also possible that patients not taking statins at baseline were somehow different than on-statin patients, accounting for the difference.
L1421: ## Baseline Statin Intensity
L1422: In the Phase III trials, 74% (2701/3660) of subjects were on high-intensity statins and 26% (959/3660) of subjects were not. In the pooled group, similar and statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were seen in subjects on a high-intensity statin (-55%) and subjects not on a high-intensity statin (-53%).
L1423: ## Baseline Triglyceride Level
L1424: Overall, 50% (1832/3660) of subjects in the Phase 3 trials had baseline triglycerides less than or equal to the median value (130 mg/dL) and 50% (1828/3660) had values greater than the median value. Similar and statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were observed in subjects with baseline triglycerides \le 130 mg/dL (-52%) and &gt; 130 mg/dL (-56%).
L1425: ## Metabolic Disease
L1426: Overall, 36% (1318/3660) of subjects in the Phase III trials had diabetes, 28% (1025/3660) had metabolic syndrome, and 36% (1317/3660) had neither. In the pooled group, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were observed for these 3 subgroups. A larger percentage change was noted for subjects with diabetes (-56%) and subjects with metabolic syndrome without diabetes (-56%) compared with subjects with neither diabetes or metabolic syndrome (-51%). In ORION-9, which enrolled patients with HeFH, 10% of patients had diabetes at baseline compared to 45% in ORION-10 and 35% in ORION-11. In the overall pool, 3.6% (48/1318) of patients had HeFH along with DM compared to 18.5% (434/2342) in the group without DM (this group included patients with metabolic syndrome). This imbalance in the proportion of patients with HeFH may have contributed to the finding that patients with diabetes experienced a slightly greater decrease in LDL-C.
L1427: ## Renal Function
L1428: In the Phase 3 studies, severe renal impairment was seen in 0.5% (20/3660) of subjects; 16% (582/3660) had moderate impairment; 56% (2046/3660) had mild impairment; and 28% (1012/3660) had normal renal function as assessed by the central laboratory at screening for study eligibility using eGFR. In the pooled group, similar and statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 were seen across the eGFR subgroups (normal renal function: -54%; mild renal impairment: -53%; moderate renal impairment: -57%).
L1429: ## Baseline LDL-C
L1430: Overall, 50.6% (1852/3660) of subjects in the Phase 3 studies had a baseline LDL-C less than or equal to the median value (100 mg/dL) and 49.4% (1808/3660) of subjects had a value greater than the median value. In the pooled group, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percentage changes from baseline to Day 510 was observed in both subgroups. Similar to what was seen in the individual trials, a larger percentage change was noted for subjects with baseline LDL-C \le100 mg/dL (-61%) compared with subjects with baseline LDL-C &gt;100 mg/dL (-47%).
L1431: Looking at baseline LDL-C by quartiles, particularly for ORION-10, ORION-11 and the pooled group, a decreasing percentage change in LDL-C was seen as baseline LDL-C values increased from the lowest to the highest quartile. However, an increasing absolute change in LDL-C was seen as baseline LDL-C values increased from the lowest to the highest quartile, showing that the higher the baseline LDL-C, the more absolute reduction of LDL-C was demonstrated, particularly in the inclisiran group.
L1432: The absolute change, and [percent change], in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 by baseline LDL-C quartile [LS mean difference (95% CI) from placebo] was as follows:
L1433: - Baseline LDL-C quartile \le 82 mg/dL: -45.4 mg/dL (-48.7, -42.1) [percent change was -65.2%]
- Baseline LDL-C quartile &gt; 82 and \le 100 mg/dL: -50.2 mg/dL (-53.7, -46.7) [percent change was -56.9%]
- Baseline LDL-C quartile &gt; 100 and \le 129 mg/dL: -55.0 mg/dL (-59.2, -50.7) [percent change was -50.7%]
- Baseline LDL-C quartile &gt; 129 mg/dL: -70.1 mg/dL (-76.2, -64.0) [percent change was -43.1%]

This shows the trend across the 3 trials of an increased absolute reduction in LDL-C with increased baseline LDL-C. This trend appears in both groups but is more obvious in the inclisiran group.

L1440: The figure below looks at the percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510 in ORION-9, -10 and -11. In ORION-10 and -11, the 2nd and 3rd quartile represents a narrow range of LDL values and may represent a more homogenous population than the 1st and 4th quartile, which includes patients in the extreme portions of the baseline LDL distribution. Across the quartiles in ORION-10 and -11, there is a slight trend showing a decreasing percentage change in LDL-C as baseline LDL-C values increased from the lowest to the highest quartile.

L1443: *Reviewer comment: Consistent, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percent changes from baseline to Day 510 were seen across various subpopulations defined by baseline demographic characteristics and disease comorbidities. The efficacy results are generally similar in various subpopulations. In the inclisiran group from ORION-10 and ORION-11, a trend towards decreasing percentage change in LDL-C, as compared to placebo, was seen as baseline LDL-C values increased from the lowest to the highest quartile. However, the regression analysis indicated that the effect is fairly flat across baseline LDL levels.*
L1444: ## 7.1.4. Dose and Dose-Response
L1445: Inclisiran was administered via single use prefilled syringe or a vial and syringe (300 mg inclisiran sodium in 1.5 mL aqueous solution containing 284 mg of inclisiran) for injection by a healthcare professional.
L1446: In each of the three Phase 3 trials (ORION-9, -10 and -11), inclisiran sodium 300 mg or placebo was administered as a subcutaneous (SC) injection on Day 1 with a second dose on Day 90,
L1447: followed by 6-month dosing intervals at Days 270 and 450 and follow-up until Day 540. The Phase III dose and dosage regimen for inclisiran was selected based on data from the ALNPCSSC-001 Phase I, ORION-1 Phase II studies and population PD modeling, since a limited number of doses and dose regimens were tested clinically. There were no clinically meaningful safety events at higher doses; therefore, dose selection was not limited or determined by safety findings.
L1448: ## ALN-PCSSC-001 Phase I study
L1449: ALN-PCSSC-001 was a placebo-controlled, Phase I, single-ascending-dose (SAD) and multiple-dose (MD) study that evaluated single subcutaneous (SC) doses of inclisiran sodium ranging from 25 mg to 800 mg and repeat doses of 125 mg to 500 mg administered either monthly (QM), every 2 weeks (Q2W), or weekly (QW). Inclisiran doses of 25 mg, 100 mg, 300 mg, 500 mg, and 800 mg were evaluated in the SAD phase of Study ALN-PCSSC-001 (see Table below). A dose-dependent reduction in LDL-C was seen up to 300 mg inclisiran, after which there was a plateauing of dose effect. Thus, doses &gt; 300 mg do not appear to result in greater LDL-C reduction.

L1463: ## ORION-1 Phase II study
L1464: ORION-1 (MDCO-PCS-15-01) was a Phase II, placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized, dose-finding study conducted in subjects with ASCVD or ASCVD risk equivalents and elevated LDL-C despite maximum tolerated dose of lipid modifying therapies. In ORION-1, subjects received a single-dose of inclisiran or matching placebo on Day 1. The single-dose groups (inclisiran 200 mg, 300 mg, 500 mg and 3 matching placebo groups) received no further study drug. The double-dose groups (placebo, inclisiran 100 mg, 200 mg, 300 mg and 3 matching placebo groups) received a second dose of study drug on Day 90.
L1465: In the single-dose groups, percentage change from baseline to Day 180, is shown in the table below. Doses &gt; 300 mg do not appear to result in clinically meaningful greater LDL-C reduction.

L1476: *Reviewer comment: Based on the evidence summarized above, the dose selection of inclisiran sodium 300 mg administered as a SC injection on Day 1 with a second dose on Day 90, followed by 6-month dosing intervals, is supported by the data and is appropriate.*
L1477: ORION-3 is the ongoing long-term extension study for subjects who completed ORION-1. Subjects who received inclisiran in ORION-1 are receiving inclisiran throughout ORION-3. Subjects who received placebo in ORION-1 received evolocumab for 1 year and were then transitioned to inclisiran for the remainder of the study. Subjects in this group either received their final dose of evolocumab on Day 336, approximately 24 days before their first dose of inclisiran or received their final dose of evolocumab on Day 360, the same study visit as their first dose of inclisiran.
L1478: There were 436 subjects from the ORION-1 study who were eligible to participate in ORION-3; 382 (88%) subjects continued into ORION-3. Of the 382 subjects participating in ORION-3, 290 had received inclisiran in ORION-1 and received inclisiran only in ORION-3; 92 subjects had received placebo in ORION-1 and received evolocumab followed by inclisiran in ORION-3.
L1479: In the placebo/evolocumab/inclisiran group, at Day 360 the percentage change in LDL-C from ORION-3 baseline was -47%. Thirty days after switching to the first administration of inclisiran, mean changes from baseline LDL-C were -56% (final dose of evolocumab on Day 336 group) and -64% (final dose of evolocumab on Day 360 group). The latter group likely had a greater change in LDL-C from the combined effect of evolocumab and inclisiran administered on the same day. By Day 450, LDL-C percentage change was similar for both groups: -42% and -44%.

L1481: Refer to 4.5 Clinical Pharmacology for details on dose-disease interactions.
L1482: ## 7.1.5. Onset, Duration, and Durability of Efficacy Effects
L1483: The onset of LDL-C lowering appears to occur by Week 2 with maximal response seen between Days 30 to 60 for most subjects in ORION-1. Inclisiran has a prolonged pharmacodynamic effect on LDL-C and PCSK9 levels after the treatment is stopped. As shown in the following figure from ORION-1, at Day 360, after a single dose, the mean percentage change in LDL-C from Baseline was -30% for 200 mg, -30% for 300 mg, and -28% for 500 mg. At Day 210, in the placebo group, the mean percent change from Baseline at Day 210 was 1%; in the inclisiran 300 mg group, the mean percent change from Baseline at Day 210 was -35%.

L1496: Inclisiran had a consistent and durable effect on the reduction of LDL-C over time in these three trials of 18 months duration.
L1497: ## 7.2. Additional Efficacy Considerations
L1498: ## 7.2.1. Considerations on Benefit in the Postmarket Setting
L1499: Inclisiran is administered by a health care provider via subcutaneous injection at Day 1, Day 90 and then every 6 months during the clinical trials and in the postmarketing setting. Thus, other than patients being non-compliant with clinic appointments to receive the injection, there should not be significant potential differences in how the drug was administered and used in the clinical trial and its expected use in the postmarket setting that could affect whether the benefit demonstrated in the clinical trials can reasonably be expected to be achieved in the postmarket setting.
L1500: In these phase 3 trials, the entry criteria and exclusions were appropriate for the population that might receive the drug. Of the 1833 patients treated with inclisiran in the Phase 3 trials, approximately 980 (54%) patients were \ge 65 years and 240 (13%) patients were 75 years of age and older. Non-white individuals and, to a lesser degree, women, were underrepresented in the enrolled population. Overall, participants studied in the Phase III program adequately represent the intended target population for inclisiran treatment for patients with ASCVD and/or HeFH as an adjunct to maximally tolerated statin therapy.
L1501: ## 7.2.2. Other Relevant Benefits
L1502: Some patients may prefer daily oral agents over injectable medications. Some patients may prefer an injection every 6 months to injections every 2 or 4 weeks, which is the dosing regimen for PCSK9 inhibitor monoclonal antibodies. It is possible, but not proven, that less frequent dosing with inclisiran may improve a patient's adherence. This may be a relevant benefit which factors in a small way into the overall assessment of the drug's benefit.
L1503: Inclisiran has a prolonged and robust pharmacodynamic effect on LDL-C levels which allows for infrequent dosing. However, unlike alirocumab, evolocumab and statins, we do not have CV outcome data with inclisiran that confirms a reduction in CV morbidity and mortality, nor do we have the long-term safety data assessment that is possible from a CVOT that is several years in duration and enrolls thousands of patients.
L1504: ## 7.3. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1505: LDL-C is a valid surrogate endpoint for evaluating risk reduction of CVD and/or CHD. LDL-C lowering has been proven to consistently reduce the risk of atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease in large randomized controlled trials of LDLC-lowering therapies, namely statins and PCSK9 inhibitors. In the absence of cardiovascular outcomes data, contemporary decisions to approve novel LDL-lowering therapies are influenced by the direction and magnitude of drug-induced changes in LDL-C, the mechanism of action of the novel agent, as well as the effects of the drug on other lipid parameters, markers of cardiometabolic risk, and any evidence for off-target toxicity.
L1506: Inclisiran's mechanism of action involves the reduction of intrahepatic PCSK9 levels which leads to an increase in LDL-C receptor recycling and expression on the hepatocyte cell surface, thereby increasing LDL-C uptake and lowering LDL-C. Patients that have defective LDL-C receptors and consequent diminution in LDL receptor activity, particularly patients with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia who are LDL receptor-negative, have a decreased LDL-C lowering response to drugs like statins and PCSK9 inhibitors that work through the LDL-C receptor. Inclisiran is therefore unlikely to show a sizable effect in these patients who have failed to show a meaningful LDL-C response to statins and PCSK9 inhibitor monoclonal antibodies.
L1507: As detailed in Section 6, ORION-9, ORION-10 and ORION-11 included a population of approximately 3500 patients with HeFH or ASCVD with elevated LDL-C despite maximally tolerated statin therapy who were treated with inclisiran sodium 300 mg administered subcutaneously at Day 1, Day 90 and every 6 months afterward for a duration of 18 months. The study population included relevant comorbidities such as increased age, renal impairment, hepatic impairment, hypertension and diabetes.
L1508: The data from these 3 trials demonstrated a robust, durable LDL-C reduction with supportive results in total cholesterol, non-HDL-C and apo-B endpoints. The LDL-C reduction of 48-52% is
L1509: clinically meaningful. The majority of subjects in the inclisiran group experienced a sizable LDL-C reduction at Day 510 compared to the placebo group. Consistent, statistically significant placebo-adjusted LDL-C percent changes from baseline to Day 510 were seen across various subpopulations defined by baseline demographic characteristics and disease comorbidities.
L1510: The applicant has submitted evidence of effectiveness that meets the statutory evidentiary standard. It is important to note that the cardiovascular outcome trial with inclisiran is ongoing so the effect of inclisiran on cardiovascular morbidity and mortality has not been determined. The lack of this data will limit the scope of the indication and thus supports a narrower indication than the applicant's proposed indication of:
L1511: - INCLISIRAN is indicated as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with primary hyperlipidemia (including heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia) to reduce low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C).
L1512: Based on data showing robust LDL-C reductions and an acceptable safety profile, this reviewer recommends approval for the following indication:
L1513: - INCLISIRAN is indicated as an adjunct to diet and maximally tolerated statin therapy for the treatment of adults with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) or clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (CVD), who require additional lowering of low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C).
L1514: ## 8. Review of Safety
L1515: ## 8.1. Safety Review Approach
L1516: This safety review focuses on the pooled data (Safety Pool) from the three controlled Phase 3 studies: ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11 which were also the focus of the efficacy review. These three studies were similar in design---all were placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized and were 18 months in duration. Additionally, the studies had the same endpoints, used the same 300 mg inclisiran dose and dosing regimen, and had the same visit schedule.
L1517: The three studies investigated the effects of inclisiran on the spectrum of patients with primary hyperlipidemia. ORION-9 enrolled patients with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia, while ORION-10 comprised patients with atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease and ORION-11 included patients with atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease and atherosclerotic risk
L1518: equivalents.
L1519: In the Phase 3 studies, patients were randomized 1:1 to either inclisiran or placebo and were required to be on a background of maximally tolerated statin therapy with or without other LDL-C lowering agents such as ezetimibe before randomization. The Safety population was defined as all patients who received at least one dose of investigational product.
L1520: ## 8.2. Review of the Safety Database
L1521: ## 8.2.1. Overall Exposure
L1522: In the Safety population, which included all patients in clinical studies (Phase 1, 2, and 3), there were a total of 4332 patients, of which 2452 patients were exposed to any dose of inclisiran. Approximately 2118 patients were exposed to inclisiran 300 mg dose.

L1611: *Reviewer Comment: Overall, slightly fewer patients on inclisiran (20%) than on placebo (23%) reported at least one serious adverse event than. Most serious adverse events were well balanced between the treatment arms.*
L1612: *The most commonly reported serious adverse events were in the Cardiac disorders SOC (9.4% in Placebo and 8.0% in Inclisiran), followed by Infections (3.6% in Placebo and 3.9% in Inclisiran) and Nervous System disorders (2.7% in Placebo and 2.4% in Inclisiran). Adverse events under Cardiac disorders SOC is expected for the high-risk cardiac population (HeFH and ASCVD). Under Nervous System disorders, the preferred term "central nervous system vascular disorders" (such as strokes) were most frequent (1.4% -placebo and 1.5% -inclisiran) and would also be expected given the study population.*
L1613: *Although serious adverse events most frequently reported for inclisiran was in the Cardiac disorders SOC, the incidence was less than placebo (8.0% on inclisiran vs. 9.4% on placebo). High Level Terms most frequently reported under the Cardiac disorders was "coronary artery disorders NEC" (n=28, 1.5% for inclisiran and n=38, 2.1% for placebo) and "ischaemic coronary artery disorders" (n=72, 3.9% for inclisiran and n=86, 4.7% for placebo). It is not surprising that in the Safety pool population composed of HeFH, ASCVD and patients at risk for ASCVD, coronary artery disorders had such high incidence in both the placebo and inclisiran treatment arms. Although the trend in cardiovascular serious adverse events favoring inclisiran is not definitive, the results are reassuring. There is an ongoing cardiovascular outcomes trial to assess the effect of inclisiran on major adverse cardiovascular events.*
L1614: *Serious adverse events second-most frequently reported for the inclisiran treatment arm were in Infections and infestations SOC (n=71, 3.9%) which was slightly higher than placebo (n=66, 3.6%). High Level Term most frequently reported under this SOC was "Lower respiratory tract and lung infections", n=26, 1.4% for inclisiran and n=25, 1.4% for placebo. These events are expected for the patient population, and the incidence of events was balanced between arms.*
L1615: *Nervous system disorders SOC were the third most frequently reported serious adverse events. "Central nervous system vascular disorders" most often reported under this SOC, and the incidence was balanced between arms. Other preferred terms were less frequently reported with inclisiran than placebo.*

L1621: Reviewer Comment: Atrial fibrillation (n=20, 1.1%), pneumonia (n=21, 1.1%) and myocardial infarction (n=12, 0.7%) were reported at slightly higher incidence rates for inclisiran than placebo (0.8%, 0.9%, and 0.5%, respectively). L1630: *Reviewer Comment: Neoplasms benign, malignant and unspecified was the SOC category that was most frequently reported as a reason for withdrawal from study drug; the incidence was 0.5% (n=10) on placebo and 0.7% (n=13) on inclisiran.*
L1631: *Most reported neoplasms occurred with a n of 1, with the exception of prostate cancer with a n=4 and incidence rate of 0.2% on inclisiran. Prostate cancer was reported in 2 patients on placebo, at 0.1%. Therefore, the incidence rate for prostate cancer was similar between arms.*
L1632: *The next most commonly reported SOC that lead to withdrawal of treatment was Nervous system disorders, which was similar between arms at 0.4% (n=7) on placebo and 0.4% (n=8) on inclisiran. There were 3 stroke-related events (ischemic stroke and cerebrovascular accident) in the inclisiran treatment arm as compared to 1 stroke-related event (cerebral infarction) on placebo, but the numbers were too small to make any definitive conclusions.*
L1633: *Musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders was the third most commonly reported SOC that lead to permanent study drug discontinuation with 0.2% (n=4) on placebo and 0.4% (n=7) on inclisiran. The difference between arms was small, and no definitive conclusions can be made.*
L1634: *The most common preferred term reported as reason for withdrawal of treatment was injection site reaction, 0.2% (n=3), with inclisiran versus 0 events on placebo. In this reviewer's opinion, injection site hypersensitivity should be combined with injection site reaction and reported on the product labeling as 0.2%, n=4.*
L1635: ## 8.4.4. Significant Adverse Events
L1636: The applicant defined adverse events of special interest as: injection site reactions; drug-related hepatic disorders (standardized MedDRA queries); acute kidney injury (SMQ), new-onset/worsening of diabetes; hypersensitivity, neurological/ neurocognitive events; and ophthalmologic events. The applicant also examined abuse liability.
L1637: ## Drug-Related Hepatic Disorders
L1638: The applicant conducted a comprehensive search for drug related hepatic disorders using standardized MedDRA queries, (SMQs), both broad and narrow.
L1639: As per the pre-defined hepatic disorders SMQ, TEAEs were similar between the placebo-treated and inclisiran-treated patients. There were approximately 76 (4.2%) patients on placebo and 70 (3.8%) on inclisiran who reported these events. L1650: *Reviewer Comments: Elevations in ALT, AST, alkaline phosphatase, and total bilirubin above baseline were similar between the two treatment arms. Trends in elevations above various threshold were inconsistent. The overall incidence of AST/ALT elevations &gt;5X ULN was small. No patients experienced AST or ALT elevations above 10X in the Inclisiran arm, while one placebo patient experienced an AST elevation &gt;10X ULN.*
L1651: Hy's Law was defined as
L1652: - Any elevated post-baseline aminotransferases defined as:ALT &gt;3 x ULN or
- AST &gt;3 x ULN
- Elevated post-baseline serum total bilirubin &gt;2 x ULN and serum ALP levels &lt;2 x ULN
L1653: Patients must have met all of the criteria listed above at the same time point and have normal lab parameters (ALT, AST, total bilirubin) at baseline to be considered a Hy's Law case. Per FDA feedback, the applicant presented a second summary that included all subjects who met all of the criteria listed above at the same time point without regard to the baseline lab parameter (ALT, AST, total bilirubin) results. There were no cases meeting the Hy's Law definition in either treatment arm.
L1654: Figure 19. Hy's Law Screening Test: Peak ALT and Peak Total Bilirubin
L1655: *Reviewer Comment: In the figure above, no patient's ALT or bilirubin fell in Quadrant II (right upper quadrant), indicating there were no values that met Hy's Law parameters.*
L1656: Figure 20. Hy's Law Screening Test: Peak ALT, AST, Total Bilirubin and Alkaline Phosphatase
L1657: **Reviewer Comment:** No Hy's Law cases were found based on ALT or AST \ge 3 \times \text{ULN} and Total bilirubin \ge 2 \times \text{ULN} within 0 days of ALT or AST elevation.

L1699: ## *New Onset Diabetes*
L1700: New onset diabetes was defined by combining information from AEs, medications and laboratory parameters. In the subset of patients without a prior medical history of diabetes, new onset diabetes was defined to have occurred when any of the following occurred:
L1701: - at least one post-baseline HbA1C \ge6.5% or
- two consecutive values of fasting plasma glucose \ge126 mg/dL or
- if a new concomitant medication for control of plasma glucose was added or
- diabetic treatment emergent adverse events identified by SMO search
L1702: The following tables summarizes the incidence of new onset diabetes in patients without a history of diabetes mellitus at baseline and those who had a baseline fasting glucose of &lt; 100 mg/dL. No diabetes at baseline was defined as no medical history diabetes in the case report form, HbA1c &lt; 6.5% at baseline, and fasting glucose &lt; 126 at baseline.

L1739: category were similar between arms. More patients shifted from normal to diabetes on inclisiran (0.7%) than on placebo (0.4%). More patients who had impaired fasting glucose shifted to diabetes on inclisiran (9.2%) than on placebo (8.0%).
L1740: In summary, mean changes in fasting glucose and HbA1C were small, and not clearly different between inclisiran and placebo. Shift analyses indicated a small, but consistent greater incidence of worsening glycemia (normal to pre-diabetes, pre-diabetes to diabetes) with inclisiran compared to placebo.

this 67-year-old female assigned to the inclisiran 300 mg dose group experienced a nonserious adverse event of dermatitis and was discontinued from the study drug. The event was ongoing at the time of discontinuation. The investigator considered the event to be mild and not reasonably possibly attributable to study drug. Subject remained on the study and reported identical event in a year, next spring.
L1760: *Reviewer Comment: Although drug-related hypersensitivity cannot be excluded, the temporal pattern described in the history, and occurrence both on and off study drug are more consistent with dermatitis associated with seasonal allergens.*
L1761: Subject ID: [REDACTED] (ORION-11), Treatment Group: Inclisiran 300 mg
On [REDACTED] (95 days after receiving the first dose of blinded study drug [Day 96]),
this 71-year-old female with a prior history of asthma, experienced a nonserious adverse event of asthma and was discontinued from the study drug. Drug-related exacerbation cannot be entirely excluded, but the relatively long time between dosing and onset of the adverse reaction suggests an alternate etiology.*

L1848: *Reviewer Comment: As shown in the table above, patients in the inclisiran-treated arm, who reported a history of allergy (identified using the high-level group term "allergic conditions"), had a higher incidence of injection site reactions 12% vs. 7.4%. L1850: ## Neurologic Events

The incidence of AEs related to neurological disorders was similar between treatment groups, 2.9% on placebo vs 2.3% on inclisiran.

L1871: ## Psychiatric Disorders
L1872: A post-marketing review of PCSK9 inhibitor monoclonal antibodies (evolocumab and alirocumab) noted various psychiatric adverse events reported with the use of those drug products. Inclisiran is a double-stranded small interfering ribonucleic acid that inhibits translation of PCSK9 protein and has selectivity for the liver. According to Pharm/tox reviewer, quantitative whole body autoradiography studies performed with 14C-labeled inclisiran in rats and monkeys showed there was minimal distribution to the brain and spinal cord in both rats and monkeys.
L1873: However, because PCSK9 levels are reduced with inclisiran (similar pharmacodynamic effect to PCSK9 antibody inhibitors, which bind circulating PCSK9 and reduce free PCSK9 levels) this reviewer examined the reported adverse events related to psychiatric disorders.
L1874: The following table summarizes the more common adverse events related to Psychiatric disorders SOC submitted by the applicant.

L1890: ## Ophthalmologic Events
L1891: An early nonclinical study suggested papilledema findings in a two female monkeys in a 15-week study. However, further investigation showed no findings of papilledema in the 40-week toxicology study in monkeys, which used a board-certified veterinary ophthalmologist. The applicant stated that the use of the term papilledema was inappropriately used to capture optic disc variations. Since papilledema specifically refers to optic disc swelling that occurs secondarily to increased intracranial pressure and the finding of papilledema was not verified by the board-certified veterinary ophthalmologist, the Agency pharmacology/toxicology team agreed with the applicant that this finding was mislabeled. Additionally, there was no papilledema noted in the histopathology evaluation of the eye.
L1892: The applicant conducted an analysis of ophthalmologic events in the Phase 3 clinical trials using the following MedDRA SMOs:
L1893: - Optic nerve disorders, (SMQ, broad and narrow)
- Retinal disorders, (SMQ, narrow)
- Corneal disorders, (SMQ, narrow)
L1894: The number of patients who reported a TEAE related to ophthalmological events was n=21 (1.2%) in placebo compared to n=27 (1.5%) in inclisiran.

L1979: ## 8.4.9. QT
L1980: The QT Consultation team determined that there was no significant QTc prolongation effect for
L1981: inclisiran.
L1982: The effect of inclisiran was evaluated in a thorough QT study (Study # ORION-12), a phase 1, randomized, double-blind, double-dummy, placebo- and positive-controlled, 3-way crossover study in healthy subjects. The highest dose evaluated was 900 mg single dose (administered as 3 subcutaneous injections), which covers the worst-case exposure scenario. The data were analyzed using the by-time analysis as the primary analysis, which did not suggest that inclisiran is associated with significant QTc prolonging effect. The study included moxifloxacin as a positive control and assay sensitivity was established using by time analysis. The findings of this analysis were further supported by the available nonclinical data and exposure response analysis.
L1983: Below are proposed edits to the label submitted to SDN001 (link) from the IRT. Our changes are highlighted (**addition**, **deletion**). Please note, that this is a suggestion only and that we defer final labeling decisions to the Division.
L1984: ## 12.2 Pharmacodynamics
L1985: ## Cardiac Electrophysiology
L1986: **At a dose 3 times the maximum recommended dose, inclisiran does not prolong the QT interval to any clinically relevant extent.**
L1987: (b) (4)
L1988: *We propose to use labeling language for this product consistent with the “Clinical Pharmacology Section of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products – Content and Format” guidance.*
L1989: ## 8.4.10. Immunogenicity
L1990: ## Anti-Drug Antibody positivity
L1991: According to the applicant, in nonclinical studies, there were incidences of the development of anti-drug antibody (ADA), but they were generally low titer responses in a small number of animals and were not dose related. There was no association between the presence of ADA and changes in either systemic drug exposure or PCSK9 levels in repeat-dose studies in monkeys. Additionally, inclisiran did not stimulate pro-inflammatory cytokines in mice or monkeys or activate complement in monkeys. Please see the pharmacology/toxicology review for the Agency's assessment of ADA in nonclinical studies.
L1992: In the Phase 3 clinical program, samples for ADA assessment were collected for all patients at all study visits including at Baseline (prior to injection). However, ADA samples collected at Baseline, Day 30, Day 150, Day 330, and Day 510 visits were analyzed in the screening assay for inclisiran treated patients only. If an inclisiran treated patient had a positive sample in the screening assay, stored samples from that patient for all other time points were also analyzed in the screening assay in order to provide a complete time course of the ADA profile for that patient. Each positive sample in the screening assay was subsequently analyzed in the confirmatory assay, and if positive in the confirmatory assay then the sample was analyzed in the titration assay to determine the ADA titer.
L1993: Figure 29. Diagram of Anti-Drug Antibody Hierarchical Testing

At some time points, there seems to be a slight attenuation of efficacy (about 5 mg/dL) in LDL-C lowering in the group with titer of 200, 400 and 800. However, at other time points, there was no difference between the three groups of patients. Overall, there was no clinically significant difference in LDL-C lowering between the ADA titer groups.

L2019: *Reviewer Comment: The pattern of individual patient responses between patients with no confirmed positive ADA, transient confirmed ADA and persistent confirmed ADA appears similar.*
L2020: The applicant submitted individual patient profiles of patients who were ADA positive. This reviewer examined all treatment emergent AEs of ADA positive patients. No systemic allergic reactions, and no signs or symptoms suggestive of systemic allergic reactions were seen in ADA positive subjects. Five patients who were positive for ADA also had injection site reactions.
L2021: The OPO/OBP team (refer to review by Dr. Takahasi, in DARRTS dated 8/14/20; Reference ID: 4656320) reviewed the immunogenicity assay validation report for ELISA method for the detection of anti-inclisiran-reactive IgG/IgM antibodies in human serum. All the validation parameters were appropriately evaluated except for deficiencies where two separately prepared unpurified positive controls were used in the assay validation. The OBP team recommends that the assay sensitivity and all other assay validation parameters of the ELISA method for the detection of anti-inclisiran-reactive IgG/IgM antibodies in human serum except the cut-points using an affinity-purified anti-inclisiran antibody positive control should be re-determined to ensure the assay is a sensitive, specific, and selective assay to measure ADA responses. All existing clinical samples should be re-analyzed using the appropriately validated assay. The OBP team recommends approval with possible PMC as described above.
L2022: ## 8.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L2023: Most submission specific safety issues are discussed under Section 8.4, Significant Adverse Events. Theoretical risks associated with low LDL-C, but not specifically identified in this submission as adverse events of special interest include cerebrovascular hemorrhage, primary adrenal insufficiency, primary gonadal insufficiency, and fat-soluble vitamin related disorders are explored in this section.

L2031: ## 8.5.2. Primary adrenal insufficiency
L2032: The sponsor conducted the analysis for adverse events related to primary adrenal insufficiency with a search for terms under the High Level Term: "Adrenal Cortical Hypofunctions". No adverse events were found with this search in either treatment arm.
L2033: ## 8.5.3. Primary gonadal insufficiency
L2034: The sponsor conducted the analysis for adverse events related to primary gonadal insufficiency with a search for terms under the High Level Term: "Endocrine Abnormalities of Gonadal Function NEC". There was one patient in inclisiran arm with hypogonadism.
