L2054: ## 8.7. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L2055: ## Phase 2 trials
L2056: The Phase 2 trials included ORION-1 (dose-finding study), ORION-2 (pilot study in HoFH), and ORION-3 (extension study of ORION-1).
L2057: ## ORION-1
L2058: ORION-1 was a Phase 2, placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized study to compare the effect of inclisiran administered as either a single-dose (200 mg, 300 mg, or 500 mg) or as 2
L2059: doses (100 mg, 200 mg, or 300 mg) on Day 1 and Day 90 in approximately 501 patients \ge18 years of age with high CV risk and elevated LDL-C. Most patients in the single-dose and double-dose parts of the study were male (67.2% and 62.9%, respectively), White (92.0% and 94.4%, respectively), and on statins (80.6% and 81.5%, respectively). Overall, 93.3% and 94.0% of patients completed the single-dose and double-dose parts of the study.
L2060: There were two deaths in the study. One death occurred in the 500 mg inclisiran single dose group due to an MI. This subject had a history of previous MI and CHD. A second death occurred in the 200 mg inclisiran double dose group due to sepsis and pneumonia following surgery for aortic disease.
L2061: In the single-dose groups, 3 (4.6%) placebo-treated patients, and 30 (16.1%) of the inclisiran-treated patients experienced SAEs. All SAEs were reported as single cases with the exception of MI (2 patients in the 500 mg inclisiran group and one patient in the 200 mg inclisiran group), angina pectoris (one in each of the 3 inclisiran groups), prostate cancer (2 subjects in the 200 mg inclisiran group), and syncope (1 patient in each the 200 mg and 300 mg inclisiran groups).
L2062: In the double-dose groups, overall, 7 (11.3%) placebo-treated patients, and 30 (16.3%) inclisiran-treated patients experienced SAEs. All SAEs were reported as single cases, with the exception for acute MI (2 patients in the 300 mg inclisiran group), atrial tachycardia (one patient in the 100 mg and 200 mg groups), CHD (2 patients in the 300 mg inclisiran group), post procedural complication (2 patients in the 200 mg inclisiran group), and syncope (one patient in the 200 mg and 300 mg inclisiran dose groups, and 1 placebo-treated patient).
L2063: No patient withdrew from treatment due to an AE in the single-dose groups. In the double-dose groups, one placebo-treated subject and one subject in the inclisiran 100 mg group withdrew due to an AE.
L2064: For the double-dose group, a total of 2 subjects discontinued study drug through Day 360 due to AEs:
L2065: - A placebo-treated patient had an AE of Herpes zoster that resulted in discontinuation of study drug prior to Day 90.
- A patient in the 100 mg inclisiran group had AEs of influenza-like illness, nasopharyngitis, back pain and myalgia that resulted in discontinuation of study drug prior to Day 90.
L2066: In the placebo-treated and inclisiran-treated patients, TEAEs occurred in 83.9% and 78.5% of subjects in the single-dose group, respectively, and 83.7% and 82.3% of subjects in the double dose group, respectively.
L2067: In the single-dose group, the most common AEs in the inclisiran-treated patients (\ge 5\% of subjects who received inclisiran) were nasopharyngitis (26 patients [14.9%]), influenza, back pain and arthralgia (12 patients [6.5%], each), upper respiratory tract infection, and myalgia (11 patients [5.9%] each), and angina pectoris (10 patients [5.4%]).
L2068: In the double-dose group, the most common AEs in the inclisiran-treated patients (\ge 5\% of patients who received inclisiran) were nasopharyngitis (24 patients [13.9%]), myalgia (17 patients [9.2%]), diarrhea (15 patients [8.2%]), influenza (12 patients [6.5%]), headache and back pain (11 patients [6.0%] each), and arthralgia (10 patients [5.5%]).
L2069: ## ORION-3
L2070: ORION-3 is an ongoing, open-label, multicenter, long-term (4 year) extension in patients who completed ORION-1. The study consists of 2 treatment groups; Group 1 (inclisiran only arm) is receiving inclisiran only and Group 2 (switching arm) received evolocumab for 1 year followed by inclisiran for the remainder of the trial. The applicant included all available data up to Day 630 of the study.
L2071: Patients entering the study were not randomly assigned to treatment arms. There were 436 patients from ORION-1 eligible to participate in ORION-3 and 382 (87.6%) patients consented. Of the 382 patients who consented to participate in ORION-3, 290 received inclisiran in ORION-1 and therefore, were assigned to Group 1 (inclisiran only arm) in ORION-3. The remaining 92 consented patients had received placebo in ORION-1 and therefore, were assigned to Group 2 (switching arm) in ORION-3.
L2072: In Group 1, there was one death. Patient ID# [REDACTED](b) (6) a 78-year-old, White, male died due to ischemic cardiovascular accident. The patient's ischemic cardiovascular accident was subsequent to a traffic accident. This patient had a medical history that included hyperlipidemia, hypertension, peripheral artery disease, prior transient ischemic attack, and right internal carotid artery stenosis.
L2073: Overall, 60 (21.1%) Group 1 inclisiran-treated patients experienced a SAE. Treatment emergent SAEs were predominantly CV events typical for patients with dyslipidemia. The majority of SAEs were reported as a single event and there was no specific SAE occurring in more than 3 patients. The SAE occurring in 3 subjects were anemia, angina pectoris, osteoarthritis, prostate cancer, and transient ischemic attack.
L2074: Overall, 8 (2.8%) Group 1 inclisiran-treated patients withdrew from treatment due to TEAEs and included an eye contusion, abnormal liver function test, diabetes mellitus, muscle weakness, plasma cell myeloma, tremor, and skin depigmentation.
L2075: The most common TEAEs (those occurring in \ge 2\% patients) observed were nasopharyngitis,
L2076: influenza, urinary tract infection, and injection site reactions.
L2077: In Group 2, there were no deaths reported. Sixteen (17.8%) patients treated with evolocumab for 360 days experienced an SAE. The majority of SAEs were reported as a single event and there was no specific SAE occurring in more than 2 patients. The SAE occurring in 2 patients was hemorrhoidal hemorrhage. After Day 360, 10 (11.5%) patients treated with inclisiran from Day 360 to the data cut-off date experienced a SAE. All SAEs were reported as a single event and there was no specific SAE occurring in more than 1 patient.
L2078: One patient (Pt ID# [REDACTED](b) (6)) treated with inclisiran from Day 360 to the data cut-off date had a SAE (hepatic enzyme increased) leading to withdrawal from treatment. The patient had hepatitis C and reported recent excessive alcohol consumption.
L2079: The most common TEAEs in patients treated with inclisiran from Day 360 to the data cut-off date were similar to TEAEs observed prior to Day 360. These events were hypertension, nasopharyngitis, arthralgia, influenza, and injection site pain.
L2080: ## ORION-2
L2081: ORION-2 was a Phase 2, open-label, single-arm, multicenter pilot study to test the efficacy and duration of effect of inclisiran sodium 300 mg in patients with HoFH. Four patients were treated with inclisiran: 3 patients receiving 2 injections and 1 patient received 1 injection.
L2082: Three of the 4 patients reported TEAEs [ORION-2 CSR Section 12]. Two patients had 1 TEAE each (unstable angina and acne flare-up). The third patient had several TEAEs, including nasopharyngitis, cystitis, dyspnea, road traffic accident, influenza-like illness, musculoskeletal pain, non-cardiac chest pain, oropharyngeal pain, abdominal pain upper, paresthesia, pain in extremity and limb injury events.
L2083: No deaths or TEAEs leading to treatment withdrawal were reported in ORION-2. One SAE was reported (unstable angina) in a 50-year-old White male with a medical history of hyperlipidemia, prior coronary artery bypass graft, and prior percutaneous coronary intervention study.
L2084: *Reviewer Comments: The overall safety profile for inclisiran in the Phase 2 studies was generally similar to that seen in the Phase 3 studies.*
L2085: ## 8.8. Additional Safety Explorations
L2086: ## 8.8.1. Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L2087: No additional safety studies.
L2088: ## 8.8.2. Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L2089: Three inclisiran exposed pregnancies occurred during the Phase 3 trials, with treatment discontinued in all cases. One pregnancy went to term with delivery of a full-term healthy baby, one pregnancy was voluntarily terminated before 12 weeks, and 1 pregnancy was ongoing.
L2090: The Division of Pediatric and Maternal Health (DPMH) completed a consult review regarding proposed labeling to comply with the Pregnancy and Lactation labeling rule34. The following summarizes DPMH's conclusions.
L2091: ## Pregnancy
L2092: - In animal reproduction studies, no evidence of teratogenic effects was observed in rats and rabbits. There was no evidence of embryolethality, fetotoxicity, or genotoxicity.
- In humans, there are sparse data on use of inclisiran in three pregnant women. Therefore, the data are insufficient to determine a drug-associated risk for major birth defects, miscarriage or adverse maternal or fetal outcomes.
- Current recommendation is that women who are planning pregnancy or who become pregnant should stop cholesterol reducing drugs therapy. Alternatively, the prescriber should consider the ongoing therapeutic needs of the individual patient.
- DPMH recommends a PMR to conduct a worldwide, single-arm, observational safety study of pregnant women exposed to inclisiran. It is a descriptive study that actively collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to inclisiran during pregnancy to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant in all exposed pregnancies of which you become aware. Infant outcomes should be assessed through at least the first year of life. The single arm pregnancy study will collect information for a minimum of 10 years from the date of market approval for inclisiran.
- Contact information for the pregnancy safety study should be added to labeling once the protocol is finalized and enrollment begins.
L2093: ## Lactation
L2094: - It is not known if the drug is present in human milk. There is no information on the effects of the drug on the breastfed infant, or the effects of the drug on the milk production.
- Oligonucleotide-based products typically have poor oral bioavailability; therefore, it is considered unlikely that low milk exposure levels will adversely impact neonatal development.
L2095: 34 Refer to the review by Christos Mastroyannis, M.D., Medical Officer, Maternal Health, Division of Pediatric and Maternal Health (DPMH); submitted to DARRTS on 10/19/2020; Reference ID: 4687201.
L2096: - As animal study findings suggest that inclisiran is not systemically absorbed by rat pups, DPMH does not recommend a PMR clinical lactation study. DPMH recommends the standard risk-benefit statement is added to subsection 8.2 of inclisiran labeling.
L2097: (b) (4)
L2098: ## 8.8.3. Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L2099: Safety and efficacy in pediatric patients have not been established.
L2100: As a new active ingredient, this application is subject to Pediatric Research Equity Act requirements. Under the iPSR agreed to by the Division and Applicant, the Applicant was granted a partial waiver for pediatric patients, birth to &lt;18 years of age, with non-familial hypercholesterolemia with clinical atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (b) (4) and elevated LDL-C, birth to &lt;8 years of age (or &lt;6 years of age and on apheresis) with elevated cholesterol with HeFH. The rationale for the partial waiver is based upon "studies would be impossible or highly impractical"
L2101: The applicant will be issued postmarketing requirements (PMRs) to conduct phase 3 efficacy and safety studies evaluating inclisiran in patients with HeFH ages 8 years (or ≥6 years of age and on apheresis) to less than 18 years.
L2102: ## 8.8.4. Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L2103: Nonclinical studies indicate that inclisiran does not have central nervous system activity and has no abuse potential.
L2104: ## 8.9. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2105: ## 8.9.1. Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L2106: Not applicable.
L2107: ## 8.9.2. Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2108: Inclisiran was well-tolerated, and the most important safety issue was injection site reactions in the Phase 3 trials. Inclisiran will be administered by a health care provider in the clinic setting, as it was during the phase 3 trials. No additional risk management activities in the postmarket setting, e.g., REMS, are anticipated.
L2109: ## 8.9.3. Additional Safety Issues From Other Disciplines
L2110: Safety issues from other disciplines are discussed in relevant sections throughout this review.
L2111: ## 8.10. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L2112: Inclisiran was generally well-tolerated, and the most important safety issue was injection site reactions in the Phase 3 trials. Preferred terms that mapped to the High-Level Term "Injection site reactions" occurred in 8.2% of patients on inclisiran as compared to 1.8% on placebo. Injection site reactions occurred on average in approximately 3.5 days after inclisiran administration and lasted on average 5.6 days, and most were mild to moderate in severity. Four inclisiran treated patients withdrew from treatment due to non-serious adverse reactions at the injection site compared to none on placebo.
L2113: Overall hypersensitivity reactions occurred in 7.4% on inclisiran and 7.5% of placebo treated patients. The incidence of some individual preferred terms under hypersensitivity disorders was greater with inclisiran, while others were greater in placebo. There was no discernable pattern.
L2114: Other frequently reported AEs that occurred with inclisiran greater than placebo were: arthralgia 5.0% on inclisiran and 4.0% on placebo; urinary tract infections 4.4% on inclisiran and 3.6% on placebo; diarrhea 3.9% on inclisiran and 3.5% on placebo and bronchitis 4.3% on inclisiran and 2.7% on placebo.
L2115: A total of 54 deaths occurred during the 18 months of the 3 Phase 3 trials. The incidence of deaths on inclisiran and placebo was exactly the same at 27 deaths or 1.5% on each treatment arm. The most common cause of death was in cardiac disorders and occurred at 0.7% on inclisiran as compared to 0.5% on placebo. Inclisiran did not appear to increase the risk of death from any particular cause.
L2116: Overall, slightly fewer patients on inclisiran (20%) than on placebo (23%) reported at least one serious adverse event. Most serious adverse events were balanced between groups.
L2117: The incidence of adverse events leading to withdrawal of study treatment was 2.5% (45/1833) on inclisiran and 1.9% (35/1822) on placebo. The most common preferred term as a reason for withdrawal of treatment was injection site reaction (n=4, 0.2%) as compared to 0 patients on placebo. Other adverse reactions that led to withdrawal of treatment were in Neoplasms disorders and Musculoskeletal disorders with a difference of approximately 0.2% greater incidence on inclisiran than placebo.
L2118: ## Adverse Events of Special Interest
L2119: ALT elevations/hepatic events: A similar proportion of patients in each treatment group experienced elevations in ALT or AST &gt;3X ULN. There were no elevations greater than 10X ULN
L2120: on inclisiran. There were no Hy's Law cases. The incidence of hepatic disorders SMQ was 3.8% on inclisiran and 4.2% on placebo.
L2121: **Renal function:** TEAEs associated with renal safety identified by the predefined SMQ search by PTs were balanced between the placebo-treated and inclisiran-treated subjects at 3.3%. There were no clinically significant differences between inclisiran and placebo for change from Baseline in serum creatinine. Shift tables for change in eGFR show similar incidence between inclisiran and placebo and no clinically significant difference between the two treatment arms.
L2122: **Myopathy:** The incidence of elevations in creatinine kinase was similar between the two treatment arms. Preferred terms such as arthralgia and pain in extremity were reported at slightly higher incidence than placebo.
L2123: **New Onset Diabetes:** Overall, the number of subjects who did not have diabetes at baseline and who developed new onset diabetes was 8.0% on inclisiran and 8.1% on placebo. In the subset of patients with no diabetes at Baseline and fasting glucose &lt; 100 mg/dL, approximately 4.3% of patients on inclisiran and 4.7% of patients on placebo developed new onset diabetes. In the subset of patients with impaired fasting glucose, 14.6% on inclisiran and 13.8% on placebo had new onset diabetes.
L2124: Overall mean change from Baseline in glucose was +3.2 mg/dL on inclisiran and +1.0 mg/dL on placebo while the mean change HbA1c was +0.1% on inclisiran and 0% on placebo. These differences were driven by changes among patients who had diabetes or impaired fasting glucose.
L2125: The similar incidence of new onset diabetes in patients who did not have diabetes at baseline and the magnitude of the changes in glucose and HbA1c in patients with diabetes/impaired fasting glucose subsets suggests that any blood glucose effects of inclisiran are likely to be small.
L2126: **Neurocognitive Events:** Using a number of MedDRA High level group terms, the number of patients with adverse events related to neurocognitive events was 0.7% on inclisiran and 1.0% on placebo. I also conducted a customized query, which resulted in a similar incidence of adverse events between treatment arms. Inclisiran did not appear to increase the risk of neurocognitive events.
L2127: **Psychiatric Events:** Psychiatric related disorders did not occur more frequently in the inclisiran group (n=83, 4.5%) vs. the placebo group (n=86, 4.7%). I conducted FDA customized queries for anxiety and depression and found a similar incidence between groups. Inclisiran did not appear to increase the risk of psychiatric events.
L2128: **Immunogenicity:** In the Phase 3 trials, anti-drug antibodies were analyzed for the patients on inclisiran and not on placebo. The incidence of patients with confirmed positive ADA at any visit was 4.9% and titers were generally low. Approximately 3.6% of patients had transient ADA, while 1.3% had persistent ADA. ADA presence by titer levels did not appear to be associated with loss of LDL-C lowering efficacy or PCSK9 levels.
L2129: Theoretical risks associated with low LDL-C such as cerebrovascular hemorrhage, primary adrenal insufficiency, primary gonadal insufficiency, and fat-soluble vitamin related disorders were investigated. There did not appear to be a signal for these events based on adverse events reporting.
L2130: ## 9. Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L2131: An Advisory Committee meeting was not held for this application.

- Add a Limitations of Use: The effect of INCLISIRAN on cardiovascular morbidity and mortality has not been determined.

L2144: ## 11. Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)

L2146: ## 12. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L2147: ## Postmarketing Requirements
L2148: - Conduct an efficacy and safety study evaluating inclisiran in patients with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) aged 12 years to less than 18 years. The Phase 3 study will be a randomized, double-blind, placebo controlled, parallel group, 12-month, multicenter efficacy and safety study in 150 patients (randomized 2:1 to inclisiran and placebo), followed by a 12-month open-label extension with all patients assigned to inclisiran in pediatric patients aged 12 years to less than 18 years with HeFH on maximally tolerated, stable lipid-modifying therapy with LDL-C \ge130 mg/dL.

Draft Protocol Submission: No later than 1st half 2020
Final Protocol Submission: No later than 2nd half 2020
Study Completion: June 2023
Final Report Submission: December 2023

- Conduct an efficacy and safety study evaluating inclisiran in patients with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) aged 6-7 if on plasma apheresis and 8 to &lt;12 years of age. The Phase 3 study will be a randomized, double-blind, placebo controlled, parallel group, 12-month, multicenter efficacy and safety study (b) (4), followed by a 12-month open-label extension with all patients assigned to inclisiran in pediatric patients aged 6-7 (if on plasma apheresis or meet eligibility criteria for lipid apheresis from their home country) and 8 years to less than 12 years with HeFH on maximally tolerated, stable lipid-modifying therapy (b) (4).

Draft Protocol Submission: No later than 1st half 2021
Final Protocol Submission: December 2021
Study Completion: June 2024
Final Report Submission: December 2024
L2149: ## Postmarketing Commitments
L2150: - Complete the ongoing randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group, multicenter trial in approximately 15,000 patients (randomized 1:1 to inclisiran and placebo) designed to assess the effects of inclisiran on the occurrence of major
L2151: cardiovascular events. The trial will include evaluation of the effects of inclisiran on the occurrence of injection site reactions, hypersensitivity reactions, antibody formation and new onset diabetes mellitus.

L2156: ## 13.2. Financial Disclosure
L2157: Covered Clinical Study (Name and/or Number):
L2158: - Protocol Number: ALN-PCSSC-01: A phase 1, randomized, single-blind, placebo-controlled, single ascending dose and multiple dose study to evaluate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of subcutaneously administered ALN-PCSSC in subjects with elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (2 principal investigators)
- Protocol Number: ORION-1 (MDCO-PCS-15-01): A placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized trial to compare the effect of different doses of ALN-PCSSC given as single or multiple subcutaneous injections in subjects with high cardiovascular risk and elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) (54 principal investigators)
- Protocol Number: ORION-2 (MDCO-PCS-16-02): An open-label, single-arm, multicenter pilot study to evaluate safety, tolerability, and efficacy of ALN-PCSSC in subjects with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (3 principal investigators)
- Protocol Number: ORION-3 (MDCO-PCS-16-01): An open label, active comparator extension trial to assess the effect of long term dosing of inclisiran and evolocumab given as subcutaneous injections in subjects with high cardiovascular risk and elevated LDL-C (55 principal investigators)
- Protocol Number: ORION-6 (MDCO-PCS-18-02): A single-dose, open-label, parallel-group study to assess the pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, and safety of inclisiran in subjects with hepatic impairment compared to subjects with normal hepatic function (1 principal investigator)
- Protocol Number: ORION-7 (MDCO-PCS-16-03): A single-dose, open-label, parallel-group study to assess the pharmacokinetics of inclisiran in subjects with renal impairment compared to subjects with normal renal function (2 principal investigators)
L2159: - ORION-9 (MDCO-PCS-17-03): A placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized trial to evaluate the effect of 300 mg of inclisiran sodium given as subcutaneous injections in subjects with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HeFH) and elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) (57 principal investigators)
- Protocol Number: ORION-10 (MDCO-PCS-17-04): A placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized trial to evaluate the effect of 300 mg of inclisiran sodium given as subcutaneous injections in subjects with atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD) and elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) (154 principal investigators)
- Protocol Number: ORION-11 (MDCO-PCS-17-08): A placebo-controlled, double-blind, randomized trial to evaluate the effect of 300 mg of inclisiran sodium given as subcutaneous injections in subjects with atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease (ASCVD) or ASCVD-risk equivalents and elevated low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) (84 principal investigators)
- Protocol Number: ORION-12 (MDCO-PCS-17-09): A double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled-parallel-design study with an open-label positive-control, to assess the electrocardiographic effects of inclisiran in healthy subjects (1 principal investigator)

L2168: Figure 1 ORION-3: Trial Design
The figure illustrates the trial design for ORION-1 (Double-blind) and ORION-3 (Open-label Extension).
ORION-1 Double-blind (Days 1 to 540):
1 dose starting regimen: Baseline, followed by 1 dose at Day 1. The regimen is Placebo (N=65), 200 mg (N=60), 300 mg (N=61), and 500 mg (N=65). The change in LDL-C (\DeltaLDL-C) is measured at Day 90, 180, 270, 360, and 540. The mean follow-up duration is 132 days.
2 dose starting regimen: Baseline, followed by 2 doses at Day 1. The regimen is 100 mg (N=61), 200 mg (N=62), 300 mg (N=61), and Placebo (N=62). The change in LDL-C (\DeltaLDL-C) is measured at Day 90, 180, 270, 360, and 540.
ORION-3 Open-label (Extension of ORION-1) (Days 540 onwards):
Group 1: Inclisiran 300 mg Q6M. Subjects who entered treatment at Day 290 (N=284) are shown.
Group 2: Evolocumab 140mg Q2W. Subjects who entered treatment at Day 92 (N=86) are shown. This group includes subjects transitioning from Evolocumab to Inclisiran.
Transitions: Trans 1: Concomitant switch and Trans 2: Sequential switch are indicated.
Randomized Treated subjects: 501 (1 dose regimen) and 497 (2 dose regimen).

L2180: Figure 10 displays three scatter plots showing the Absolute Change in LDL-C from Baseline to Day 510 (Y-axis) versus Baseline LDL (X-axis) for ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11. Data points are colored red for Inclisiran and teal/cyan for Placebo. Vertical dashed lines indicate quartiles of baseline LDL. Nonparametric regression curves (black lines) and linear regression lines (colored lines) are overlaid. The plots show that the absolute reduction in LDL-C increases with increasing baseline LDL, particularly for the Inclisiran group.
L2181: Figure 11 displays three scatter plots showing the percentage change in LDL-C from baseline to Day 510 (Y-axis) versus Baseline LDL (X-axis) for ORION-9, ORION-10, and ORION-11 trials. Data points are colored red (Inclisiran) and teal (Placebo). Vertical dashed lines indicate quartiles of baseline LDL. Black lines represent local regression fits, and red lines represent linear regression lines for comparative purposes. The plots show that Inclisiran generally results in greater LDL-C reduction compared to Placebo, especially at lower baseline LDL levels.
L2182: Figure 12 is a line graph showing the Mean Percent Change (\pm SEM) from Baseline LDL-C (mg/dL) over Visit Day (BL, 14, 30, 60, 90, 120, 150, 180, 210, 240, 270, 300, 330, 360) for three single-dose groups: Inclisiran 200 mg Single Dose (blue squares), Inclisiran 500 mg Single Dose (red diamonds), and Inclisiran 300 mg Single Dose + Placebo Single Dose (green circles).
The Y-axis ranges from -80 to 20. The X-axis shows Visit Day. The graph illustrates that all three groups show a rapid decrease in LDL-C from Baseline (BL) at Day 14, reaching approximately -50% by Day 30. The Inclisiran 500 mg group shows the lowest LDL-C levels (around -55%) at Day 30. From Day 60 onwards, all groups show a gradual increase in LDL-C levels, with the Inclisiran 200 mg group generally maintaining the lowest LDL-C levels (around -40% to -50%) by Day 360. The Inclisiran 500 mg group shows a slight rebound around Day 300, reaching approximately -40%.

L2183: Line graph showing Mean Percent Change from Baseline LDL-C (mg/dL) over Visit Day (BL, 14, 30, 60, 90, 120, 150, 180, 210, 240, 270, 300, 330, 360) for three double-dose groups: Inclisiran 100 mg Double Dose (pink line), Inclisiran 300 mg Double Dose (green line), and Inclisiran 200 mg Double Dose + Placebo Double Dose (blue line). L2202: Flowchart illustrating the Anti-Drug Antibody Hierarchical Testing process:
Start: Study Sample
Step 1 (Screening, Tier 1): Screen Positive?If Yes: Proceed to Confirmatory (Tier 2).
If No: Report Negative.

Step 2 (Confirmatory, Tier 2): Confirm Positive?If Yes: Proceed to Titration (Tier 3).
If No: Report Negative.

Step 3 (Titration, Tier 3): Report Positive Titer.
L2203: Line graph showing Mean Actual Values LDL-C (mg/dL) versus Visit Day (Baseline, 90, 150, 270, 330, 450, 510, 540) for three ADA Titer groups: None (gray line), 50 and 100 (red line), and 200, 400 and 800 (blue line). All groups start around 115 mg/dL at Baseline and decrease significantly by Visit Day 90 (around 60-65 mg/dL). The groups generally maintain low LDL-C levels (around 55-65 mg/dL) from Visit Day 150 onwards, with minor fluctuations. The group with ADA titers of 200, 400, and 800 shows a slight dip around Visit Day 330 (around 55 mg/dL) compared to the other groups (around 60 mg/dL).
L2204: Line graph showing Mean Actual Values of PCSK9 (ng/dL) versus Visit Day (Baseline, 90, 150, 270, 330, 450, 510, 540) for three groups based on Maximum Post-Baseline ADA Titer: None (Black line), 50 and 100 (Red line), and 200, 400 and 800 (Blue line). PCSK9 levels start high at Baseline (around 380-400 ng/dL) and decrease sharply by Visit Day 150 (around 100-110 ng/dL). Levels stabilize thereafter, fluctuating between 100 and 150 ng/dL across all groups, indicating no significant difference in PCSK9 levels based on ADA titer.
L2205: Figure 32 displays three waterfall plots showing the LDL-C Percent Change from Baseline at Last Value for patients treated with Inclisiran, categorized by Anti-Drug Antibody (ADA) status. The Y-axis represents the LDL-C Percent Change from Baseline at Last Value, ranging from -100 to 150. The X-axis represents the individual patients.
The plots are:
ADA Status = Persistent (N=24): Shows a wide distribution of LDL-C percent change, ranging from approximately 50% to -100%. The majority of patients show a decrease in LDL-C.
ADA Status = Transient (N=66): Shows a distribution of LDL-C percent change, ranging from approximately 40% to -100%. The majority of patients show a decrease in LDL-C.
ADA Status = None (N=1740): Shows a distribution of LDL-C percent change, ranging from approximately 150% to -100%. The majority of patients show a decrease in LDL-C.
The overall pattern of LDL-C percent change from baseline is similar across all three ADA status groups, with a strong trend towards LDL-C reduction.
